# 4.4 Design Decisions

> **Status:** In review · **Owner:** Avana Labs · **Last reviewed:** 2026-11-10

Every design decision behind Avana, each with its final resolution and the reasoning for it: the risk it controls or the failure it prevents. Decisions are grouped by topic. A decision that a later one replaced keeps its row and names its replacement, and a decision that a later one changed in part names the amending decision in its row, so every `Q-NNN` referenced from the spec pages and the verification models resolves here. Q-IDs are stable. Where a decision states a parameter value, [1.5 Protocol Parameters](../01-architecture/05-parameters.md) holds the full table.

## Scope and assets

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-001` | Supported LP positions | Uniswap v3 positions (ERC-721) only at launch. Uniswap v2, Uniswap v4, Curve, Balancer and Aerodrome are later expansions. Every collateral position is a non-fungible NFT; there is no ERC-20 LP custody. This differs from the original Aave governance proposal (ARFC), which also listed Uniswap v2. **Superseded by `Q-291`:** Avana is AMM-neutral: one LPVault per AMM protocol per chain implements the AMM integration interface, Uniswap v3 on Ethereum and Aerodrome Slipstream on Base are fully specified, and Uniswap v2, Curve, Balancer and Uniswap v4 positions, fungible LP included, are listable once their readiness guards pass (`Q-302`). | One custody model keeps the LPVault, the backing ledger and seizure on a single code path to audit. Uniswap v2 would add ERC-20 custody with different accounting. | — |
| `Q-004` | Borrowable asset | Superseded by `Q-233`. Was: GHO was the only borrowable asset. | — | — |
| `Q-005` | Launch chain | Ethereum mainnet. Base and other networks each need their own Aave governance approval. **Superseded by `Q-279`:** Ethereum and Base launch together, each with its own Hub, timelock and Guardian, and the liquidation flash loan comes from the chain's Aave V3 Pool, not the GHO FlashMinter (`Q-334`). | The Uniswap v3 pools, Chainlink feeds, GHO FlashMinter and GSM Avana depends on are on mainnet; each extra chain adds bridge and feed dependencies that need their own review. | — |
| `Q-017` | Approved range | Full-range positions only: `tickLower` and `tickUpper` equal the minimum and maximum usable ticks for the pool's tick spacing. Each approved pool (which fixes its fee tier) is one market with one vault token and one Borrow Spoke reserve. Concentrated ranges are a later expansion that would need a governance-approved list of exact tick pairs per pool. **Superseded by `Q-293`:** a market is one exact position shape, so concentrated ranges are listable, each `[tickLower, tickUpper]` pair its own market, and a pledge must match the market's ticks exactly. | Liquidity units are interchangeable only within one tick range. With full range, every vault-token unit in a market is interchangeable, 1:1 backing is checkable with one equality, token amounts follow `q0 = L/√P` and `q1 = L·√P` with no range clamping, and removing part of the liquidity in a seizure leaves the position's shape unchanged. | — |
| `Q-021` | Initial Spoke set | Superseded by `Q-032`. Asked which risk-tier Spokes and pools launch under the single Hub. | — | — |
| `Q-024` | First engineering fixture | One full-range Uniswap v3 pool, one Borrow Spoke and GHO on an Ethereum mainnet fork. No Uniswap v2 fixture is built. **Superseded by `Q-366`:** each AMM integration has a fork test on its own chain, Uniswap v3 on Ethereum (`TEST-264`) and Aerodrome Slipstream on Base (`TEST-265`). | Follows `Q-001`: a v2 fixture would test a custody path that does not ship. | — |
| `Q-031` | Multiply | Leveraged LP (Multiply) is not part of v1. | Looping adds recursive borrow-and-pledge flows and liquidation cascades that the launch caps and verification do not cover. | — |
| `Q-032` | Launch Hub scope | One Hub at launch, the Avana Stable LP Hub, accepting stable LP positions only (stablecoin/stablecoin full-range Uniswap v3 pools). Correlated and volatile LP Hubs are a later stage. **Superseded by `Q-279`:** an Avana Stable LP Hub launches on Ethereum and on Base, and Correlated LP and Volatile LP Hubs can be added on any chain. | Stable pairs have the smallest LP price moves, so oracle, slippage and cap assumptions are easiest to bound; one Hub keeps the credit line and deficit accounting in one place. | `Q-021` |
| `Q-033` | Lender product name | The later lender product is the Lend Spoke. At launch the Lend Spoke carries only the DAO credit line (`Q-070`, `Q-168`). **Amended by `Q-239`:** the Lend Spoke can carry several approved supply assets (`Q-233`). **Amended by `Q-282`:** each chain has one supply-only Lend Spoke whose reserves point to (Hub, asset) pairs. | One name across spec, code and app. | — |
| `Q-058` | Listing rule | Eligible: large stable Uniswap v3 pools, including USDC/USDT and pools containing GHO. Each listed token needs a Chainlink feed with an alternate Chainlink route, a primary conversion route to GHO (GHO Stability Module, Sky PSM then GSM, or a proven deep DEX route) and an independent backup route (`Q-187`). Listing also requires the collateral margin (`Q-113`), feed cadence (`Q-182`), the common-mode guard (`Q-176`) and the oracle math guards (`Q-191`). **Amended by `Q-339`, `Q-341`, `Q-302`:** eligible pools come from any integrated AMM protocol whose readiness guards pass; each token needs a primary and an independent backup swap route into each approved debt asset of its Spoke, not a route to GHO, and the GSM is never a primary route. | Every leg must be priceable and convertible to GHO inside a liquidation transaction; a token lacking either cannot be liquidated and would leave bad debt. | — |
| `Q-078` | Launch allowlist | No borrower allowlist. Exposure at launch is limited by caps only. | Caps bound Avana's loss directly; an allowlist adds an operator-controlled gate and identity handling without bounding loss further. | — |
| `Q-233` | Multiple debt and supply assets | Avana supports several approved supply assets (Lend Spoke) and debt reserves (Borrow Spoke) from launch. GHO is the first credit line: DAO GHO is supplied through the Lend Spoke into the Avana Hub and Borrow Spokes draw against their caps. Another asset (WETH, USDC, USDT) becomes a live debt reserve only when its funding, caps, Aave oracle price source and buy and sell routes are approved and both swap legs of its liquidation pass stress validation; if the Aave DAO approves only GHO, GHO is the only live debt reserve. Each debt reserve's funding path is explicit: either supply into the Avana Hub through the Lend Spoke (the Lend Spoke `addCap` on that asset equals its credit line) or a capped draw from a source Hub (the Borrow Spoke reserve points at the source Hub's asset, with a `drawCap` equal to the approved line). The MarketRegistry records, per Borrow Spoke, each approved debt reserve with its asset, decimals, Hub, Hub asset ID, price source, Coverage Spoke and buy routes; the ParameterStore holds its `buySlippageAllowanceBps`. Borrow and repay name the debt reserve; the receiver rule (`Q-105`) applies to whichever asset is borrowed. Supersedes `Q-004`; amends `Q-006`, `Q-070`, `Q-095`, `Q-096`, `Q-105`, `Q-168`. **Amended by `Q-282`, `Q-333`, `Q-312`:** each debt reserve records its funding Hub and asset; liquidation flash-borrows the debt asset itself and settles in it, so no buy or sell-back route and no `buySlippageAllowanceBps` exists, and a debt asset needs swap routes from every pool token into it. | Borrowers holding stable LP positions want to borrow different assets; listing them on the same Borrow Spoke gives one account one health factor instead of splitting collateral across accounts. Gating each asset on funding, pricing and both liquidation legs means no asset is borrowable unless Avana can liquidate it inside one transaction. Naming the funding path per asset keeps each lender's exposure, and each deficit, on the Hub that supplied the asset. | `Q-004` |
| `Q-239` | Spokes at launch | At launch the Avana Stable LP Hub has one Stable LP Borrow Spoke (the CDP Spoke), the Lend Spoke and the GHO Coverage Spoke; a Coverage Spoke for another debt asset is added when that asset is listed (`Q-236`). Later Borrow Spokes are each a separate credit account with its own PositionProxy per wallet (`Q-225`), its own approved debt-reserve set (`Q-237`) and its own listing. Amends `Q-003` and `Q-033`. **Superseded by `Q-280`:** each Hub on each chain has one CDP Spoke per (AMM protocol, risk tier), at launch "Uniswap v3 Stable" on Ethereum and "Aerodrome Slipstream Stable" on Base, with one PositionProxy per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market) and one Coverage Spoke per (Hub, debt asset) (`Q-284`). | One Borrow Spoke keeps the launch to one risk tier and one health factor per wallet; a separate account per later Spoke keeps a riskier tier's collateral from backing debt in the stable tier, because Aave V4 tracks health per account per Spoke. | — |
| `Q-280` | CDP Spoke grouping and account isolation | A CDP Spoke is one standard Aave V4 Spoke per (chain, Hub, AMM protocol, risk tier), for example "Uniswap v3 Stable" on Ethereum or "Aerodrome Slipstream Stable" on Base. It lists several markets; each market is one collateral reserve. A market is one (chain, AMM protocol, pool, exact position shape); for concentrated liquidity the shape is the exact `[tickLower, tickUpper]`. Each wallet gets one PositionProxy per (CDP Spoke, market), created by CREATE2 on first use, so each market has its own Aave account and health factor and nothing is cross-collateralized. A wallet using two markets has two proxies. Each CDP Spoke has a home Hub. Supersedes `Q-234` (several collateral markets per account) and `Q-276`. | One market per Spoke costs about 2.4 times the governance actions and splits each credit line into one cap per market; several markets in one account let one stale oracle liquidate healthy collateral (11.4 wrongly liquidated accounts in topology T2 of `SIM-R7`). Several markets per Spoke with one proxy per market gives the isolation of one-market Spokes (0 accounts liquidated through a healthy market) at the governance cost of shared Spokes (16 actions per market against 38 to 56). | `Q-234`, `Q-276`. Resolves AMMN-ARCH-02, AMMN-FLOW-07 |

## Chains

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-279` | Chains, Hubs and instance scope | Avana runs on several chains, Hubs and AMM protocols. Launch: Ethereum and Base together, each with an Avana Stable LP Hub; the Correlated LP and Volatile LP Hub tiers can be added on any chain. Contract instances are scoped as follows. Per chain: ProxyManager, VaultTokenFactory, AvanaTimelock, AaveAdapter, LPVaultSwap, Collateralization (one AMM-neutral ledger), MarketRegistry, ParameterStore, RiskModule, SettlementBudget, PayoutEscrow, Lock, each DebtOracle, the `IFlashSource` implementation, and the Lens as a periphery contract. Per AMM protocol per chain: one immutable LPVault and one pricing library (or oracle implementation). Per market: one VaultToken and one LPOracle instance. Per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market): one PositionProxy. Every cap, budget, pause, breaker, route permission and emergency control is keyed by Hub, and by Spoke and market where it applies. The Lock has exactly two members, the AaveAdapter and LPVaultSwap; every LPVault and Collateralization entry point that moves value is reachable only through them. Hub-wide caps (token, depositor, pool share) live in the one ledger keyed by Hub, so adding a Spoke never multiplies them. Supersedes `Q-005` (Ethereum only) and `Q-032` (one Hub); amends `Q-225`, `Q-240`. **Amended by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is not deployed; the PayoutEscrow stays as payout escrow (`Q-401`). | Per-Hub copies of the Adapter and LPVaultSwap run the same code under the same timelock and Guardian, so they add deployments and upgrade calls without shrinking the blast radius. A vault and ledger per Spoke cannot join an immutable Lock deployed earlier. One ledger keyed by Hub keeps Hub-wide caps from multiplying with the number of Spokes. One Adapter per chain also removes cross-Hub proxy squatting, because only one contract can ask the ProxyManager for a proxy. | `Q-005`, `Q-032`. Resolves AMMN-ARCH-03, AMMN-FLOW-17, AMMN-RT-10, AMMN-FLOW-18, AMMN-RT-05, AMMN-ARCH-01, AMMN-FLOW-01, AMMN-GAS-09 |
| `Q-285` | Per-chain governance and pause scope | Each chain has its own AvanaTimelock, 4-of-7 governance Safe and 3-of-5 Emergency Guardian Safe (distinct signer sets per chain are allowed; the same rules apply). Cross-chain governance does not exist: an operation on one chain never changes another. The pause state, breakers and address freeze are held per Hub in the chain's MarketRegistry; `pauseAll(reason)` and `freezeAll()` change every Hub of the chain in one guardian call, and the address-freeze set is per chain, read by every Hub. Aave-side actions on Base go through the Aave DAO's Base executor, whose latency the runbook states. Amends `Q-114`, `Q-227`, `Q-228`, `Q-242`. | One code base serves every Hub on a chain, so a code incident must be containable in one call; per-Hub pause keeps a local incident local. A freeze that covered only one Hub would let a frozen address keep acting on others. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-19 |
| `Q-287` | Per-chain parameters and minimum debt | Parameters that depend on the chain are set per chain and stated in 1.5: block time (Ethereum 12 s, Base 2 s), the sequencer-uptime feed and its grace period (Base feed `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433`, grace 3,600 s; none on Ethereum), feed heartbeats (each feed's own, for example 86,400 s for Chainlink stablecoin feeds), gas assumptions and the minimum debt `minDebtUsd`, derived from liquidation gas by `SPEC-CHAIN-001` so that a keeper's net income on the smallest debt covers the gas of liquidating it: `minDebtUsd ≥ ⌈(G × gP95 × ethUsd + f) / r⌉`, with `G` the gas of a one-record liquidation (1,550,000), `gP95` the chain's 95th-percentile gas price, `f` an L2's L1 data fee and `r` the keeper's net income per unit of debt. Launch values: Ethereum $2,000 against a break-even of $1,716 (5 gwei) and $1,974 with `minDebtSafetyBps` = 1,500; Base $50 against $32 and $37 (0.05 gwei plus about $0.20 of L1 data fee). At most `MAX_RECORDS_PER_ACCOUNT_MARKET` = 16 open records per account per market. Supersedes `Q-169`, `Q-215` and `Q-277` (no minimum debt). **Superseded by `Q-381` on minimum debt:** there is no minimum debt on any chain; `SPEC-CHAIN-001` states only the per-chain gas model, which sizes the 16-record cap and Avana's keeper gas budget. | A $10 pledge carries about $3 of debt and earns a keeper about $0.04, while one liquidation costs about $23 at 5 gwei on Ethereum, so small debts on Ethereum are never liquidated at a profit. An uncapped record list lets the dust rule force a seizure of 125 records, about 31M gas, above the per-transaction gas limit. | `Q-169`, `Q-215`, `Q-277`. Resolves AMMN-ARCH-05, AMMN-GAS-01, AMMN-GAS-02, AMMN-FLOW-03 (scope) |
| `Q-311` | Minimum debt enforced at borrow | `RiskModule.preBorrowCheck` refuses a borrow that leaves the account's debt value above 0 and below the chain's `minDebtUsd` (`BelowMinDebt`). Repay and liquidation never read it, so any partial repay stays allowed. The parameter satisfies `SPEC-CHAIN-001` (`Q-287`): launch values $2,000 on Ethereum and $50 on Base, against break-even values of $1,716 and $32, or $1,974 and $37 with `minDebtSafetyBps` = 1,500. The keeper's net income rate is the one 2.9 states, `netKeeperRate = 0.8 × (bonus − swapCost × (1 + bonus))`, which is 1.3552% at a 2% bonus and 0.3% swap cost. `minPledgeUsd` stays $10. **Superseded by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt; `preBorrowCheck` reads none and `BelowMinDebt` is removed. | A keeper's income scales with the debt repaid, not with the collateral, so a pledge floor cannot keep small accounts liquidatable; a floor on the debt a borrow may leave does. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-02 |
| `Q-324` | L2 sequencer-uptime gate with a grace period | Per chain `SequencerConfig {feed, gracePeriod}` (Base `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433`, 3,600 s; Ethereum none). Down (`answer == 1`): seizure paused (`liquidationPaused`) and borrowing blocked. Restart: during `[startedAt, startedAt + gracePeriod)` borrowing is blocked and `inGrace` is true, so LPVaultSwap liquidates only below `graceHealthFactor`. Unreadable or unexpected feed: borrowing blocked, seizure not paused. The sequencer status never changes state, served price, accepted price or timers. The DebtOracle applies the same gate. | While the sequencer is down only L1-forced transactions run; borrowers cannot top up or repay, so seizing them is unfair and new debt would be priced on stale data. After restart borrowers need time to act. A broken uptime feed must never stop liquidation. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-03 (1, 2), AMMN-INT-06 |
| `Q-326` | TWAP observation buffer per chain block time | `twapMinObservations = ⌈twapWindow / CHAIN_BLOCK_TIME⌉ + 1`, with `CHAIN_BLOCK_TIME` an implementation constant per chain (Ethereum 12 s → 151, Base 2 s → 901 for a 1,800 s window); `checkConfig` requires the cardinality and a successful `observe`; cardinality growth may be split over several transactions. The consistency check reads `observe` through the pricing module, never `slot0`. | k observations written one per block span (k − 1) × block time; a buffer sized for 12 s blocks covers 300 s on Base, so about $2 of dust swaps forces `OLD` and keeps the market Drifting. | Resolves AMMN-RT-08 |
| `Q-381` | No minimum debt; Avana's keepers pay liquidation gas | Avana has no minimum debt on any chain: a borrow of any amount above 0 that passes the other checks is accepted. `minDebtUsd`, `minDebtSafetyBps`, `BelowMinDebt`, `tightenMinDebtUsd` / `loosenMinDebtUsd`, the minimum-debt step of `preBorrowCheck`, the break-even sizing and every keeper-profitability claim are removed. The approved keepers are Avana's own; they liquidate every account below health factor 1 whatever its size, and Avana pays their gas as its own operating cost. The permissionless fallback stays. Liquidation never depends on a keeper making a profit. `SPEC-CHAIN-001` now states only the per-chain gas model `G(R) = G_base + R × g_record` (launch 1,310,000 + 240,000 × R gas on Ethereum and Base), which sizes the record cap and Avana's keeper gas budget; the gas price is a budget input, never a protocol value. The cap of 16 records per account per market stays because one liquidation of the largest account must fit the per-transaction gas limit: `G(16) = 1,310,000 + 240,000 × 16 = 5,150,000 ≤ 16,777,216`. Keeper gas reimbursement from the SettlementBudget becomes Avana's keeper cost; the budget stays only where it serves solvency (recovery shortfall). Supersedes the minimum-debt parts of `Q-287`, `Q-311`, `Q-317`, `Q-362` and `Q-380`, and restores `Q-169` on minimum debt. Amends FINDING/AMMN-GAS-02 to: resolved by decision, no minimum, Avana's keeper pays gas. | A minimum debt only stops borrowers opening small loans: small debts still arise from partial repays, partial liquidations and price moves, so the gas cost it addressed exists either way. With Avana's own keepers liquidating every account, a small liquidation is a bounded operating cost of at most `G(16)` gas, not a reason for an account to go unliquidated. The one gas limit that can make an account unliquidatable is the per-transaction gas limit, and the record cap keeps the largest liquidation under half of it. | `Q-287`, `Q-311`, `Q-317`, `Q-362`, `Q-380`. Resolves AMMN-GAS-02 |
| `Q-357` | Chain-specific gates in every flow | Each chain's flows read the chain's sequencer gate (`Up`, `Down`, `Grace`) from its sequencer-uptime feed (Base `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433`; Ethereum has none, so its gate is always `Up`). `Down`: borrow, withdraw-with-debt, new-account pledge, repay with collateral and liquidation revert `SequencerDown`; top-ups, repay, debt-free withdrawal and fee collection run. `Grace` (`gracePeriod` after restart, launch 3,600 s): borrow, withdraw-with-debt and new-account pledges revert `SequencerGrace`; liquidation runs only for accounts with health factor below `graceHealthFactor` (0.95); everything else runs. Every feed is fresh while its age is at most its own heartbeat plus `heartbeatMargin` (1,800 s), so a stablecoin feed with an 86,400 s heartbeat is fresh for 88,200 s (`Q-322`); borrow and withdraw-with-debt also require the independent second source to agree (`Q-323`). Time windows are in seconds, never blocks; on Base (2 s blocks) the TWAP buffer is sized for `twapWindow / 2 s` observations (`Q-326`) and the per-block quote-change cap applies per 2 s block. Minimum debt is set per chain (`Q-287`). | After an outage, borrowers had no access while prices moved; liquidating at once would be unfair, and transactions forced in from L1 during the outage would act on stale prices. Day-long stablecoin heartbeats make a 3,600 s age bound unlistable, so freshness follows each feed, with a second source covering the long silent periods. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-03, AMMN-INT-01 (flow level), AMMN-RT-08 (flow timing) |

## Architecture and contracts

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-002` | Avana Hub | A dedicated Avana Hub that is a standard, unmodified Aave V4 Hub owned by the Aave DAO. Avana Labs prepares its configuration; the DAO owns and applies it. One Hub at launch (`Q-032`); separate Stable, Correlated and Volatile LP Hubs are a later stage. **Amended by `Q-279`:** an Avana Stable LP Hub launches on Ethereum and on Base; Correlated LP and Volatile LP Hubs can be added on any chain. | Unmodified Aave code keeps Aave's audits and accounting guarantees; a separate Hub keeps LP-collateral losses away from the Core Hub. | — |
| `Q-003` | Borrow Spokes | One Borrow Spoke per risk tier; at launch, one Borrow Spoke for the stable tier. Spokes are standard Aave V4 Spokes owned by the Aave DAO. "CDP Spoke" and "Borrow Spoke" mean the same thing; the spec uses Borrow Spoke. **Amended by `Q-222`:** every Borrow Spoke is Aave Labs' liquidation-manager Spoke variant, with LPVaultSwap as its liquidation manager. **Amended by `Q-239`:** at launch there is one Stable LP Borrow Spoke (the CDP Spoke) beside the Lend Spoke and the Coverage Spoke; each later Borrow Spoke is a separate credit account with its own PositionProxy per wallet. **Amended by `Q-280`, `Q-281`:** a CDP Spoke (the name the spec now uses) is one standard, unmodified Aave V4 Spoke per (chain, Hub, AMM protocol, risk tier); the liquidation-manager variant of `Q-222` is not used, and each wallet has one PositionProxy per (CDP Spoke, market). | Aave V4 tracks health per account per Spoke, so collateral in one tier cannot back debt in another. | — |
| `Q-037` | RiskModule and app checks | The RiskModule is the on-chain eligibility gate: pool, fee tier, full range, oracle state, caps and Avana's maximum share of pool liquidity (`Q-054`). The app runs the same checks first and shows the borrower why a position is or is not eligible; app-only advisory checks are listed in `Q-054`. **Amended by `Q-293`, `Q-376`:** the gate checks the AMM integration's exact-shape match instead of fee tier and full range. | The on-chain gate protects against direct contract callers; the app pre-check avoids failed transactions and explains rejections. | — |
| `Q-049` | Immutability | Superseded by `Q-226`. Was: an immutable LPVault, vault tokens, PositionProxy implementation, ProxyFactory, Lock, GuardianRestrictions, SettlementBudget and routes, with AvanaConfig, RiskModule, LPOracle, LPVaultSwap and the Adapter upgradeable behind the 14-day `upgradeDelay`. | — | — |
| `Q-077` | Adapter form | Superseded by `Q-223`. Was: the Adapter as an upgradeable view-only veto with a frozen interface for the proxy and no mint, custody or borrower authority (upgradeability is `Q-226`). | — | — |
| `Q-092` | Limiting a hostile Adapter | Superseded by `Q-223`. Was: minting, LP release and acting for borrowers kept outside the upgradeable Adapter so that a hostile Adapter could only refuse actions. | — | — |
| `Q-148` | Reentrancy | One immutable Lock contract: outer entry points acquire it; calls among the fixed set of Avana contracts are accepted only while it is held and only from those addresses. **Amended by `Q-223`:** the Lock's fixed members are the AaveAdapter, Collateralization, the LPVault and LPVaultSwap; the Adapter takes it for borrower actions and proxies are reached only through the Adapter. **Amended by `Q-318`:** the Lock has exactly two members, the AaveAdapter and LPVaultSwap; Collateralization and every LPVault check that one of them holds it and never acquire it. | Pledge, burn and settlement span several contracts in one transaction; a shared lock stops a token, route or Spoke callback from re-entering a half-finished operation, which per-contract guards cannot detect. | — |
| `Q-206` | Cross-page consistency rulings | Restriction state (Guardian pauses and freezes, incident clock, cooldown, quarantine flags, in-kind enablement) lives in the immutable GuardianRestrictions contract; the Adapter, RiskModule, LPOracle and LPVaultSwap read it; the keeper list stays on LPVaultSwap. Quarantine is lifted at once by Recovery. Withdraw-with-debt follows the emergency matrix and is blocked whenever liquidation is halted, including Hub shares ≠ assets. A USD-band breach blocks borrow and withdraw-with-debt; pledges stay allowed. Tolerances tighten immediately within liveness bounds and loosen at `upgradeDelay`. Grace applies per account. Each contract page is authoritative for its own function, event and error names; the role table in 2.13 is authoritative for roles. Repay at zero debt returns without reverting. **Amended by `Q-227`:** GuardianRestrictions no longer exists; the restriction rulings are replaced by the application pause state held in the MarketRegistry, and withdraw-with-debt is blocked under Paused and whenever liquidation is halted. **Amended by `Q-285`, `Q-308`:** the pause state and its reason are kept per Hub; the address freeze is one set per chain. | Restriction state in one immutable contract means an upgrade of a contract that reads it cannot erase an active restriction or reset the incident clock. A single authoritative source per name stops pages from contradicting each other. | — |
| `Q-209` | Interface rulings | Pool broken is a GuardianRestrictions restriction kind set by the Guardian on the incident clock, lifted by Recovery, and read by the RiskModule (pledge) and the Adapter (borrow, withdraw-with-debt). Stray-NFT return and SettlementBudget withdrawal each use one AccessManager-scheduled call at `upgradeDelay`, with no in-contract timer. The RiskModule computes the pledge cap charge from LPOracle with the regular/slice split; the LPVault passes the current counters as arguments and books the result after custody, atomically. The PositionProxy calls `poke()` on each relevant market feed before asking the Adapter; the LPVault does the same before the pledge check; LPVaultSwap and keepers also poke. Repay at zero debt, partial or repay-all, returns 0 with no token movement. Liveness bounds: the minimum slippage allowance is stored per market at listing; the grace HF, grace duration and TWAP tolerance bounds are global immutables. Borrow and withdraw-with-debt checks revert `AaveReservePaused(market)` for any paused vault-token reserve in the account and `GhoReservePaused()` for a paused GHO reserve. **Amended by `Q-227`:** pool broken no longer exists; `DebtReservePaused(reserveId)` replaces `GhoReservePaused()`. **Amended by `Q-228`:** stray-NFT return and SettlementBudget withdrawal are timelock operations at the normal delay; the RiskModule, not the proxy, pokes the feeds for borrow-side checks. **Amended by `Q-240`:** Collateralization, not the LPVault, passes the counters and books the charge. | One delay mechanism (the AccessManager) gives one place to watch and cancel; poking before every check makes each gate decide on the latest price; specific errors let the app tell the borrower why an action failed. | — |
| `Q-217` | Zero-amount transfers | Every Avana contract skips, rather than attempts, a token `transfer` or `transferFrom` whose amount is zero, and an NFPM `collect` whose two caps are both zero. This covers every computed amount on every path: liquidation payouts, route inputs and residues, in-kind shares, remainders and refunds, escrow deposits and claims, pending-settlement payouts, repay refunds, budget draws and reserve funding. Checks that reject a zero user input (`ZeroAmount`, `InvalidAmount`) are unchanged. | Some ERC-20 tokens revert on a zero-amount transfer and the Uniswap NFPM reverts a `collect` with both caps zero; attempting either on a computed zero would make a liquidation, settlement or exit revert for no economic reason. | — |
| `Q-223` | Adapter call path and custody trust | Every borrower action goes wallet → AaveAdapter → the wallet's PositionProxy for that Borrow Spoke → Borrow Spoke. The PositionProxy accepts calls only from the Adapter (`OnlyAdapter()`), including from its own wallet; the wallet never calls its proxy. The Adapter resolves the proxy from `msg.sender` and the Borrow Spoke argument for pledge, borrow and withdrawal, and from the named borrower for repay. It executes each action: it asks Collateralization to take custody and record backing, mints and burns vault tokens (`Q-224`), creates proxies (`Q-225`), runs the RiskModule's borrow-side checks and reverts on any failure (`Q-229`), and has the proxy call the Spoke. Repay accepts any payer, who approves the Adapter; the Adapter pulls the exact amount from `msg.sender` to the proxy. Fee collection (`LPVault.collectFees`) and the release of a zero-credit record (`Collateralization.releaseZeroCredit`) stay direct calls by the depositor and never reach the Adapter. The Lock's fixed members are the AaveAdapter, Collateralization, the LPVault and LPVaultSwap. Trust model: the Adapter's code and the timelock that upgrades it (`Q-228`) are trusted for custody of every proxy position. Blast radius of a faulty or hostile Adapter implementation: it can borrow every debt reserve against every proxy up to each Borrow Spoke's `drawCap`, withdraw supplied vault tokens from any proxy, mint vault tokens to the Hub, itself or any registered proxy, burn any holder's vault tokens, register any address as a valid proxy, spend every allowance granted to it and pledge any NFT whose owner approved the LPVault for it; it cannot send an NFT to anyone but its depositor of record, change the Borrow Spoke's liquidation manager, raise a Hub or Spoke cap, or spend a Coverage Spoke. Supersedes `Q-077`, `Q-092`, `Q-093`, `Q-122`; amends `Q-029`, `Q-051`, `Q-064`, `Q-075`, `Q-076`, `Q-148`, `Q-212`. **Amended by `Q-254`, `Q-255`, `Q-260`:** the Adapter also runs partial removal, re-credit and repay with collateral, and exits read no registry state. **Amended by `Q-296`, `Q-307`, `Q-318`, `Q-281`:** the proxy is resolved per (wallet, market); fee collection and zero-credit release are Adapter entry points that pass `msg.sender` as depositor and read no pause or policy; the Lock's members are the AaveAdapter and LPVaultSwap only; and a hostile Adapter still cannot change any CDP Spoke setting, since there is no liquidation manager to change. | One executing entry point runs every gate (pause state, price states, opening health factor, caps) in one place on every path and gives borrowers one contract to approve and one address for the app and indexer; the earlier split between a veto-only policy and fixed proxy code could not be corrected without moving every position. The cost is that the Adapter's code controls custody, so its upgrades wait the full timelock delay with independent cancellers, and the funds an upgrade could reach are bounded by the Hub and Spoke caps. Repay and debt-free withdrawal never depend on a pause or a gate, so a borrower can leave inside that delay (`Q-228`). | `Q-077`, `Q-092`, `Q-093`, `Q-122` |
| `Q-226` | Upgradeable and immutable contracts | Upgradeable, each a UUPS proxy (ERC-1967) whose `_authorizeUpgrade` requires `APPLICATION_ADMIN`, held only by the Avana timelock (`Q-228`): AaveAdapter, ProxyManager (and through its beacon every PositionProxy), MarketRegistry, ParameterStore, RiskModule, each market's LPOracle and LPVaultSwap. Immutable, with no admin able to change code or bindings: vault tokens and the VaultTokenFactory, LPVault, Collateralization, Lock, SettlementBudget, PayoutEscrow, every settlement and buy route version, the Lens (which also produces liquidation previews, `Q-232`) and the timelock itself; each Coverage Spoke is controlled by the Aave DAO (`Q-236`). AvanaConfig, GuardianRestrictions and the ProxyFactory no longer exist: AvanaConfig's content is split between the MarketRegistry and the ParameterStore (`Q-240`) and the remaining restriction state is the MarketRegistry's pause switch (`Q-227`). Supersedes `Q-049`; amends `Q-090`, `Q-219`. **Amended by `Q-248`:** each non-GHO debt asset's DebtOracle is upgradeable like the LPOracle. **Amended by `Q-306`, `Q-288`, `Q-291`:** the ProxyManager and every PositionProxy are immutable, with no beacon; the Lens is a periphery contract outside the audited core; there is one immutable LPVault per AMM protocol per chain. **Amended by `Q-379`, `Q-397`:** the debt buy routes and the SettlementBudget are removed; no chain deploys either. | Code that decides gates, prices and settlement needs tuning after launch; one proxy pattern and one upgrade role give one path to watch and cancel. The vault token stays immutable so no upgrade can change who may hold or mint it beyond the Adapter it names. The LPVault and Collateralization stay immutable so NFT custody, the backing ledger and the rule that an NFT returns only to its depositor hold under any upgrade of the contracts around them. The Lens is a read-only tool, replaced by deploying a new one. | `Q-049` |
| `Q-240` | File size limits | Every implementation file in `contracts/src` is at most 500 lines of code (non-blank, non-comment lines) and every interface file at most 300 lines including NatSpec; CI counts both and fails above either limit. Contract splits, each piece targeting 450 lines or less: the LPVault keeps custody only (NFT in and out, fee collection, liquidity removal during settlement, NFT return) and a new immutable Collateralization contract holds the full-range check, credited-units math, backing records per account and market, seizure order, reconcile-on-exit, the pending-settlement balance and the exposure counters, and tells the Adapter how many vault tokens to mint and burn; LPVaultSwap keeps entry points, keeper and permissionless gating, `liquidationCall`, flash-mint orchestration and payouts, and an internal SettlementMath library holds the recovery floor, slippage bound, surplus split and the 20% protocol share, with both swap legs running through route contracts; AvanaConfig becomes the MarketRegistry (markets and their status, Spokes, debt reserves, routes and the None/Frozen/Paused switch) and the ParameterStore (every risk parameter with its tighten and loosen bounds); the AaveAdapter keeps user actions, mint and burn and proxy calls, and the opening checks move to the RiskModule (`Q-229`); the LPOracle keeps the price-state machine, grace latch and `poke`, and an internal LPPriceMath library holds the full-range price formula, square root and feed normalization. Interfaces are split by audience and kind: `I<Name>.sol` for external functions (role-based files for large surfaces: user actions, keeper, governance, views), `I<Name>Events.sol`, `I<Name>Errors.sol`, and shared structs and enums in `types/`. Libraries are internal, so no contract links an external library. Rules keep their IDs when they move to the page of the contract that now holds them. Amends `Q-148`, `Q-206`. **Amended by `Q-291`, `Q-321`, `Q-334`:** the AMM integration's LPVault makes the exact-shape check; LPVaultSwap orchestrates a flash loan of the debt asset through `IFlashSource` instead of a GHO flash mint; and each AMM protocol's pricing module replaces the full-range price formula. | An auditor reviews a file completely; a fixed ceiling keeps each file reviewable in one sitting and each concern in one place. Separating pure arithmetic into libraries lets each verification model map onto one file, and splitting interfaces by audience lets an integrator import only what it calls. | — |
| `Q-265` | Relisting after a cancelled registration | `cancelRegistration` keeps the cancelled `marketId` reserved for its pool. A later `registerMarket` for a pool whose vault token already exists in the VaultTokenFactory reuses the `marketId` that token carries and requires the token's `lpOracle()` to match the new registration; a pool without a token gets `marketCount + 1` as before. A pool maps to at most one market that is not cancelled. **Amended by `Q-299`:** vault tokens are keyed by (CDP Spoke, market key), so a pool can have several markets, one per exact shape, and a cancelled (Spoke, market) pair reuses its reserved `marketId`. | The factory's pool slot is write-once and each vault token carries its market ID as an immutable, so issuing a fresh ID after a cancellation left the pool with a token that no new market could accept and a factory that refused a second token: the pool could never be listed again. Reusing the reserved ID keeps every immutable binding valid. | — |
| `Q-268` | Previews that match execution | The Lens builds every preview so the transaction made from it succeeds when it lands in a later block. `previewLiquidation` computes `expectedUnitsSeized` with Aave's own collateral formula (`_calculateCollateralToLiquidate`) on the drawn shares and premium that the pinned sizing returns, capped by the account's supplied shares; for a GHO debt reserve it sets `debtToCover` to the account's whole debt plus `LENS_DUST_MARGIN_BPS` (500), `⌈total × 10,500 / 10⁴⌉` (Aave still repays only its target amount), and for any other reserve to that margined whole debt when the repayment is the whole debt, when the sizing seizes all of the account's supplied shares in the market, or when the debt left after the previewed amount is within the same margin of Aave's dust threshold; the margin keeps the call valid after interest accrues or a pledge lands before inclusion. Record paging returns an empty page when `offset ≥ nextRecordId` and otherwise walks `min(limit, nextRecordId − offset)` records, so no input overflows. `predictProxy` returns the Adapter's stored proxy when one exists and the CREATE2 prediction otherwise. `previewBorrowMax` sizes a "max" borrow to a health factor of `minOpeningHealthFactor × (1 + rate × (deadline − now)) × 1.005`, and `quoteRepayAll` returns `debt × (1 + rate × (deadline − now)) × 1.001`, rounded up, for both the repay cap and the approval (`Q-269`). Amends `Q-232`. **Amended by `Q-306`, `Q-319`:** the ProxyManager is immutable, so a predicted proxy address always equals the deployed one; `predictProxy` takes (wallet, Spoke, market), and previews size a flash loan of the debt asset. **Amended by `Q-398`:** the Lens shows a recommended borrow at health factor 1.10 next to the maximum at Aave's 1.00. | A preview is only useful if what it predicts is what executes: Aave sizes the seizure from shares and rounds the pulled debt up, a one-wei repay can move an account across the dust threshold between preview and inclusion, interest accrues between signing and inclusion, and a recomputed proxy address can differ from the stored one after a ProxyManager upgrade. Each of these made a correct-looking preview revert; building the margin into the preview removes the failure without changing any on-chain check. | — |
| `Q-286` | Three deployment operations | All three run through the chain's AvanaTimelock at the normal delay, followed by a short-delay activation that re-checks the live Aave configuration. `deployCdpSpoke(hub, integrationId, riskTier, implementation, aaveOracle)` deploys a standard Aave V4 Spoke and its AaveOracle and registers them under a Hub with one AMM integration, after checking the required Spoke settings of `Q-281`; the Aave DAO payload of the same listing then lists the Spoke on the Hub. `listCollateralMarket(spoke, def)` deploys the market's VaultToken (VaultTokenFactory) and LPOracle instance, records the exact range or shape after the integration's listing guards, binds the integration's LPVault and wires the Spoke reserve. `enableDebtReserve(spoke, reserveId, fundingHub, asset)` records the funding Hub and asset, checks the credit-line sums, the flash-liquidity and route-depth bounds, the Coverage Spoke's funding and the swap routes, and enables borrowing. Amends `Q-068`, `Q-119`. | Avana prepares the Spoke but the Aave DAO owns Hub listing, so the Aave payload and the Avana batch form one joint listing; naming three operations gives every listing the same checklist and the same activation check. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-21, AMMN-RT-15 (configuration pairing) |
| `Q-288` | Lens outside the audited core | The Lens is a periphery contract: read-only, deployed per chain, replaceable by a new deployment, and outside the audited immutable core. No core contract calls it, and nothing it returns authorizes an action. | It carries no security role, and keeping its 888-line interface in the immutable core adds audit cost and makes every view fix a core redeployment. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-08 |
| `Q-296` | Lock membership and routing in custody | Collateralization and every LPVault are not Lock members and never acquire it. Every value-moving ledger and LPVault function requires the Lock held by the AaveAdapter or LPVaultSwap; fee collection and zero-credit release go through the Adapter, which passes `msg.sender` as depositor and reads no pause or policy. `returnStray` is a timelock-only LPVault call that requires the Lock free and touches no record. Amends `Q-030`, `Q-104`, `Q-148`, `Q-223`. | An immutable Lock with exactly two members serves every later Spoke, market and AMM protocol; the LPVault still pays only the depositor of record, so a hostile Adapter can block but not redirect fees. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-01, AMMN-FLOW-01 |
| `Q-308` | One AaveAdapter per chain, scoped by Hub | One Adapter per chain takes each market's Hub, Spoke and integration from the MarketRegistry for new activity, and from the vault token's immutables and the binding stored at proxy creation for exits. It holds no Hub immutable. Entry points name `marketId`, `integrationId` and `positionKey`, and every amount is an n-token array. The Adapter checks that a given `integrationId` matches the market's (`IntegrationMismatch`). Fee collection, zero-credit release and `releaseIdle` are Adapter entry points; stray-position return stays the timelock-only LPVault call of `Q-296`. The pause state and its reason are kept per Hub, with `freezeAll` and `pauseAll`; the address freeze is one chain-wide set (`Q-285`). **Amended by `Q-396`:** `releaseIdle` no longer exists; fee collection and zero-credit release remain Adapter entry points. | A per-Hub Adapter gives no smaller blast radius, because the proxies, the Lock and LPVaultSwap are shared per chain. Per-Hub pauses stop one Hub without stopping others. An exploiting address threatens every Hub on its chain, so the freeze is chain-wide. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-03, AMMN-FLOW-19, AMMN-FLOW-24 (Adapter signatures) |
| `Q-312` | MarketRegistry: Hubs, funding Hubs, credit lines and deployment operations | The registry records Hubs with their tier; each CDP Spoke's home Hub, integration, tier, standard implementation and AaveOracle; each debt reserve's funding Hub, funding asset ID and the Coverage Spoke of that (funding Hub, asset); and each market's pending-settlement reserve (`settlementReserveId`). One registry sums `drawCap`s per (funding Hub, asset) against the line the funding Hub's governance records, and each line is at most `fundedBalance / coverageRatioBps` (`Q-353`). Cross-Hub lines are specified and off at launch (`Q-283`); a pause of the funding Hub stops borrowing on its lines. The three timelocked deployment operations are those of `Q-286`; `enableDebtReserve` also checks the reserve's identity, Coverage Spoke, flash support (the flash source's lender is never the funding Hub), swap routes from every pool token into the asset and liquidation liveness (`Q-317`). Buy and sell-back routes are retired; swap routes are keyed by (market, token, debt asset, kind). The vault-token decimals rule no longer requires an even decimal sum. | The funding Hub's suppliers bear the loss, so that Hub owns the cap, and only a sum across all drawing Spokes bounds it. The Aave DAO owns Hub listing, so deployment splits into Avana steps plus one batched Aave payload per Spoke. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-06, AMMN-FLOW-21, AMMN-SM-01, AMMN-RT-11 (registry level) |
| `Q-318` | Lock: exactly two members, one Lock per chain | The Lock's members are the chain's AaveAdapter and LPVaultSwap, constructor immutables at their fixed proxy addresses. Collateralization and every LPVault are not members: each value-moving entry point checks its caller and `holder() ∈ {AaveAdapter, LPVaultSwap}` and never acquires. Fee collection and zero-credit release are AaveAdapter outer entry points; stray-position return is the timelock-only LPVault call of `Q-296`. `acquire()` costs one TLOAD, one TSTORE and at most two immutable comparisons. Amends `Q-148`, `Q-223`. | With per-deployment custody contracts as immutable members, a CDP Spoke, market or LPVault deployed later could never join, and every flow on it would revert `NotMember`. A storage registry adds a cold read per call and an upgrade surface. Two fixed members plus callee-side checks make the member set independent of how many Hubs, Spokes, markets and AMM protocols the chain runs. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-01, AMMN-GAS-09 |
| `Q-319` | Lens views per market | One immutable Lens per chain, outside the audited core (`Q-288`): no core contract calls it, stores it or depends on its output. It has no Hub, Spoke, LPVault or AMM-protocol immutable and reads each market from the MarketRegistry. Account views are per (wallet, market) because each market has its own PositionProxy; `getAccounts` lists several. Positions are named by `(integrationId, positionKey)`; amounts are n-token arrays in pool order. Pause views take the Hub. `previewBorrowMax` also returns the minimum first borrow implied by `minDebtUsd`. Liquidation previews size the flash loan of the debt asset and check its available liquidity and premium against `maxFlashFeeBps`. Amends `Q-232`, `Q-268`. | A Lens defect can only mislead a caller into a reverting or less profitable call; it cannot move value, so auditing it with the core spends audit time without reducing risk. Keeping it free of deployment constants keeps it valid as markets are listed. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-08, AMMN-GAS-02 (minimum-debt preview), AMMN-FLOW-24 (Lens views) |
| `Q-365` | External dependencies per chain | 4.3 records dependencies per chain (Ethereum and Base). Aave V4: standard Hubs and Spokes; Base V4 is live with an equities Hub and no GHO. Flash: one source per chain, the Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` (single asset, mode 0); premium, idle liquidity and reserve flags are read at each call, and `FLASH_BORROWER` is requested by AIP per Pool. GHO's flash minter and the GSMs are not dependencies. AMM protocols: Uniswap v3 (Ethereum) and Aerodrome Slipstream (Base: unstaked positions, one factory and position-manager generation per market); others only after their readiness guards pass. Base sequencer: uptime feed `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433` with a 3,600 s grace period for new risk. Hub halt: stops add, remove, draw, restore and share transfers; deficit elimination, fee shares and premium refresh continue, so an Aave-side halt is containment with a playbook entry and a keeper retry. GSM: the seized GSMs are not used; a GSM can serve only as a backup route after a live check that it is not seized or frozen and has cap and liquidity. New IDs `EXT-028`…`EXT-032`. | Base has no FlashMinter and no GSM, and Aave V4 has no flash loans; flash-borrowing the debt asset removes the leg that bought the debt with GHO. GSM state changes over time (seized, frozen, about $0.03 of buy-side liquidity), so only verified facts are stated and a live check is required. | Resolves AMMN-INT-02…08, AMMN-FLOW-02, AMMN-FLOW-03, AMMN-FLOW-10, AMMN-FLOW-12, AMMN-FLOW-14, AMMN-RT-08, AMMN-RT-09, AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-ARCH-04, AMMN-ARCH-05 |
| `Q-377` | Lock constructor and inner callees | The Lock's constructor takes `aaveAdapter` and `lpVaultSwap` only. Collateralization and every LPVault are inner callees reachable only inside a call tree a member opened. Their former outer entry points move to the Adapter (`releaseZeroCredit`, `collectFees`). The LPVault's timelock-only stray-position return requires the Lock free and does not acquire it. Same rule as `Q-318`, stated for the interfaces. | Immutable membership listing the vault and ledger means a later Spoke's or AMM protocol's LPVault can never join, and every flow on it reverts. Two fixed proxy addresses never change, so new Spokes, markets and LPVaults need no Lock redeploy. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-01, AMMN-GAS-09, AMMN-ARCH-01 |
| `Q-378` | MarketRegistry records, cross-Hub lines, drift check and per-Hub pause | No `HUB`, `GHO`, `NFPM`, `UNISWAP_V3_FACTORY`, `GHO_FLASH_MINTER` or `LP_VAULT` immutables. The registry records Hubs with a `RiskTier`, AMM integrations (`registerIntegration(integrationId, lpVault, pricing)`), Coverage Spokes per (Hub, asset) and the `IFlashSource`. The three timelock deployment operations of `Q-286` take `SpokeDef{hub, integrationId, riskTier, implementation, aaveOracle}`, `MarketDef` and `DebtReserveDef{fundingHub, crossHub, coverageSpoke, ...}`; `enableDebtReserve` checks the budget windows, flash source and swap routes. Cross-Hub credit lines: `setCrossHubLine(fundingHub, asset, spoke, cap)`, summed per (funding Hub, asset), capped by that Hub's first-loss coverage, off at launch (`crossHubLinesEnabled = false`), stopped by a pause of the funding Hub. `spokeSettingsDrift(spoke)` reports `receiveSharesEnabled`, `liquidationFee`, reinvestment controller, implementation and Hub drift; drift blocks new activity only. Pause, freeze and their reasons are per Hub. | Avana uses a standard Aave V4 Spoke, so the required settings are the only barrier against receive-shares liquidations that would leave unsettleable vault-token shares. Copied configuration paired with the wrong position manager or factory is refused by checking the market against its registered integration. Per-Hub pause keeps one Hub's incident from stopping another. | Resolves AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-ARCH-07, AMMN-RT-03, AMMN-SM-04, AMMN-INT-07, AMMN-SM-01, AMMN-RT-11, AMMN-FLOW-05, AMMN-SM-06, AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-FLOW-19, AMMN-ARCH-02 |

## AMM protocols and integrations

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-291` | AMM-neutral custody and integration interface | One immutable LPVault per AMM protocol per chain implements the AMM integration interface (2.19: custody, composition, unwind, fees, risk metadata). The VaultTokenFactory keeps a write-once `lpVaultOf[integrationId]` registry set by the timelock; Collateralization trusts an LPVault only through it. The core (ledger, vault tokens, Adapter, LPVaultSwap) calls no AMM protocol contract. Uniswap v3 on Ethereum and Aerodrome Slipstream on Base are fully specified; Uniswap v2, Curve StableSwap-NG, Balancer weighted and stable, and Uniswap v4 are supported by the core and listable once their readiness guards pass (`Q-302`). Supersedes `Q-001`. | Adding an AMM protocol adds an LPVault, a pricing library and an LPOracle implementation, with no change to the immutable ledger, factory or Lock; a hostile registration reaches only that integration's markets. | `Q-001`. Resolves AMMN-ARCH-01, AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-FLOW-10 |
| `Q-292` | Position and market keys | Records, events and indexes use (`integrationId`, `positionKey`), never `tokenId`. NFT positions: `positionKey = bytes32(tokenId)` on Uniswap v3 and `bytes32((generation << 248) \| tokenId)` on Slipstream, whose three position-manager generations share token IDs; fungible LP: `keccak256(marketKey, depositor, nonce)`. `marketKey = keccak256(abi.encode(chainid, integrationId, pool, poolId, shape))`; pools carry a `bytes32 poolKey` (address or pool ID). | `tokenId`s collide across position managers (three on Slipstream on Base) and chains; Uniswap v4 and Balancer pools have no address. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-24, AMMN-ARCH-06 |
| `Q-293` | Exact shape at pledge | Each market stores its pool and exact shape write-once in its LPVault at listing; a pledge requires an exact match (for concentrated liquidity, `tickLower` and `tickUpper` equal to the market's), checked by the LPVault and again by Collateralization through `marketOf(positionKey) == vaultToken.marketKey()`. Range alignment uses signed `int24` `%`. Composition is fair composition at the clamped oracle price. Supersedes `Q-017`. | A unit of liquidity is worth a different amount in every range; a narrow position credited at a wider market's per-unit value is overvalued ×49.9 (±1 tick against ±50 ticks) to ×979. | `Q-017`. Resolves AMMN-RT-01, AMMN-FLOW-08 |
| `Q-297` | n-token amounts and hooks | Every amount array has one entry per market token (2 to 8, `MAX_POOL_TOKENS`); pending settlement holds `amounts[]`. Native ETH is wrapped to WETH by the integration and accepted only under the Lock; rate tokens need an approved capped rate feed; `feesSeparable` is false for AMM protocols that roll fees into the position (`collectFees` reverts `FeesNotSeparable`); `topUpPosition` for fungible LP; single-token exits are allowed with an oracle floor on the one output. | The ledger must not assume two tokens; Uniswap v2, Curve and Balancer roll fees in; Curve and Uniswap v4 pay native ETH; 8-token proportional exits cost about 3.0M gas per record. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-13, AMMN-FLOW-24, AMMN-ARCH-06 |
| `Q-298` | Vault-token decimals by value | `d` is the smallest integer in [6, 18] with `10^d · v ≥ $1`, `v` the fair value of one unit at the listing reference prices; activation requires the whole-token price in [$0.50, $20.00]. Replaces `d = (d0 + d1)/2`, which gives the same value for full-range Uniswap v3 pairs. The lower bound 6 is the one `Q-325` and the VaultTokenFactory (`DecimalsOutOfRange`) apply. | Aave needs `d ≤ 18`; the old rule assumed the full-range value `2·√(p0·p1)` and does not fit ranges or fungible LP. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-08 |
| `Q-301` | Slipstream integration | One Slipstream LPVault on Base with an immutable allowlist of the four position-manager and factory generations; each market pins one (factory, position manager) pair at listing with factory, pool and clone-code checks; unstaked NFTs only, gauge-owned NFTs refused by `ownerOf == pool.gauge()` (`GaugeStakedPosition`), no approvals or staking; fees read live; the unstaked-fee share is shown as borrower yield only. A later generation needs a new LPVault. | Slipstream differs from Uniswap v3 in `getPool` keying (by `tickSpacing`), the `positions()` and 6-field `slot0` layouts, dynamic fees and multiple position managers; staking moves the NFT to the gauge with its fees. | Resolves AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-GAS-14, AMMN-FLOW-10 |
| `Q-302` | Listing guards for later AMM protocols | Before its first market: Uniswap v2 uses `getReserves` and effective supply including pending `feeTo` liquidity, spot within 1%, and a minimum supply; Curve and Balancer value at the minimum token price (or the weighted invariant at actual supply) and probe the pool or vault reentrancy lock before every read; Uniswap v4 lists only hookless pools or allowlisted immutable hooks with no remove-liquidity bits or deltas and refuses subscribed positions; every AMM protocol has a per-block quote-change cap. | Read-only reentrancy, donations, fee-mint overstatement and removal hooks each let a pool overstate or withhold value. | Resolves AMMN-RT-06, AMMN-RT-07, AMMN-GAS-03 |
| `Q-305` | Pool checks in the integration | Pool identity (pinned factory and position manager, `getPool`, init-code or clone check) and the pool-lock read (`slot0.unlocked` in the correct layout) are made by the LPVault, never by the ledger; the Uniswap v3 constructor requires `NFPM.factory() == FACTORY`. | A copied configuration can pair the wrong factory and position manager, and a Uniswap v3 ABI misreads Slipstream's `slot0`. | Resolves AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-FLOW-10 |
| `Q-313` | AMM integrations pinned per chain; no AMM, Hub or debt-asset immutables in the core | `registerIntegration` pins each AMM protocol's LPVault, position manager, factory or pool manager, and pool code hash. It checks that `lpVault.INTEGRATION_ID()`, `POSITION_MANAGER()` and `FACTORY()` match the integration record; that `positionManager.factory()` matches the pinned factory; at listing, that `factory.getPool(...) == pool` with the integration's keying parameter (fee for Uniswap v3, `tickSpacing` for Slipstream); and that `pool.factory()` is the pinned factory and the pool's code hash matches (the Uniswap v3 init-code derivation, or a Slipstream clone of `poolImplementation`). `HUB`, `GHO`, `GHO_FLASH_MINTER`, `NFPM` and `UNISWAP_V3_FACTORY` are removed from the MarketRegistry, the RiskModule and the Adapter. Hubs, integrations and debt assets are records. | On Base, Uniswap v3 and Slipstream share interfaces, and Slipstream has three factory generations; copied configuration could pair the wrong position manager and factory, or bind a fork pool. Records instead of immutables let the core serve new Hubs and AMM protocols without an upgrade. | Resolves AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-GAS-16 (immutables) |
| `Q-321` | AMM-neutral LPOracle: price-state core plus a pricing module per AMM protocol | The LPOracle is a price-state core that works on n-token price vectors (`2 ≤ n ≤ MAX_POOL_TOKENS = 8`, no token0/token1 in core rules) and an internal pricing module per AMM protocol per chain, compiled into that protocol's LPOracle implementation; every market is a proxy of its protocol's implementation. The module supplies `composition(units, prices)` (the same function the AMM integration's composition view uses), `consistency(quotes)`, `poolLocked()` and `listingChecks`. The concentrated-liquidity module (Uniswap v3, Aerodrome Slipstream) is specified on 2.10; other AMM protocols' modules are specified in their readiness sections. `price8` is the fair composition at the valuation price vector (`SPEC-ORC-008`), never spot, 50/50 or weights × price. Reason bits become a market bitmap plus per-token bitmaps; `getTokenPrices()` returns a price vector. Amends `Q-028`, `Q-191`. | One audited price-state machine serves every AMM protocol; only the composition differs, and the composition is the minimum of the holding's value over pool states (AMM-A1a, B1a, C1a, C2c), so one valuation rule is safe for all. | Supports AMMN-RT-02 and the readiness guards |
| `Q-325` | Concentrated-liquidity pricing | The oracle sqrt price (`SPEC-ORC-009`) is clamped to `[sqrtRatio(tickLower), sqrtRatio(tickUpper)]`; amounts come from `getAmountsForLiquidity` rounding down; each amount is reduced by one raw unit; `price8 = max(1, Σ ⌊a'_i·v_i/10^d_i⌋)`. Tick alignment is checked on signed `int24` (`tick % spacing == 0`). Uncollected fees are excluded. The vault-token decimals `d` is the smallest integer in [6, 18] with a whole token worth at least $1 at listing prices (`Q-298`). Listing shock check: `price8 ≥ 10^6` at prices moved by `LISTING_SHOCK_FACTOR`. Amends `Q-066`, `Q-191`. | Unclamped math overvalues a depegged narrow-range market ×2.99 (worked example on 2.10); floors plus one raw unit keep the price at or below the exact fair value (AMM-A3f, MATH-UO-51); a `uint24` cast accepts misaligned negative ticks (AMM-A5c); fees are wash-tradable. A value-based `d` works for any range width and odd decimal sums. | Resolves AMMN-RT-02, AMMN-FLOW-21 (decimals, oracle side) |
| `Q-370` | n-token arrays and market shape | Every amount, price, minimum, counter and route is an array in the pool's own token order, length n with 2 ≤ n ≤ `MAX_POOL_TOKENS` = 8; a wrong length reverts `LengthMismatch`. A market is (chain, AMM integration, pool address or pool id, exact `PositionShape`), with `ShapeKind {TickRange, FullPool}`. Fee tier and tick spacing are not market fields; the integration stores them in its per-market configuration (`ClMarketConfig`). The vault-token decimals come from the integration's unit definition, checked in [6, 18]. The two-token views (`PledgeCounters`, `PledgePreview`, `CapView`, `PriceView`, `PendingView`, `SettlementReport`) and `Pending`, `InitParams`, `PriceStateView` are n-token. `SettlementReport` is denominated in the repaid debt asset and has no GHO buy leg. | Curve StableSwap-NG and Balancer pools hold up to 8 tokens; a fixed pair shape would force a second immutable ledger and factory for any later AMM protocol. The exact shape in the market key makes the exact-range match (AMMN-RT-01) a key equality. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-RT-01 (interface), AMMN-GAS-15 (`PledgeCounters` packing) |
| `Q-371` | AMM integration interface and its hooks | `amm/IAmmIntegration` has five groups: custody (`receivePosition`, `returnPosition`, `validatePosition`, plus `bindMarket` once at listing by the MarketRegistry), composition (`composition(marketId, units, prices)` returning n amounts and an `exact` flag), unwind (`unwind(positionKey, units, minAmounts, recipient)` and `releaseToDepositor`), fees (`collectFees(positionKey, depositor)`), and risk metadata (`riskMetadata`: in range, read-safe, pool share). `amm/IAmmIntegrationHooks` covers what one shape does not fit: `checkReadSafe` (Uniswap v3 and Slipstream `slot0.unlocked`, Curve and Balancer reentrancy guards), `consistencyDeviation` (TWAP check, `available = false` where none exists), `rateProviders`, `wrappedNative`, `isAllowedPoolHook` (Uniswap v4 hook allowlist with no remove-liquidity or delta flags), `effectiveSupply` (Uniswap v2 supply plus pending `feeTo` liquidity). `IntegrationCapabilities` fixes `PoolKeying {Address, Id}`, `FeeMode {Separate, RolledIn}`, `PricingMode {ClosedForm, LowerBound}`. Each AMM protocol's immutable LPVault implements both (`ILPVault`). `IClIntegration` is the shared concentrated-liquidity extension; `IUniswapV3Integration` and `ISlipstreamIntegration` add the protocol specifics (Slipstream: `tickSpacing`-keyed factory, 6-field `slot0`, dynamic fees, three position-manager generations encoded in the high byte of `positionKey`, unstaked NFTs only). Every LPVault and Collateralization entry point that moves value is callable only while the AaveAdapter or LPVaultSwap holds the Lock; depositor fee collection and zero-credit release go through the AaveAdapter; `returnStray` requires the Lock free and touches no record. | Composition at the oracle price vector is the only quote proved never above the redeemable value (AMM-A1a, AMM-B1, AMM-C2c); spot reserves, `balanceOf` and max-price quotes are manipulable (AMM-A1c, AMM-B7, AMM-C2d). Hooks with neutral values let callers never branch on the AMM protocol. A fixed two-member Lock means new AMM protocols and markets never need a Lock redeploy. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-ARCH-01, AMMN-ARCH-03, AMMN-FLOW-01 (custody side), AMMN-RT-02 (clamp in `IClIntegration.oracleSqrtPrice`), the Slipstream decoding and staking findings |
| `Q-374` | Vault tokens and the factory are AMM-neutral and multi-Hub | The VaultTokenFactory (one per chain, immutable) keeps two append-only registries: `registerIntegration(integrationId, lpVault)` and one vault token per (CDP Spoke, market key) (`Q-299`), salted into CREATE3. `deployVaultToken` takes the integration, pool or pool id, shape, Hub, CDP Spoke, marketId, LPOracle and decimals. Each vault token carries `marketKey`, `integrationId`, `integration` (its LPVault), and its own `hub` (the market's home Hub); `pool()` is removed. The transfer rule is the allowlist of `Q-300`, which makes LPVaultSwap the only effective liquidator on a standard Spoke. `IAaveV4Spoke` is the standard Spoke: the liquidation-manager getters are removed and `DriftReason.LiquidationManager` keeps its ordinal but is never reported. | Collateralization is immutable and cannot read an upgradeable registry for market identity; append-only factory registries give it immutable sources while still admitting new AMM protocols and markets. Two ranges of one pool are two markets, and a market relisted on a successor Spoke gets a new token. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-RT-03 (interface side) |

## Custody, vault tokens and minting

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-009` | LP fees while pledged | Trading fees belong to the borrower. Liquidity stays in the pool and keeps earning. Withdrawal returns the NFT with any uncollected fees; after a partial liquidation the borrower keeps the remaining liquidity and accrued fees. | LPOracle values liquidity only, so fees are not collateral; leaving them with the borrower keeps the LP's yield, which is the reason to pledge an LP rather than sell it. | — |
| `Q-011` | Collecting fees while pledged | The depositor of record can collect fees from a pledged NFT at any time, at any health factor and in every Avana state, through the LPVault (`Q-030`). Collection never reduces liquidity (no non-zero `decreaseLiquidity`), never mints or burns vault tokens, and pays only the depositor of record. Liquidation sizing and proceeds estimates never count uncollected fees. `Q-218` states how principal owed at pledge is treated. | Collecting does not change collateral value or health factor, so blocking it protects nothing. Excluding fees from proceeds estimates stops a borrower's last-minute collection from leaving a liquidation short. | — |
| `Q-030` | Fee-collection mechanism | After a pledge the NFT belongs to the LPVault. The depositor of record calls the LPVault's fee-collection function; the vault checks the caller is that depositor, calls Uniswap `collect()` and transfers the fees to the depositor. Fee collection never consults the upgradeable policy contracts (`Q-122`). **Amended by `Q-296`, `Q-297`:** the depositor calls the AaveAdapter's `collectFees`, which passes `msg.sender` as depositor and reads no pause or policy, and the LPVault still pays only the depositor of record; an AMM protocol that rolls fees into the position has no separate collection (`FeesNotSeparable`). | Only the NFT owner can call `collect()`, so the vault must do it; fixing both the caller and the recipient to the depositor means no one else can redirect fees. | — |
| `Q-066` | Vault-token units | Per-market decimals (6–18) fixed at market creation; multiplier 1: one base unit is one unit of credited Uniswap v3 liquidity, minted and burned in exact amounts. LPOracle prices one whole token at full precision in Aave's 8-decimal format; decimals are chosen per market for price precision, cap granularity and small liquidations (target about $1–10 per whole token). Credited liquidity per NFT record is at most `MAX_CREDITED_LIQUIDITY` (`type(uint120).max`). Invariants: each market's total supply equals the sum of credited backing; each record's live liquidity is at least its credited units. **Amended by `Q-298`, `Q-325`:** one base unit is one unit of the position measure the AMM integration credits, and `d` is the smallest integer in [6, 18] with a whole token worth at least $1 at the listing prices. | A 1:1 unit makes backing checkable with one equality and no exchange-rate rounding; per-market decimals keep the whole-token price where 8-decimal prices and USD caps are precise. | — |
| `Q-067` | Pledge and withdraw rules | Only the NFT's current owner can pledge it, and that owner becomes the depositor of record. Liquidity added to a pledged NFT by anyone stays with the NFT and is never collateral. Withdrawal returns the whole NFT, only to the depositor of record. NFTs that reach the vault any other way are not credited and are handled by `Q-104`. **Amended by `Q-254`:** the depositor of record can remove part of the credited liquidity and can credit liquidity added to a pledged NFT through a re-credit with every top-up check; whole-NFT withdrawal is unchanged. | Fixing the record to the owner at pledge stops anyone pledging an NFT they do not own; ignoring added liquidity stops a record's collateral growing outside the cap and oracle checks. | — |
| `Q-068` | Vault-token rules | Each vault token has immutable Hub, LPVault and LPVaultSwap addresses (predictable CREATE3 deployment) and recognises proxies registered by the immutable ProxyFactory (`Q-120`); no owner, no setters. Allowed holders: Hub, LPVault, LPVaultSwap and registered proxies; transfers to anyone else revert. On Aave: native liquidation fee 0, not borrowable, `receiveShares` disabled. Avana operations require Hub shares == assets for the token (`Q-103`, `Q-189`). **Amended by `Q-224`:** each vault token names the Adapter as minter and burner; transfers check only the recipient (Hub, Adapter, registered proxy, LPVaultSwap); the proxy registry is inside the token. **Amended by `Q-300`, `Q-374`:** the transfer rule checks sender and recipient (`Q-300`): supply from a registered proxy into the Hub, withdrawal from the Hub to a registered proxy and seizure from the Hub to LPVaultSwap; each token also carries its market key, integration and home Hub. | Restricting holders keeps vault tokens from reaching a liquidator or market where they could be redeemed outside the LPVault's ledger. A zero liquidation fee and no share payouts mean every seized unit reaches LPVaultSwap and is burned against backing. | `Q-026` |
| `Q-069` | Mint and burn authority | Superseded by `Q-224`. Was: the immutable LPVault as the vault token's only minter and burner. | — | — |
| `Q-094` | Mint flow | Superseded by `Q-224`. Was: the LPVault minting vault tokens to the borrower's proxy at pledge. | — | — |
| `Q-104` | Stray NFTs | NFTs that reach the LPVault without a pledge are not credited. Governance can return an uncredited NFT to an address proven off-chain, through one AccessManager-scheduled call at `upgradeDelay` (14 days). Credited NFTs can never be moved by this path. **Amended by `Q-228`:** the return is one Avana timelock operation at the normal delay (5 days). **Amended by `Q-258`:** a stray NFT cannot be pledged with the LPVault or Collateralization as depositor. **Amended by `Q-296`:** the return is a timelock-only LPVault call that requires the Lock free and touches no record. | Return-to-sender fails for NFTs sent from contracts or wrong addresses; the delay and the credited check stop the path being used against collateral. | — |
| `Q-120` | Vault-token holders and proxy registry | Superseded by `Q-224`. Was: an immutable ProxyFactory as the only proxy registry writer, with the Adapter excluded from holders and the registry. | — | — |
| `Q-123` | Pledge flow | Superseded by `Q-224`. Was: pledge through `LPVault.pledge()`, with the LPVault minting and calling the proxy's `supplyPledgedCollateral()`. | — | — |
| `Q-124` | Dust and balances | Contracts check amounts received or spent within a call (before/after deltas), never absolute balances. Stray dust is ignored; there is no sweep role. **Amended by `Q-271`:** every later step uses the measured amount, and a check that compared a received amount with an expected one accepts a smaller amount where the token takes a fee. | A donation cannot change a delta-based check (for example to force a revert or inflate proceeds), and without a sweep role there is no privileged way to move tokens out. | — |
| `Q-134` | Record after full seizure | A record whose credited units fall to 0 stays in place; the depositor can collect fees and withdraw the NFT with no debt check; no one else can touch it. | Fees and any added liquidity left in a fully seized NFT still belong to the depositor; a zero-credit record backs no debt, so releasing it cannot affect solvency. | — |
| `Q-218` | Owed tokens at pledge | An NFT pledged with non-zero owed amounts (`tokensOwed0`, `tokensOwed1`: principal left by an earlier `decreaseLiquidity` without `collect`, plus uncollected fees) brings them into custody without credit: the LPVault credits only the position's `liquidity`, LPOracle never values owed amounts, and `collectFees` pays all owed amounts to the depositor of record. Seizure and reconciliation collect only the principal their own `decreaseLiquidity` returned (`Q-061`), so principal owed before the pledge stays with the depositor. No Avana computation derives fees as collected minus removed. | Principal owed at pledge belongs to the depositor who removed it before pledging; crediting it would mint vault tokens against tokens that are not liquidity, and computing fees as collected minus removed would count that principal as fees. | — |
| `Q-224` | Vault-token mint, burn and holders | **Transfer rule superseded by `Q-300`:** mint only to a registered proxy, burn only from a registered proxy or LPVaultSwap, and transfers only on the paths proxy → Hub, Hub → proxy and Hub → LPVaultSwap (2.4 `REQ-VTOKEN-006`); the recipient-only rule below no longer applies, and the rest of this decision stands. The AaveAdapter (its fixed proxy address, an immutable of each vault token) is each vault token's only minter and only burner (`OnlyAdapter()`). `mint(to, amount)` reverts unless `to` is the Hub, the Adapter or a registered proxy. `burn(from, amount)` burns from any holder without an allowance. Transfers check only the recipient, which must be the Hub, the Adapter, a registered proxy or LPVaultSwap (which receives seized vault tokens from the Hub in `liquidationCall`); the sender is not checked. Neither the LPVault nor Collateralization ever holds vault tokens, so neither is a recipient. The proxy registry lives in each vault token: `addValidProxy(proxy)`, Adapter only, append-only, emits `ProxyAdded(proxy)`; the Adapter registers a proxy in a market's token at its first pledge into that market. The token has no owner, setter, pause or upgrade. The LPVault keeps NFT custody, fee collection, liquidity removal and the NFT return; Collateralization keeps backing records, credit math, exposure counters, seizure debits and the pending-settlement balance. Pledge: Collateralization returns the credited units and the Adapter mints exactly that amount to the proxy and has the proxy supply it in the same transaction. Withdrawal: the proxy withdraws to itself, the Adapter burns exactly the released units from the proxy, then Collateralization debits the record and the LPVault returns the NFT. Liquidation: LPVaultSwap asks the Adapter to burn the seized units from LPVaultSwap (`burnSeized`) between `beginSeizure` and `settleSeizure` and before `settlePending`, and Collateralization requires the proxy's Spoke shares and the token's total supply each to have fallen by exactly the debited units. After every pledge and withdrawal the Adapter requires `totalSupply == totalCredited + pendingUnits` for the market; on the seizure and pending-settlement paths Collateralization checks the supply drop itself. Supersedes `Q-069`, `Q-094`, `Q-120`, `Q-123`, `Q-165`; amends `Q-068`, `Q-160`, `Q-201`, `Q-211`. **Amended by `Q-257`:** the Adapter's check is one-sided and keeps the market's backing gap unchanged. **Amended by `Q-254`, `Q-255`:** the Adapter also mints in a re-credit and burns in a partial removal and a repay with collateral, exactly the units Collateralization books. **Amended by `Q-300`, `Q-307`:** transfers check sender and recipient by the allowlist of `Q-300`, with no Hub-to-Adapter transfer (withdrawn units reach the proxy and are burned there), and the Adapter registers a proxy in the token of its one market. | The contract that moves vault tokens into and out of Aave for the borrower also holds mint and burn, so the backing ledger and the supply change in one call without a token round trip. Checking recipients keeps vault tokens inside the Hub, the Adapter, LPVaultSwap and registered proxies, the only places Avana's flows put them, so a liquidator or market outside Avana can never receive one. The backing equality now depends on the reviewed Adapter; Collateralization's share-drop and supply-drop checks on seizure still stop a faulty LPVaultSwap from releasing liquidity that was not burned. | `Q-069`, `Q-094`, `Q-120`, `Q-123`, `Q-165` |
| `Q-254` | Partial liquidity removal and re-credit | A depositor can remove part of a pledged NFT's credited liquidity with `AaveAdapter.removeLiquidity(spoke, vaultToken, tokenId, units, amount0Min, amount1Min, deadline, minHealthFactor)`, for `0 < units < credited` after reconciliation (`Q-201`): the proxy withdraws `units` vault tokens, the Adapter burns them, Collateralization debits the record by `units` and releases its exposure charge with the exit-release formula, and the LPVault decreases the NFT's liquidity by exactly `units` and collects only the principal that removal returned, paid to the depositor of record (`Q-061`); the NFT stays in custody and keeps its fees. With no debt on any debt reserve the call reads no pause state, breaker, freeze or price. With debt it is a withdraw-with-debt: Paused refuses it, a frozen wallet cannot make it, the RiskModule's pre-checks and the health check of `Q-253` apply, and the record left behind must be worth at least `minPledgeUsd`. A depositor can credit liquidity added to a pledged NFT through Uniswap `increaseLiquidity` with `AaveAdapter.recredit(spoke, vaultToken, tokenId, deadline)`: after reconciliation the uncredited liquidity `live − credited` is checked and booked as a top-up of exactly that amount with every top-up check (rescue rules of `Q-252` included), the Adapter mints it to the proxy and supplies it, and the record's charge basis becomes the remaining charge plus the new charge over the new credited units, so later exits still release exactly what each unit carries. Only the depositor of record calls either function. Whole-NFT withdrawal is unchanged. Amends `Q-061`, `Q-067`, `Q-224`. **Amended by `Q-376`:** the call is `removeLiquidity(marketId, integrationId, positionKey, units, minAmounts, deadline, minHealthFactor)`, with one minimum per market token. | LPs rebalance and add to positions; with only whole-NFT withdrawal a partial exit needed a repay, a withdrawal and a new pledge, and liquidity added to a pledged NFT sat outside the collateral its owner thought it backed. Both operations change the record and the vault-token supply by the same units in one call, keep the NFT's live liquidity at least its credited units, and run every check the equivalent withdrawal or top-up runs, so they add no risk the existing paths do not already bound. Tokens from a partial removal go only to the depositor of record, and a moved pool price can only cost the depositor, who sets the minimum amounts; the remaining collateral is valued at oracle prices, which a pool trade cannot move. | — |
| `Q-257` | One-sided backing check | The backing check keeps the market's backing gap rather than an absolute equality.
