After every pledge, withdrawal, partial removal, re-credit and repay with collateral the Adapter requires `totalSupply ≤ totalCredited + pending.units` and that `totalCredited + pending.units − totalSupply`, read after its call into Collateralization, equals the same quantity read before its first mint or burn of the call (`BackingMismatch` otherwise). Collateralization's `settlePending(x)` requires `totalSupply + x ≤ totalCredited + pending.units`. The invariant is `totalSupply ≤ totalCredited + pending.units` for any LPVaultSwap implementation, with equality while every `burnSeized` is followed in the same call by the `settleSeizure` or `settlePending` that debits it. Amends `Q-224`. | With an absolute equality, one `burnSeized` that a faulty LPVaultSwap never settled would make every later pledge and withdrawal in the market revert, debt-free exits included, and only an Adapter upgrade five days later could restore them. The one-sided form still proves that every vault token in circulation is backed and that conforming calls keep the equality, while the market keeps working; the liquidity behind an orphan burn stays in custody, so the vault tokens in circulation stay more than fully backed. | — |
| `Q-258` | Stray NFTs cannot be pledged | `LPVault.receiveNft(owner, tokenId)` reverts `NotTokenOwner` when `owner` is the LPVault itself, and `Collateralization.pledge` reverts `InvalidDepositor` when the depositor is the LPVault or Collateralization. Amends `Q-104`. **Amended by `Q-371`:** the custody call is the integration's `receivePosition`; the depositor checks are unchanged. | An NFT sent to the LPVault without a pledge waits for its governance return. The ownership check passes for such an NFT when the claimed owner is the vault, and a transfer from the vault to itself succeeds, so a faulty Adapter could credit it, borrow against it and later return it to an Avana contract; refusing the vault and Collateralization as depositors closes that path for any Adapter. | — |
| `Q-294` | Record limit | At most 16 open records per account per market (`MAX_RECORDS_PER_ACCOUNT_MARKET`), rescue top-ups included; a borrower at the limit re-credits an existing position (or uses `topUpPosition` for fungible LP). A listing's measured 16-record seizure must stay at most 8,000,000 gas. Amends `Q-194`, `Q-230`. | Aave's dust rule seizes the last chunk whole; 125 records of $8 need 31.3M gas, beyond the 16,777,216 per-transaction cap. 16 records cost about 5.15M (1.31M + 16 × 0.24M). | Resolves AMMN-GAS-01, AMMN-GAS-13 |
| `Q-295` | Hub-keyed counters in one ledger per chain | `tokenUsd`, `depositorUsd` and `poolUnits` are keyed by the vault token's Hub in the chain's single Collateralization, summed over every Spoke and market of the Hub; `marketUsd` stays per market. Amends `Q-055`. | Per-Spoke ledgers would admit each Hub-wide cap once per Spoke, and two ranges on one pool would double the pool-share cap. | Resolves AMMN-RT-05 |
| `Q-299` | Vault-token registry key | The factory keys tokens by (CDP Spoke, `marketKey`), CREATE3 salt `keccak256(abi.encode(spoke, marketKey))`, written once per pair; `tokenOfPool` is retired. Amends `Q-265`. | A pool can be listed with two ranges, and a wound-down market can be relisted on a successor Spoke (`Q-355`). | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-11, AMMN-RT-12 |
| `Q-300` | Sender-aware vault-token transfer rule | A market's vault-token balance changes only in these ways, and everything else reverts, zero-amount transfers included: mint by the AaveAdapter to a registered proxy of the market; supply from a registered proxy into the market's home Hub (the CDP Spoke as spender); withdrawal from the home Hub to a registered proxy (`Spoke.withdraw` sends to its caller, the proxy); seizure from the home Hub to LPVaultSwap; burn by the AaveAdapter from a registered proxy or from LPVaultSwap. There is no Hub-to-Adapter transfer; proxy-to-proxy and LPVaultSwap-to-Hub transfers revert. The rule relies on the standard Spoke's `receiveSharesEnabled = false`, `liquidationFee = 0` (or Avana's receiver) and no reinvestment controller, enforced by the drift check (`Q-309`). Amends `Q-068`, `Q-224`. | On a standard Spoke `liquidationCall` is public; the Hub's transfer to any other liquidator reverts, giving LPVaultSwap exclusive settlement; per-market proxies must not move collateral between isolated accounts. | Resolves AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-RT-03 (token side) |
| `Q-303` | BackingRecord packing | Six slots, with charges below `2^96`. **Superseded by `Q-369`:** the record packs into five slots with `uint64` USD charges, which the compiled `BackingRecord` uses. | About 88,400 gas saved per pledge against 10 slots. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-15 |
| `Q-304` | One market per proxy in the ledger | Collateralization binds each proxy to one vault token at its first pledge (`proxyMarket`) and refuses a record of another market (`ProxyMarketMismatch`). | Account isolation (one PositionProxy per wallet × Spoke × market) is enforced in the immutable ledger, not only in the upgradeable Adapter. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-07 |
| `Q-369` | Position identity, record layout and the record limit | A position is `(uint32 integrationId, bytes32 positionKey)` (`types/PositionRef.sol`); no interface takes a `tokenId`. Collateralization keeps record IDs for events and indexers and maps `recordIdOf(integrationId, positionKey)`; every call after `pledge` names the record by `positionKey` within the market's integration (`vaultToken.integrationId()`). `BackingRecord` packs into 5 slots: (positionKey) (depositor, integrationId, marketId, inList) (proxy, pledgedAt uint40, lastDebitedAt uint40) (credited, originalUnits) (rescueUnits, originalUsdCharge uint64, currentUsdCharge uint64). The seizure list is a separate array of at most `MAX_RECORDS_PER_ACCOUNT_MARKET` = 16 keys per (proxy, market); `pledge` reverts `TooManyRecords` at 16 and a re-credit does not count again. Exposure counters for tokens and depositors are keyed by Hub in the one ledger per chain. Supersedes `Q-303`. | `bytes32` keys fit NFT positions, fungible LP balances and Uniswap v4 alike. USD charges below 2^64 at 8 decimals bound a record at 184 billion USD, above every cap, which lets the record fit 5 slots (about 110k gas saved per pledge). 16 records bound a seizure at about 1.31M + 16 × 0.24M = 5.15M gas, under 31% of the 16.78M per-transaction cap. Hub-keyed counters stop caps multiplying when Spokes are added. | `Q-303`. Resolves AMMN-GAS-15, AMMN-GAS-01, AMMN-RT-05 (ledger side), AMMN-ARCH-06 (position keys) |

## PositionProxy and policy

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-018` | Minimum health factor after borrowing | Enforced on-chain after every borrow and every withdrawal that leaves debt: the account's health factor on that Spoke must be at least `minOpeningHealthFactor` (AvanaConfig, WAD, launch value 1.10, bounds 1.03–1.25), checked by the policy the PositionProxy consults; the proxy separately enforces its immutable floor (`Q-126`). Not applied to repayment, fee collection, liquidation or debt-free withdrawal. **Amended by `Q-229`:** the RiskModule checks `minOpeningHealthFactor`, the 1.05 floor and the caller's minimum, and the Adapter calls it after every borrow and withdraw-with-debt; the proxy enforces nothing. **Superseded by `Q-398`:** Avana enforces no opening health factor on-chain; a borrow or a withdrawal that leaves debt needs Aave's health factor ≥ 1.00 and the caller's `minHealthFactor`, the Lens recommends 1.10, and an optional per-market `hardHealthFloor` is off by default. | Direct contract callers skip any UI check. A buffer above 1.0 stops a borrower creating a position that the next small price move makes liquidatable. | — |
| `Q-019` | Proxy scope | Superseded by `Q-225`. Was: one PositionProxy per wallet for every Borrow Spoke, an immutable clone at a CREATE2 address salted by the wallet. | — | — |
| `Q-054` | RiskModule checks | On-chain at pledge: pool approved, full range, canonical Uniswap v3 position manager; market not frozen and pledges not paused (top-ups of indebted accounts bypass a pledge pause, `Q-164`); oracle state Live for a new account, Live or Drifting for a top-up (`Q-132`); caps (`Q-055`) and Avana's maximum share of pool liquidity (`poolShareMaxBps`, 10%; amended by `Q-403` to 10,000 bps, off by default, with an optional per-pool cap); Aave configuration drift and Hub shares == assets for new-account pledges only. App-only, shown with reasons: wallet age and reputation, pool history and depth, position-size suggestion, projected liquidation price. No allowlist (`Q-078`). **Amended by `Q-227`:** the pledge gate reads the application pause state and the Aave reserve flags instead of Guardian freezes and pledge pauses. **Amended by `Q-230`:** `checkPledge` also enforces `minPledgeUsd`. **Amended by `Q-252`:** rescue top-ups are exempt from the pool-share cap. **Amended by `Q-293`, `Q-313`, `Q-376`:** the pledge gate checks the AMM integration's exact-shape match and the pinned position manager and factory of the market's integration instead of full range and the Uniswap v3 position manager. | On-chain checks enforce protocol limits against direct callers. The proxy has no execute, ERC-1271 or position-manager approval functions. Borrowed GHO goes Hub → proxy → the receiver the borrower signed. **Amended by `Q-223`:** the proxy calls the Spoke only when the Adapter calls it; borrowers call the Adapter, never their proxy, and the LPVault never calls a proxy. **Amended by `Q-306`:** the proxy has one state-changing function, `execute(target, data)`, callable only by the chain's AaveAdapter, which holds all account logic. | With no generic execute or approvals, nothing but the defined borrower operations can move the account, which also closes any path around the health-factor checks. | — |

Borrows also carry the per-proxy nonce (`Q-106`). **Amended by `Q-223`:** the Adapter resolves the proxy from `msg.sender` and the Borrow Spoke (or the named borrower for repay) and enforces the bounds; borrow names its debt reserve. | Each bound blocks one misuse of a signed transaction: execution long after signing, spending more than intended, ending at a worse health factor, or paying an address the borrower did not sign. | — |

| `Q-093` | Proxy authorization | Superseded by `Q-223`. Was: borrower → own proxy → Spoke, with the proxy consulting the Adapter and RiskModule for a yes or no on risk-increasing actions. | — | — |

Top-ups follow `Q-132`. **Amended by `Q-234`:** a stale debt-asset price also stops every seizure in the account. **Amended by `Q-247`:** liquidation checks staleness only for the seized collateral market and the repaid debt reserve; other reserves count conservatively in Aave's health factor. **Amended by `Q-280`:** each account holds one collateral market, so no seizure in one market depends on another market's state. | Freezing one market must not stop liquidation of another market on the same account; a stale price anywhere in the account makes the health factor itself unreliable, so it stops every seizure in that account. | — |

It uses the units actually removed; the pledge and aggregate counters update in the same transaction; the same remaining units give the same charge however exits are split; the last exit clears exactly. | A fixed basis stops a borrower releasing more cap than was charged by splitting exits or exiting at a different price; rounding up keeps the counter from falling below the true exposure. | `Q-109` |

Every pledge and top-up is booked for cap purposes at a price of at least the stress floor (health still uses the oracle price); the fixed-basis remainder (`Q-174`) keeps the bound through partial exits. The rescue budget is reserved on-chain: regular pledges use only their own budget; the slice is available only to eligible top-ups. Sizing allows for any bounded difference between Hub supply accounting and recorded backing. Property: a pledge or top-up within its Avana budget always fits under Aave's `addCap` (Aave pause, freeze and halt still apply). | If Aave's `addCap` were reached before Avana's USD cap, a pledge or rescue top-up approved by Avana would fail inside Aave; booking at the floor price keeps token counts within `addCap` at any oracle price. | — |

| `Q-225` | Beacon PositionProxies per wallet and Borrow Spoke | Each wallet has one PositionProxy per Borrow Spoke: an OpenZeppelin `BeaconProxy` whose beacon is the ProxyManager. Only the Adapter can create a proxy (`OnlyAdapter()`), on the wallet's first pledge to that Spoke, with CREATE2 and salt `keccak256(abi.encode(wallet, spoke))`, so `predictProxy(wallet, spoke)` gives the address before creation; the creating call initializes the proxy with its wallet and Spoke and emits `ProxyDeployed(wallet, spoke, proxy)`, and the Adapter records the binding and emits `UserProxyCreated(wallet, spoke, proxy)`. The binding never changes and no function rebinds a proxy. `setProxyImplementation(newImplementation)` on the ProxyManager (`APPLICATION_ADMIN`, normal delay) replaces the code of every proxy at once; there is no per-proxy upgrade or opt-out. The proxy has no generic execute, multicall, ERC-1271, position-manager or liquidation function. Supersedes `Q-019` and `Q-065`; amends `Q-107` and `Q-144`. **Amended by `Q-266`:** proxy implementations carry no reinitializer. **Superseded by `Q-306`:** each PositionProxy is a logic-free immutable contract created by CREATE2 per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market) by an immutable ProxyManager, with no beacon or upgrade, and all account logic lives in the timelocked Adapter. | Aave V4 tracks health per account per Spoke, so a proxy per Spoke makes each Borrow Spoke a separate credit account, as later tiers need. A beacon lets a proxy bug be fixed for every account in one reviewed upgrade instead of migrating positions, at the cost that the same upgrade could change every proxy; that power sits behind the same timelock and cancellers as the Adapter (`Q-223`). CREATE2 keeps the address known before the first pledge, which the app and the indexer use to show an account before it exists. | `Q-019`, `Q-065` |
| `Q-229` | Borrow-side checks in the RiskModule | The RiskModule holds Avana's borrow-side checks and the Adapter calls them on every borrow and every withdraw-with-debt, reverting with the RiskModule's error on any failure. Before the Spoke call (`preBorrowCheck`, `preWithdrawCheck`): poke every collateral market's LPOracle; every collateral market Live; every collateral and debt price used for the account within `borrowPriceMaxAge`; no vault-token reserve of the account frozen on Aave (borrow only); no paused vault-token or debt reserve of the account; Hub shares == assets; no Aave configuration drift; no liquidation halt for any market of the account; the pause state (`Q-227`). After the Spoke call (`postHealthCheck`): the account's health factor on that Spoke is at least the largest of `minOpeningHealthFactor` (1.10, ParameterStore), `proxyHealthFactorFloor` (1.05, a constant of the RiskModule implementation, changed only by an upgrade) and the caller's `minHealthFactor`. The Adapter itself keeps the call bounds: the deadline, the per-proxy borrow nonce, the receiver rules and the resolution of the proxy from the caller. Supersedes `Q-126`; amends `Q-018`, `Q-106`, `Q-161`, `Q-182`, `Q-203`. **Amended by `Q-241`, `Q-253`:** the checks include the circuit breakers, and the health check excludes rescue units. **Amended by `Q-311`, `Q-323`, `Q-324`:** `preBorrowCheck` also enforces the chain's `minDebtUsd`, the second-source agreement and the sequencer gate. **Amended by `Q-398`:** the RiskModule enforces no opening health factor floor; the optional per-market `hardHealthFloor` applies only where set. | LP collateral is priced by Avana's own oracle and can move between feed updates, so a borrow opened at health factor 1.0 could become liquidatable on the next update; the 1.10 buffer, the 1.05 floor and the price-state and age gates stay the protection against that. Keeping them in the RiskModule puts every risk rule in one contract and leaves the Adapter as the small piece that moves funds (`Q-240`); the floor is no longer immutable and changes only by a RiskModule upgrade at the normal delay. | `Q-126` |
| `Q-230` | Minimum pledge value | Each market has `minPledgeUsd` (USD, 8 decimals) in the ParameterStore. `RiskModule.checkPledge` reverts `BelowMinPledge(pledgeUsd, minPledgeUsd)` when the pledge's value, credited liquidity times the served LPOracle price per unit rounded down, is below it, for new pledges and top-ups alike. It is set in each market's listing operation with the caps (`Q-197`); launch value $10 (`10e8`); bounds 0 to $100,000; raising it is a short-delay change, lowering it a normal-delay change; exits never read it. Avana still sets no limit on NFTs or records per account and market (`Q-194`) and no minimum debt (`Q-169`). Amends `Q-054`, `Q-055`, `Q-169` (minimum position value only), `Q-197`. **Amended by `Q-252`; reaffirmed by `Q-277`:** rescue top-ups need `min(minPledgeUsd, $10)`. **Amended by `Q-287`, `Q-294`:** a per-chain minimum debt now applies to borrows, and an account holds at most 16 open records per market. | Every record costs liquidation gas when it is seized and every pledge adds a record; a floor on each pledge's value stops an account being built from many dust NFTs that cost more to liquidate than they are worth, without capping how many positions a real LP can bring. Debt keeps no minimum because partial repays and liquidations create small debts anyway, and Aave's dust rule and keeper reimbursement handle them. | — |
| `Q-234` | Accounts with several reserves | An account is one PositionProxy on one Borrow Spoke (`Q-225`); it can hold several vault-token collateral reserves and several debt reserves under one Aave health factor, bounded by the Spoke's per-account reserve limit, which counts collateral and debt reserves (`Q-193`). Borrowing and withdraw-with-debt check every collateral market and every debt reserve of the account (`Q-081`). A stale or failing price for any of the account's reserves blocks liquidation of the whole account (`Q-128`), and a rise in a debt asset's price lowers the health factor and can make the account liquidatable without any collateral move. Debt-free means zero debt on every debt reserve of the account; debt-free withdrawal and closing an account require it, and `repayAllAndWithdraw` clears each debt reserve it names and requires all of the account's debt reserves to be zero. Amends `Q-029`, `Q-081`, `Q-121`, `Q-128`, `Q-180`, `Q-192`, `Q-193`. **Amended by `Q-247`:** a stale price in one reserve no longer stops seizure of the account's other reserves. **Superseded by `Q-280`:** each PositionProxy holds one collateral market plus its debt reserves, so every market has its own health factor and nothing is cross-collateralized. | One health factor across reserves lets collateral in one market support debt in any approved asset, as borrowers expect from an Aave account. The cost is shared risk, stated so the app shows it and the caps include it: one bad feed stops every seizure on the account, and a debt-asset rally can push stable-LP accounts below 1 together, which the per-asset cap sizing covers. | — |
| `Q-260` | Exits read no registry state | Repay and withdrawal read nothing from the MarketRegistry. The Adapter decides debt-free from the Spoke: `spoke.getUserAccountData(proxy).borrowCount == 0`. `repay` accepts any debt reserve the Spoke accepts: the Spoke lends only its approved set, so an unknown reserve has no debt and the call returns 0. A withdrawal resolves the collateral reserve from the vault token's immutables and the Hub and Spoke (`HUB.getAssetId(vaultToken)`, `spoke.getReserveId(hub, assetId)`) and checks the token against the VaultTokenFactory's pool map. The pause state, circuit breakers, the address freeze and the approved debt-reserve set still gate new activity. Amends `Q-212`, `Q-223`, `Q-234`. **Amended by `Q-375`, `Q-299`:** the Adapter stores the proxy's binding (Spoke, market, vault token, reserve) at creation, and the factory's registry is keyed by (CDP Spoke, market key). | The MarketRegistry is upgradeable and trusted only to gate new activity, never to hold funds. If exits read it, a faulty registry could block every repay and withdrawal, or, by listing no debt reserves, make an indebted account look debt-free and skip the health floor on withdrawal. Reading Aave's own borrow count and the immutable bindings makes the exits and the debt-free decision depend only on Aave and on contracts no upgrade can change. | — |
| `Q-266` | No reinitializer in proxy implementations | A PositionProxy implementation contains no initializer other than the first and no `reinitializer`. Storage that a new implementation adds is initialized lazily inside an Adapter-only function. A ProxyManager upgrade keeps the BeaconProxy creation code and the `initialize` selector. Amends `Q-225`. **Superseded by `Q-306`:** PositionProxies have no implementation, initializer or upgrade, so no reinitializer rule applies. | A UUPS upgrade runs its reinitializer atomically in the upgrade call, but a beacon change replaces the code of every proxy with no per-proxy call, so a reinitializer in a proxy implementation would be callable by anyone first on every proxy. Keeping the creation code fixed also keeps every predicted proxy address equal to the deployed one. | — |
| `Q-276` | One account per wallet per Borrow Spoke | Each wallet keeps one PositionProxy per Borrow Spoke (`Q-225`); several isolated accounts per wallet are not added. Borrowers who want isolated positions use separate wallets. **Superseded by `Q-280`:** each wallet has one PositionProxy per (CDP Spoke, market), so two markets on one Spoke are two isolated accounts. | Several accounts per wallet would multiply proxies, records and gate checks for every action. With liquidation gated only by the seized and repaid reserves (`Q-247`), a failing market no longer shields the rest of an account; what remains of sharing one account is that a failing market blocks new borrowing for the whole account, which is the fail-closed outcome for new debt. | — |
| `Q-306` | PositionProxy is a logic-free immutable account | Each PositionProxy is a plain contract created by CREATE2 with constructor immutables `ADAPTER`, `WALLET`, `SPOKE` and `MARKET_ID`, and one state-changing function, `execute(target, data)`, which only the chain's AaveAdapter can call. It delegates to no other contract and has no implementation slot, initializer, upgrade or per-proxy version. All account logic lives in the Adapter: approvals, supply, enable-and-assert, borrow forwarding, repay with refund, withdrawal, balance-delta checks and cleanup. The Adapter upgrades through the chain's timelock. The ProxyManager is immutable too, with no roles. At the pinned Aave V4 commit, `Spoke.withdraw` sends to its caller, so withdrawn vault tokens go from the Hub to the proxy, and the Adapter burns them there; there is no Hub-to-Adapter transfer. Supersedes `Q-225`, `Q-266`; amends `Q-064`, `Q-107`, `Q-226`. | Aave V4 has no position transfer, so moving a position means a repay, a withdrawal and a re-borrow; the re-borrow is new debt, refused in exactly the states that would force a move. Pinned proxy versions would also force every Adapter version to drive every proxy version forever. A proxy with no logic has no version to fall behind, and a fix reaches every account in one timelocked Adapter upgrade. A generic `execute` adds no risk, because the Adapter was already trusted with custody of every account. | `Q-225`, `Q-266`. Resolves AMMN-SM-03, AMMN-RT-04, AMMN-RT-16, AMMN-FLOW-04, AMMN-ARCH-09 |
| `Q-307` | One proxy per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market); one ProxyManager per chain | The CREATE2 salt `keccak256(abi.encode(wallet, spoke, marketId))` and the constructor arguments both carry `(wallet, spoke, marketId)`. The Adapter keys bindings as `proxyOf[wallet][marketId]` and keeps `walletOf[proxy]` and `accountOf[proxy]` (Spoke, market ID, vault token, reserve ID); the ProxyManager keeps no map. Each proxy holds one collateral reserve (its market's) plus its debt reserves. Only the chain's single Adapter can deploy, and only for `msg.sender` on a market listed on that Spoke. | Each market gets an isolated health factor, so a stale feed or depeg in one market cannot liquidate a healthy position in another. Keeping the binding in the salt and having a single deployer means one Hub's listing cannot occupy another Hub's proxy address. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-18, AMMN-RT-10, AMMN-RT-04(c) and the contagion finding of the topology simulation |
| `Q-310` | Caps for n tokens keyed by Hub; debt-free charge moves to `Idle` | Token and depositor caps are read per Hub (`tokenUsdCap(hub, token)`, `depositorUsdCap(hub)`). Counters are kept in the one ledger per chain, and the token test runs for each of the market's n tokens. `SPEC-CF-001` uses `min_i CF_i`; for tokens without a Core Hub collateral factor an explicit value is set at listing. The pool-share cap uses the integration's time-weighted depth. The pool-share breaker uses `Π q_i ≤ 10^{8n}`. A dormant debt-free account's charge moves from `Regular` to `Idle` and back at its first borrow (`Q-354`). Amends `Q-055`, `Q-020`. **Amended by `Q-396`:** there is no `Idle` bucket; a debt-free account's charge stays in `Regular`. | Per-Spoke counters would multiply the caps by the number of Spokes. Charging at pledge alone lets debt-free LPs block borrowers. | Resolves AMMN-RT-05, AMMN-FLOW-16 |
| `Q-349` | Exits never depend on PositionProxy code | The PositionProxy is a logic-free immutable account created by CREATE2 per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market) (`Q-306`, `Q-307`), executing only calls from the chain's AaveAdapter. No flow contains an implementation pointer, an implementation pin, a version check or a proxy move. Every exit (repay, repay-all-and-withdraw, withdraw, partial removal, repay with collateral, liquidation) is driven by Adapter or LPVaultSwap logic, which upgrades through the chain's timelock; a bug in exit logic is fixed by an Adapter upgrade that every existing proxy uses at once. Every repay, a third party's included, runs through `AaveAdapter.repay` and the proxy's `execute`: Aave V4's `Spoke.repay` is `onlyPositionManager(onBehalfOf)` and proxies approve no position manager (checked by the drift check, `Q-352`), so no one can repay on a proxy's behalf directly. The binding `(wallet, spoke, marketId) → proxy` never changes and no record, seizure list, rescue counter or nonce is ever re-keyed. Amends `Q-212`. | Aave V4 has no position transfer, so moving a position means repaying and re-borrowing, which is new debt refused in exactly the states (pause, freeze, breaker, full `drawCap`) in which a proxy bug would force it. Holding no logic in the proxy removes the need to move; the only code a proxy runs is "forward the Adapter's call", which has no version to fall behind. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-04, AMMN-SM-03, AMMN-RT-04 (flow level) |
| `Q-383` | Pool-share cap per pool | The pool-share cap is per pool, summed over every market (every range) of that pool on the chain: `(poolUnits[poolKey] + L) × 10^4 ≤ poolShareMaxBps(poolKey) × depth(poolKey)`. Collateralization holds one `poolUnits` counter per pool, across every Hub, Spoke and integration, updated at every pledge, re-credit, exit and seizure. `poolShareMaxBps` is stored per pool; a setter that names a market writes the value of the market's pool, so every market of one pool reads one value. For concentrated liquidity every range of a pool counts liquidity in the same unit and the depth is the pool's time-weighted active liquidity, so the sum is exact; out-of-range markets count too, which only makes the test stricter. A full-pool AMM protocol has one shape per pool, so its sum has one term. **Amended by `Q-403`:** the chain default `poolShareMaxBps` is 10,000 bps (no limit); the per-pool sum is tested against a per-pool cap only where a pool's listing sets one. | With a per-market cap, k ranges of one pool let Avana hold k × `poolShareMaxBps` of the pool's active liquidity, and unwinding seized collateral could then move the pool price far more than the cap intends. | Amends `Q-054`, `Q-361`. Resolves AMMN-RT-05 |
| `Q-384` | Idle charge decided by utilisation | An account's charge may move from `Regular` to `Idle` once its latest pledge or re-credit is at least `idleBookingSeconds` old and its utilisation is below `IDLE_UTILIZATION_BPS` (RiskModule constant, launch 1,000): `debtValue × 10^4 < IDLE_UTILIZATION_BPS × borrowCapacity`, both from the Spoke's account data (2.7 `SPEC-CAP-009`), whatever the debt. Every borrow by an account whose charge is in `Idle` moves the charge back to `Regular` and is refused with `CapExceeded(MARKET)` without room. `releaseIdle` no longer requires zero debt. **Superseded by `Q-396`:** the idle-collateral mechanism is removed. | Deciding idle by zero debt let one token borrow keep a whole charge in `Regular`, so sybil wallets could hold `marketUsdCap` for the interest on token debts. Utilisation measures whether the collateral backs real debt: a $900,000 charge with $50 of debt is idle, and a squatter who borrows again is moved back to `Regular` only if room exists, then is releasable again at once. | Amends `Q-354`. Resolves AMMN-FLOW-16 |
| `Q-354` | Debt-free pledges cannot fill the borrowers' cap budget | A market's USD cap budget has three parts: `Regular` (accounts with debt, and debt-free accounts for `idleBookingSeconds` after their latest pledge), `Slice` (rescue top-ups) and `Idle` (debt-free accounts older than that). Anyone may call the AaveAdapter's `releaseIdle(proxy)` once an account has had no debt for `idleBookingSeconds` (launch 7 days) since its latest pledge; Collateralization moves the account's booked charge from `Regular` to `Idle` in O(1) using the per-account charge. `Idle` is bounded by `idleUsdCap[m]` (launch = `marketUsdCap[m]`). An account's first borrow moves its charge back from `Idle` to `Regular` and is refused with `CapExceeded(Regular)` if `Regular` has no room. The token caps and `depositorUsdCap` count all three parts, and the pool-share cap counts the credited liquidity of all accounts. The Hub `addCap` is sized above `Regular + Slice + Idle` at the stress floor. **Superseded by `Q-396`:** the idle-collateral mechanism is removed; cap squatting by a non-borrowing LP (AMMN-FLOW-16) is an accepted risk. | Caps are charged at pledge, so a debt-free whale or sybil set could pledge, keep its fees and block borrowers. Moving dormant debt-free charge out of `Regular` returns that room to borrowers; a new borrower still pledges into `Regular` and has `idleBookingSeconds` to borrow. Repeated withdraw-and-re-pledge to stay in `Regular` costs two transactions per week per position. **Amended by `Q-384`:** idle is decided by utilisation, not by zero debt. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-16 |
| `Q-375` | Immutable, logic-free PositionProxy per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market) | A PositionProxy is a plain immutable contract (no delegation, implementation slot, initializer or upgrade) created by CREATE2 by an immutable ProxyManager with salt `keccak256(abi.encode(wallet, spoke, marketId))` and constructor immutables `ADAPTER, WALLET, SPOKE, MARKET_ID`. Its only state-changing function is `execute(target, data)`, callable only by the chain's AaveAdapter. All account logic (supply, enable collateral, borrow, repay, withdraw, balance-delta checks, approvals) lives in the Adapter, which upgrades through the timelock. The Adapter stores the binding (Spoke, market, vault token, reserve) at proxy creation, so repay, debt-free exits and `burnSeized` read no MarketRegistry state. Same decision as `Q-306`, stated for the interfaces. | With pinned or versioned proxies, an Adapter that drops an old proxy version strands paused borrowers, and letting a wallet re-point its proxy creates a second proxy per (wallet, Spoke) that can dodge a liquidation. A logic-free proxy has nothing to re-point, so every exit runs against the same proxy forever. One proxy per market gives each market its own Aave account and health factor. The salt and constructor arguments bind the proxy to its (wallet, Spoke, market), and only the Adapter deploys, so no Hub or Spoke can occupy another's proxy address. | Resolves AMMN-SM-03, AMMN-RT-04, AMMN-RT-16, AMMN-FLOW-04, AMMN-ARCH-09, AMMN-RT-10, AMMN-FLOW-18 |
| `Q-376` | Adapter, RiskModule and registry entry points keyed by market and `PositionRef` | Every borrower entry point names `marketId` and a `PositionRef {uint32 integrationId; bytes32 positionKey}`; the Adapter checks `integrationId` against the market's (`IntegrationMismatch`). Every token amount is an array in the market's token order: `removeLiquidity`, `repayWithCollateral` and `deleverageRelease` take `minAmounts[]` and return `amounts[]`; the seizure unwind of a liquidation keeps zero per-token minimums, with oracle-derived minimums on every swap (`Q-131`, `Q-250`). `checkPledge` requires the AMM integration's exact-shape match (`ShapeMismatch`) and counts caps with per-Hub n-token counters. New Adapter outer entry points `releaseZeroCredit` and `collectFees` (the latter returns zero amounts for AMM protocols with fees rolled into the position). Repay with collateral stays open under `Paused(General)`, refused only under `Paused(MintPathSuspect)`, because it only reduces debt (`Q-356`). `expectedCollateralFactorBps = floor(min_i CF_i × poolRiskFactor / 1e4)` over n tokens. The market key is that of `Q-292`. Amends `Q-020`, `Q-054`, `Q-254`. | `tokenId` and `amount0`/`amount1` lock the core to Uniswap v3. A narrow range priced at the market's per-unit value is the ×979 overvaluation of AMMN-RT-01. The weakest token bounds the factor because an unwind can return the whole position in it. | Resolves AMMN-RT-01, AMMN-FLOW-24, AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-FLOW-26, AMMN-RT-05 |
| `Q-396` | No idle-collateral mechanism | Removed: `releaseIdle`, `admitIdleCharge`, `markDebtFree` where it served only the idle bucket, `idleUsdCap`, `idleBookingSeconds`, `IDLE_UTILIZATION_BPS` and the `Idle` cap bucket. A market's cap budget has two parts, `Regular` and `Slice` (rescue top-ups); a debt-free account's charge stays in `Regular` until its units are withdrawn or seized. Cap squatting by a non-borrowing LP (AMMN-FLOW-16) is an accepted risk, handled by caps set at listing and by monitoring of each market's cap use. Supersedes `Q-354` and `Q-384`; amends `Q-310`. | The idle bucket added state, a permissionless mover, a utilisation test and a third cap budget to answer a griefing case whose cost to the attacker is the capital it locks in the pool and earns nothing on. Caps set at listing bound the case, and monitoring sees a market's cap filling with debt-free charge long before it matters, with less code to audit. | AMMN-FLOW-16 |
| `Q-398` | Health floors leave the contracts | `minOpeningHealthFactor` and `PROXY_HEALTH_FACTOR_FLOOR` are removed as on-chain requirements. A borrow or a withdrawal that leaves debt needs Aave's health factor ≥ 1.00 and the caller's `minHealthFactor`. The Lens previews a recommended borrow at `RECOMMENDED_HEALTH_FACTOR` (1.10) next to the protocol maximum at Aave's health factor 1.00: $100,000 of collateral at a 70 % collateral factor gives `⌊70,000 / 1.10⌋ = $63,636` recommended and $70,000 maximum. An optional per-market `hardHealthFloor` exists, 0 (off) by default, set only if stress tests justify it. Rescue top-ups are open to an indebted account below `rescueHealthFactor` (1.10). `graceHealthFactor` (0.95, oracle-recovery grace) stays. Supersedes `Q-018`; amends `Q-072`, `Q-196`, `Q-229`, `Q-246`, `Q-252`, `Q-268`. | Aave's health factor 1.00 is the solvency boundary the Spoke enforces and the liquidation bonus is sized for. A floor above it limits what a borrower may take without bounding Avana's loss, which the caps, the bonus and the First-Loss Reserve bound. Showing the recommended amount keeps the guidance in front of the borrower, and the per-market floor stays available for a market whose stress tests show a need. | — |

## Oracle and price states

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-020` | LP collateral factor | Computed off-chain and set on the Spoke reserve by Aave governance: `LP CF = min(CF0, CF1) × poolRiskFactor`, where CF0 and CF1 are the tokens' Core Hub collateral factors recorded at listing (a GHO leg uses its explicit listing value, `Q-188`) and `poolRiskFactor` ≤ 0.40 (`Q-186`). **Amended by `Q-310`, `Q-376`:** the factor is `min_i CF_i × poolRiskFactor` over the market's n tokens, with an explicit listing value for a token without a Core Hub collateral factor (`Q-317`). | Aave V4 collateral factors are Spoke reserve settings owned by the DAO; computing them off-chain avoids an on-chain push path that could change a CF without governance. | — |
| `Q-028` | No oracle haircut | LPOracle reports the unhaircut value of one vault token, `q0 × P0 + q1 × P1`. All risk adjustment is applied once, in the Spoke collateral factor; unwind slippage is handled in settlement (`Q-085`), not in the price; the RiskModule applies no second haircut. **Amended by `Q-321`:** LPOracle reports the fair composition `Σ amountᵢ × priceᵢ` of one vault token at the oracle price vector over its n tokens. | Haircuts in two places compound and are hard to calibrate; one factor gives the risk reviewer one number to reason about. | — |
| `Q-036` | Oracle failure response | Superseded by `Q-079` and `Q-130`. Was: a pre-approved backup feed takes over automatically and liquidation is blocked on-chain while Expired. | — | — |
| `Q-056` | Price feed behaviour | Each market's LPOracle never returns 0 and never reverts on a price read; the only revert is `InsufficientGas` when gas left after a caught failure is below `pokeMinGas`. Any caught failure that passes the gas check moves the market to a stricter state. Caching a price happens in a separate `poke()` call. Combined feeds report the oldest input timestamp. Feed redundancy is `Q-079` and `Q-130`; the liquidation floor is `Q-085`, with no spot-price check. | Aave reads the price inside every account operation, so a reverting or zero price would block repay and liquidation for everyone. The gas check stops a caller starving an inner call to force a failure state; the oldest timestamp stops a fresh leg hiding a stale one. | — |
| `Q-057` | GHO inside an LP | Superseded by `Q-177`. Was: GHO priced from Chainlink GHO/USD, capped at $1. | — | — |
| `Q-079` | Oracle redundancy at launch | Chainlink-only pricing with the pool TWAP as a guard. Each token has a primary Chainlink feed and an alternate Chainlink route; every leg passes its own freshness and validity checks; shared feed or provider dependencies count as a single point of failure (`Q-176`). If both routes fail, the last accepted price is used only within `cachedWindow` (1 hour, `Q-183`); the effect of each price state is `Q-128`. Launch caps are sized for the bad debt that can build while liquidations are paused (`Q-192`). An independent provider can later be added to veto a cached price; keeping liquidations running through a Chainlink outage would need an independent source approved to supply fresh prices. **Amended by `Q-323`:** borrow and withdraw-with-debt also require an independent second source (another oracle network or a deep pool's TWAP) to agree with the Chainlink price. | Chainlink is the source Aave already relies on; an alternate route covers a single-feed failure; a bounded cache lets short outages pass without stopping repayment, and caps limit what a long outage can cost. | `Q-036` |
| `Q-086` | Liquidation in Drifting and Cached | Superseded by `Q-128`. Was: LPVaultSwap also required the account to be liquidatable at the TWAP-implied price. | — | — |
| `Q-088` | Recovery grace period | After feeds recover from Expired, for `graceDuration` (30 minutes, at most `graceDurationMax`, 2 hours) only accounts with health factor below `graceHealthFactor` (0.95, at least `graceHealthFactorMin`, 0.90) can be liquidated. Grace applies per account; latching rules are `Q-129` and `Q-154`. | After an outage many accounts may sit just below 1.0 on a price that has just jumped; a short, deeper threshold gives prices time to settle and borrowers time to add collateral, while badly unhealthy accounts are still liquidated. | — |
| `Q-112` | USD-level feed check | Each coin's primary Chainlink feed is compared with its alternate Chainlink route whenever both report (`Q-130`); disagreement beyond `feedAgreementToleranceBps` (`Q-184`) moves the market to Drifting and starts the disagreement pause (`Q-162`). | A primary feed that is fresh but wrong passes freshness checks; comparison with an independent route is the only on-chain signal of it. | — |
| `Q-113` | Listing margin | Listing requires (1 − CF) larger than the feed deviation threshold plus heartbeat drift (`heartbeatDriftBps`), a TWAP window and tolerance, and minimum pool liquidity backed by a manipulation-cost study. | A feed can lag the market by up to its deviation threshold plus drift within a heartbeat; the collateral margin must exceed that lag or accounts could be undercollateralized before the price shows it. | — |
| `Q-128` | Price feeds and liquidation by state | LPOracle always returns the last accepted positive price to Aave, even when Expired; it exposes freshness separately and keeps the original source timestamp; a valid price is seeded before listing. Borrowing and withdraw-with-debt are blocked when any relevant price is Cached or Expired; repay and debt-free withdrawal stay available, with zero debt read from the Spoke's debt balances. Drifting: if Chainlink inputs are fresh and valid, Aave decides eligibility (HF < 1) and sizes the seizure at served prices; TWAP disagreement still blocks new borrowing. Cached or Expired: LPVaultSwap refuses liquidation until a fresh, valid price returns. LPVaultSwap checks every collateral and debt price that affects the account's health on that Spoke; if any is stale, no market in that account can be seized. **Amended by `Q-238`:** debt prices are the Aave oracle sources approved per debt reserve, checked against the same age limits. **Amended by `Q-247`:** an Expired market's LPOracle serves 1 to Aave until it recovers and a disagreeing market serves the lower route; a stale reserve blocks only its own seizure or repayment, not the account's other reserves. | A seizure priced on a stale or cached value can take too much or too little collateral. Serving the last price keeps Aave's own repay and withdraw paths working while Avana blocks the actions that depend on a current price. | `Q-086` |
| `Q-129` | Grace latch | `poke()` and any Avana call that observes Expired (from source timestamps, `Q-154`) store a flag; the first fresh update after it stores `recoveredAt`; LPVaultSwap applies `graceHealthFactor` until `recoveredAt + graceDuration`. **Amended by `Q-264`, `Q-272`:** without recovery evidence the window starts at the newest input of the fresh price; phase changes are read through the previous phase. | Storing the transition on-chain starts the grace window at the actual recovery, not whenever a keeper first calls. | — |
| `Q-130` | Feed failover | Failover is automatic inside each feed: the market stays Live while the alternate Chainlink route passes freshness; the primary-vs-alternate agreement check (`Q-112`) runs only when both report. No stored switch disables a primary; permanently replacing a feed is a new price source at `upgradeDelay` (`Q-091`). **Amended by `Q-248`:** each token also has a pre-approved standby route that a guardian can switch in at once; only the Aave DAO can reverse such a switch quickly. | Automatic failover keeps markets Live through a single-feed outage with no operator action; without a manual switch, no key can force a market onto a chosen feed. | `Q-036` |
| `Q-154` | Grace latch hardening | Expired is latched only from source timestamps on both routes, never from a caught error, a failed configuration read or a gas shortfall; `poke()` requires at least `pokeMinGas` (150,000, bounds 100,000–500,000); `recoveredAt` is the first fresh update; one grace window per outage. **Amended by `Q-264`, `Q-272`.** | If a caller could set the latch by forcing an error, it could create grace windows at will and delay liquidations; tying the latch to Chainlink timestamps makes it reflect real outages only. | — |
| `Q-162` | Feed disagreement | While a token's primary and alternate routes are both fresh but disagree, liquidation of every account using that price is paused as if Cached (per-account rule, `Q-128`) for at most `disagreementPauseMax` (1 hour) from the first stored observation (`Q-199`); borrowing stays blocked. If the disagreement persists, `Q-208` applies. **Amended by `Q-247`:** the pause applies to seizure of the disagreeing market only; during it the market counts at its lower route in Aave's health factor. | When two Chainlink routes disagree one of them is wrong, and a seizure could be mispriced; a bounded pause avoids that without letting a disagreement stop liquidation indefinitely. | — |
| `Q-176` | Common-mode price guard | Listing rule: a pool's two coins have backup routes that share no intermediate feed. A band on the LP's USD level per $1 of liquidity at peg (`usdBandLow` 0.90, `usdBandHigh` 1.02) moves the market to Drifting when breached; the effect of a breach is `Q-195`. **Amended by `Q-275`:** the upper bound is tested on capped prices. **Amended by `Q-329`:** the band applies to the n-token geometric mean and only to markets whose tokens are all USD stables. | Two stablecoins that depeg together keep their ratio, so the TWAP guard misses it; the USD band catches a shared move, and separate backup feeds stop one feed failure affecting both legs. | — |
| `Q-177` | Stablecoin $1 ceiling | Each approved USD-pegged stable leg (GHO, USDC, USDT, DAI, USDS and other listed stables) is valued at min(validated price, $1) in the LP valuation only, both to derive the price ratio for token amounts and to value those amounts. Freshness, feed-agreement and TWAP checks use uncapped quotes in the same direction, so the ceiling never creates a Drifting signal by itself. Below-peg prices pass through. Borrowed GHO uses the Borrow Spoke's separate debt price (`Q-100`). | A stablecoin briefly above $1 would otherwise let borrowers borrow against value that disappears when it returns to peg; capping only in valuation avoids that without making above-peg moves look like feed faults. | `Q-057` |
| `Q-182` | Oracle freshness for borrowing and liquidation | Markets are listed only if their feeds meet the required update cadence; a faster approved Chainlink route is used where available, and markets without one are deferred. Reading or caching the same Chainlink report never refreshes its timestamp. Borrow and withdraw-with-debt: the proxy checks the source timestamps of every collateral and debt price used for the account against `borrowPriceMaxAge` (1 hour). Liquidation: LPVaultSwap requires every price within `liquidationPriceMaxAge` (`feedHeartbeat` + 10 minutes), and an approved fallback must supply the price the Spoke actually uses for health and seizure sizing. If every approved route stalls, the cached price stays readable, LPVaultSwap pauses liquidation, and repayment and debt-free withdrawal stay available. **Amended by `Q-229`:** the RiskModule checks the source timestamps for borrow and withdraw-with-debt. **Amended by `Q-238`:** debt feeds are checked against the same limits. **Amended by `Q-248`, `Q-274`:** primary legs publish at most hourly, `borrowPriceMaxAge` is the heartbeat plus 10 minutes up to 70 minutes, a serving alternate is held to that age for liquidation, and non-GHO debt prices come from a DebtOracle with failover. **Amended by `Q-322`, `Q-323`:** every age limit is the feed's own heartbeat plus `heartbeatMargin` (1,800 s), with no hourly cadence rule, and a borrow also needs the second source to agree. | Borrowing on an old price lets borrowers draw against value that has already fallen; a separate, looser liquidation limit keeps liquidations running between heartbeats while still refusing prices from a stalled feed. | — |
| `Q-183` | Cached window | Cached is measured from the first failed read and lasts `cachedWindow` = 1 hour. The feed-disagreement pause (`Q-162`) has its own bound, `disagreementPauseMax`, at most 1 hour. | One hour covers ordinary feed delays while capping the time positions run on an unrefreshed price. | — |
| `Q-184` | Feed-agreement tolerance | `feedAgreementToleranceBps` ≥ primary deviation threshold + alternate deviation threshold + `feedAgreementMarginBps` (50 bps), enforced at listing as a hard lower bound. Test: `\|p − b\| × 10⁴ ≤ T × min(p, b)`. | Two healthy feeds can differ by up to both deviation thresholds; a tighter tolerance would flag disagreement in normal operation and pause liquidations for no reason. | — |
| `Q-191` | Oracle math guards | LP price = isqrt(4·a0·a1) − 1, so it never overvalues. Listing requires d0 + d1 even, normalised `minAnswer` ≥ 1, standard tick spacing, raw price < 2^64 under a `LISTING_SHOCK_FACTOR` (100×) shock, route answers at most `MAX_ROUTE_ANSWER` ($10,000) and full-range approximation error at most `MAX_FULL_RANGE_EPSILON` (10⁻¹²). Code clamps and treats a normalised 0 as a failure. **Amended by `Q-325`, `Q-321`:** the price is the fair composition at the oracle price clamped to the market's range, each amount floored and reduced by one raw unit; the even-decimal-sum rule and the full-range approximation bound are removed, and `d` is set by value. | Integer square roots and decimal scaling can overstate a value or overflow; these guards make every rounding step favour the protocol and rule out overflow at listing instead of during a liquidation. | — |
| `Q-195` | USD band breach | Leaving the USD band blocks new borrowing and withdraw-with-debt only; pledges stay allowed. Liquidation continues while both Chainlink routes are fresh and agree; feed disagreement still pauses it (`Q-162`). **Amended by `Q-275`.** **Amended by `Q-329`:** the band applies only to markets whose tokens are all USD stables. | A shared depeg is when liquidations matter most, so they keep running; blocking new borrowing stops borrowing against a depegged value. | — |
| `Q-199` | Disagreement pause start | `poke()` and every successful Avana call store the first time they observe a primary-vs-alternate disagreement; the liquidation pause ends `disagreementPauseMax` after that stored time. Keepers poke every heartbeat. | A stored start time means calling at different times cannot extend the pause, and keepers poking every heartbeat keep the start close to the real onset. | — |
| `Q-200` | GHO in the TWAP guard | The TWAP guard and agreement checks use the uncapped GHO/USD price; only valuation applies the $1 ceiling, so GHO markets stay Live above peg. | Using the capped price in the TWAP check would show a false pool-versus-oracle gap whenever GHO trades above $1. | — |
| `Q-208` | Persistent feed disagreement | If the primary and alternate routes still disagree after `disagreementPauseMax`, the market is treated as Expired for liquidation, blocked until the routes agree or governance approves a fix; repay and debt-free withdrawal stay available; borrowing stays blocked. **Amended by `Q-247`, `Q-248`:** only the disagreeing market's seizure waits; a guardian can switch to the standby route at once. | When a disagreement does not resolve, Avana cannot tell which price is right; seizing on either could take collateral wrongly, so the market waits for a fix as in any other outage. | — |
| `Q-238` | Debt-asset prices | Each debt reserve is priced by the Aave oracle source approved for it in the listing payload: GHO keeps Aave's fixed GHO price source (`Q-100`); every other debt asset uses its approved Chainlink feed through the AaveOracle. The MarketRegistry records each debt reserve's price source and the ParameterStore its feed definition; the drift check compares the Spoke oracle's source for the reserve with the recorded one. The RiskModule (borrow, withdraw-with-debt) requires every debt feed of the account within `borrowPriceMaxAge`, and LPVaultSwap (liquidation) within `liquidationPriceMaxAge`; a fixed source has no age. A stale debt feed blocks borrowing and liquidation for every account that holds that debt (`Q-234`). Amends `Q-100`, `Q-128`, `Q-182`. **Amended by `Q-247`, `Q-248`:** a non-GHO debt reserve's Aave source is its DebtOracle, which serves a stress price once Expired. | Aave values the debt for health and seizure sizing with this source, so Avana checks the same source rather than pricing debt itself; holding it to the same age limits as collateral feeds stops a borrow or a seizure on a stale debt price. | — |
| `Q-247` | Liquidation gated by the seized and repaid reserves | Liquidation requires fresh, agreeing prices only for the collateral market being seized and the debt reserve being repaid: the seized market is not Cached, Expired or in a disagreement pause, every input of it is within `liquidationPriceMaxAge`, and the repaid debt reserve's price is Live within its age limit (GHO's fixed source always passes). A stale or disagreeing price in another reserve of the account no longer blocks the call. In Aave's health factor and sizing those other reserves count conservatively, so a failing price can only make the account easier to liquidate: an Expired market's LPOracle serves `latestAnswer() = 1` (one unit of 10⁻⁸ USD per whole vault token, the smallest positive price, so the collateral counts as worthless) until it recovers; during a feed disagreement it serves the LP price built from the lower of each token's two route answers; a Cached market serves its last accepted price for at most `cachedWindow`. A non-GHO debt reserve's DebtOracle (`Q-248`) serves, once Expired, its last accepted price raised by `debtStressRiseBps` (the asset's stress rise of the cap-sizing rule) and, during a disagreement, the higher of its two route answers. A market or debt reserve whose own price is stale is never seized or repaid at these prices; it waits for a fresh price, the standby switch of `Q-248` or in-kind settlement. Borrowing and withdraw-with-debt keep the account-wide gate: every collateral and debt price of the account Live and within `borrowPriceMaxAge`. The grace window after a recovery stays per account. Avana's own valuations of the market (caps, minimum pledge value, previews) keep using the last accepted price. Amends `Q-121`, `Q-128`, `Q-162`, `Q-208`, `Q-234`, `Q-238`. | A stale price makes a health factor uncertain, and the account-wide gate resolved that uncertainty in the borrower's favour: one $10 pledge in a failing market, or a small debt in an asset with a slow feed, shielded every other market of the account from liquidation for as long as the failure lasted, and in a systemic failure it shielded every account holding the failing market. Seizing only reserves whose own prices are fresh, and counting collateral that cannot be priced as worthless and debt that cannot be priced at its stress price, resolves it in the lenders' favour: an account that is unhealthy on its fresh reserves alone is liquidated there, and the reserve that cannot be valued is never touched. The cost falls on a borrower who holds a failing market alongside debt, is bounded by the bonus on what is seized, and is avoidable by repaying or adding collateral in a fresh market. During a disagreement both routes are fresh, so the conservative one of the two is used instead of zero. | — |
| `Q-248` | Standby price sources for collateral and debt | Each listed token records, beside its primary and alternate Chainlink routes, a pre-approved standby route, set in the listing operation and changed only by `setPriceSource` at the normal delay; the standby passes every listing check of the routes it can replace (decimals, heartbeat, deviation threshold, no shared intermediate feed with the other route). Each non-GHO debt reserve is priced through a DebtOracle, one upgradeable contract per debt asset that is that reserve's Aave price source on the Borrow Spoke, with the collateral legs' model: primary and alternate routes with automatic failover, the agreement check, Cached and Expired states, `poke` and a standby route. GHO keeps Aave's fixed source. `useStandbyRoute(token, slot)` replaces the token's primary or alternate route with its standby at once; the Emergency Guardian and the Aave Protocol Guardian call it through `APPLICATION_PAUSE_GUARDIAN`, and `APPLICATION_ADMIN` can schedule it at the short delay, an operation that only `DAO_CANCELLER_ROLE` can cancel. The Aave governance executor can undo a guardian's switch at once (`cancelStandbySwitch`); otherwise switching back (`restorePriceRoute`) or to any new source waits the normal delay. A switch never bypasses freshness, validity or the agreement check. Keepers add every DebtOracle implementation slot to their code check. Amends `Q-091`, `Q-130`, `Q-182`, `Q-208`, `Q-219`, `Q-226`, `Q-238`. | When a feed fails or two routes disagree, the market's own seizures wait for a usable price, and with only a normal-delay fix that wait lasted at least five days, far past the 48-hour window caps are sized for. A standby route approved at listing under the same checks lets a guardian end the outage in minutes without choosing an unreviewed source, so the switch cannot point a market at a price of the guardian's choosing. A non-GHO debt asset had a single source and no failover, so one stale feed stopped borrowing and liquidation for every account holding that debt; the collateral legs' model removes that single point. Only the Aave DAO can reverse a switch quickly or cancel a scheduled one, so a compromised canceller cannot hold a market on a failed feed. | — |
| `Q-264` | Grace start without recovery evidence | When the grace latch is set and source timestamps give no recovery interval, `recoveredAt` is the time of the newest input of the fresh price, `max(t0, t1)`, never later than the current block. Amends `Q-129`, `Q-154`. | The grace window must start no earlier than the recovery. The older input of the fresh price can predate the recovery by up to a whole heartbeat of its leg, which ended the window before it began and let accounts between `graceHealthFactor` and 1 be liquidated at the first fresh price. | — |
| `Q-272` | Chainlink phase changes | When a leg's current round is the first of a new aggregator phase (`uint64(roundId) == 1`), the recovery-evidence lookup reads the last round of the previous phase through the proxy's `phaseAggregators(phaseId − 1)`; if that read is unusable, the leg's gap starts at the `updatedAt` of the last round the oracle itself recorded for the leg. LPOracle and DebtOracle legs follow the same rule. Amends `Q-129`, `Q-154`. | Chainlink proxy round IDs restart with each aggregator upgrade, so `roundId − 1` names no round on the first round of a phase and the lookup used to give no evidence; an outage that ended on an aggregator upgrade then never opened the grace window. The fallback uses a source timestamp the oracle stored, never a caught error, and can only lengthen the measured gap, which errs toward the bounded grace window. | — |
| `Q-274` | Feed cadence margins | Every leg of a primary route publishes a heartbeat of at most 3,600 seconds, recorded in the listing operation; a token without such a primary route is not listed. Each feed's `borrowPriceMaxAge` is `min(heartbeat + 600 s, 4,200 s)`, so a primary leg with a 3,600-second heartbeat accepts prices up to 4,200 seconds old for borrowing and an alternate with a 24-hour heartbeat still only 4,200 seconds. While a token is served by its alternate route because the primary is stale, liquidation also requires every alternate leg to be at most its `borrowPriceMaxAge` old; otherwise the token counts as Cached for liquidation. Amends `Q-182`, `Q-196`. **Superseded by `Q-322`:** each feed is fresh while its age is at most its own heartbeat plus a 1,800 s margin, feeds with heartbeats up to 86,400 s are listable, and the independent second source of `Q-323` bounds what a long heartbeat can hide. | A feed publishes a round only after its heartbeat has elapsed, plus block and network delay, so an age limit equal to the heartbeat refused valid borrows for a few minutes every hour; a ten-minute margin removes those gaps while keeping every borrow within about seventy minutes of a fresh price. Alternates often publish once a day: when both routes stalled, an alternate updated fifteen hours earlier still counted as fresh, so liquidation ran on a frozen price for hours before Cached began; holding the serving alternate to the borrow age makes that case Cached at once. | — |
| `Q-275` | USD band upper bound on capped prices | The USD band's upper bound `usdBandHigh` is tested on the capped prices `v0`, `v1`; the lower bound `usdBandLow` stays on the uncapped `q0`, `q1`. Amends `Q-176`, `Q-195`. **Amended by `Q-329`:** the upper bound is tested on the capped prices of all n tokens through the geometric mean. | A stablecoin above $1 is valued at the $1 ceiling, so an upward move cannot inflate collateral; testing the upper bound on uncapped prices blocked borrowing in GHO markets exactly when GHO traded at a premium, with no risk to control. Downward common-mode moves are what the band exists for, and they are still measured uncapped. | — |
| `Q-322` | Freshness from each feed's own heartbeat | `feedMaxAge = liquidationPriceMaxAge = heartbeat + heartbeatMargin` per leg (margin 1,800 s on Ethereum and Base); `borrowPriceMaxAge ≤ feedMaxAge`, equal by default and lowerable at the short delay. The `≤ 3,600 s` primary-heartbeat rule (`MAX_PRIMARY_HEARTBEAT`) and `borrowPriceMaxAge = min(heartbeat + 600, 4,200)` (with `BORROW_AGE_MARGIN`, `BORROW_AGE_CAP`) are removed. Heartbeats up to 86,400 s are listable. Supersedes `Q-274`; amends `Q-182`, `Q-196`. | Chainlink stablecoin feeds have 82,800–86,400 s heartbeats and update on a 0.25% deviation trigger; while a feed operates its answer is within its deviation threshold, so an age up to heartbeat plus publication margin is normal. The old rule made the launch markets unlistable and closed borrowing most of the day. A feed that stops while appearing fresh is caught by the agreement check (liquidation) and the second source (borrow, `Q-323`). | `Q-274`. Resolves AMMN-INT-01, AMMN-FLOW-03 (3) |
| `Q-385` | Standby second source | Each token may record a standby second source beside its active second source, set together by `setSecondSource(token, src, standby)` at the normal delay and checked the same way (code present, `0 < toleranceBps ≤ 500`, no shared feed with the token's routes or with the active source). Either guardian, the Emergency Guardian or the Aave Protocol Guardian through `APPLICATION_PAUSE_GUARDIAN` on the ParameterStore, may switch the token to its standby at once (`useStandbySecondSource(token)`). Switching back requires the timelock (`restoreSecondSource(token)`, normal delay). While a token's active second source fails or disagrees, borrow and withdraw-with-debt of the accounts that hold the token revert `SecondSourceDisagrees(token)`; the market's price state is unchanged and stays Live, and liquidation never reads either source. | With one second source replaceable only at the normal delay, a failure of USDC's second source would stop every borrow touching USDC on the chain for at least five days. A standby reviewed at the normal delay keeps the check and cuts the outage to the guardians' reaction time; requiring the timelock to switch back stops a guardian returning to a source that failed. | Amends `Q-323`, `Q-364`. Resolves AMMN-INT-01 |
| `Q-323` | Independent second source for borrow and withdraw-with-debt | Each token (and each non-GHO debt asset) has a second source: another oracle network's aggregator (`OracleNetwork`) or a deep pool's TWAP times a validated reference quote (`PoolTwap`). `borrowAllowed = Live ∧ sequencerReady ∧ every token confirmed (\|q − s\|·10^4 ≤ S·min(q, s)) ∧ age ≤ borrowPriceMaxAge`. The RiskModule requires `borrowAllowed`. Liquidation never reads the second source; its failure never changes state, served price or `liquidationPaused`. Tolerance lower bound: the primary legs' deviation thresholds + the second source's deviation or TWAP lag + `feedAgreementMarginBps`. No shared aggregator with the token's routes; a `PoolTwap` pool is never the market's own pool. Amends `Q-079`, `Q-229`. **Amended by `Q-385`:** a standby second source per token, switched by either guardian at once and back only through the timelock. | With day-long heartbeats a single network's frozen answer could back a borrow for up to a day; an independent source bounds that error, while keeping liquidation independent of it means a broken second source can never protect an unhealthy account. | Resolves AMMN-INT-01 (borrow side) |
| `Q-405` | Second-source confirmation and token decimals | An out-of-range second-source word leaves that token's second source unset: the token is unconfirmed (token bit 5, market bit 16) and `borrowAllowed` is false, while the primary price is computed and served as usual and its freshness, Cached and Expired rules apply unchanged; a second-source failure alone never starts, resets or extends a primary failure or grace timer, and repay and liquidation never read it. `latestAnswer`, `getPriceState` and `poke` share one primary computation; every borrow and withdraw-with-debt path enforces the confirmation on-chain in the RiskModule after its poke. Token decimals are stored at listing and re-read on every `poke()`; a mismatch or failed read fails the market's configuration (borrow, withdraw-with-debt and seizure at that valuation stop, repay and debt-free exits stay open), new decimals are never adopted automatically, and `borrowAllowed` also needs a successful verification within `DECIMALS_CHECK_MAX_AGE` (7,200 s). `POKE_MIN_GAS_FLOOR` is 900,000 gas at the implementation's entry check, provisional until a fork benchmark sets it; a gas-starved poke commits nothing. | A configuration word from a broken or upgraded ParameterStore must not freeze pricing for repay and liquidation, and a confirmation the RiskModule enforces keeps the served views simple. Decimals are delayed detection, not prevention: a transaction between a token upgrade and the next poke can still use the old decimals, so the maximum age bounds that window even when the keeper stops. | `REQ-ORACLE-100`, `REQ-ORACLE-107`, `REQ-ADAPTER-015`, `TEST-294`, `PROP-100` |
| `Q-406` | Decisions of 2026-11-11 | (1) Market-count limit: `MAX_MARKET_COUNT` is a MarketRegistry implementation immutable set at deploy, enforced on every listing path before a market is written, counting Closed and Cancelled markets; it is sized on a fork so the costliest ParameterStore re-check at that count, with the worst supported market and cold reads, uses at most 50 % of the chain's per-transaction gas limit, and measured again when an upgrade raises that cost (`REQ-MREG-034`). (2) Integration record: `recordIntegrationContracts` and `finalizeIntegrationContracts` (normal delay); no route of an integration's markets is accepted until its record is finalized; the record key is `(chain, LPVault, LPVault code hash, AMM_PROTOCOL, pool keying)`, so a replacement integration starts with an empty record; an addition applies at once and re-checks the integration's routes, disabling each that names the added contract or whose dependencies cannot be read (`REQ-MREG-035`, `REQ-MREG-020`). (3) The route rule applies to every market, address-keyed ones included (`REQ-CONFIG-040`). (4) ParameterStore second sources: a pool-TWAP source or standby never reads the pool of a market holding the token (`REQ-PSTORE-028`); an empty standby has every field zero; `useStandbySecondSource` runs `checkConfig` with the standby in force and the tighten setters that re-run `checkConfig` check the standby too; two sources served through one reader address count as shared even when their pools differ (`REQ-PSTORE-029`, `REQ-PSTORE-009`). (5) Bounded probes: each fee-recipient identity probe and each `registerIntegration` getter read runs with its own 30,000 gas and copies at most one word beyond the answer it accepts (`REQ-CONFIG-056`, `REQ-VTOKEN-044`). (6) On an L2 the sequencer grace covers every market's TWAP window and every pool-TWAP source and standby window (`REQ-PSTORE-034`), and a borrow needs the served price's rounds newer than the sequencer's restart (`SPEC-ORC-021`, `REQ-ORACLE-099`). (7) Legacy (pre-NG) Curve pools may carry a route hop only from the chain's pinned list of at most 8 reviewed pools (`REQ-ROUTE-046`). (8) The operator's flash liquidation helper accepts `flashAmount` 0: no loan, the funding Safe funds the whole repayment (`REQ-HELPER-011`, `REQ-ARCH-148`). | (1) The re-check walks every market identifier in one transaction, so the count it can afford is a property of the deployed code, which only an upgrade changes; half the gas limit leaves room for later cost increases and the setter's own work. (2) The record is what the route rule and the freeze protection check against: a route accepted before the record is complete could run through a contract added later, and a key bound to the LPVault's code keeps a record from carrying over to another build. (3) An address-keyed market's routes can also run through a protected AMM contract. (4) A market's own pool is what its LPOracle's consistency check already reads, so a TWAP of it moves with any manipulation of that pool; one encoding of an empty standby and a switch the oracles accept keep the guardian's tool usable; one reader contract is one point of failure. (5) A candidate or integration answering with a huge buffer must not raise the cost of the call. (6) A TWAP over a window that includes the outage carries prices nobody could trade against. (7) A legacy pool has no factory to pin, so review of its code gives it the provenance a factory gives an NG pool. (8) An operator holding the debt asset needs no loan and pays no premium. | `REQ-MREG-034`, `REQ-MREG-035`, `REQ-CONFIG-040`, `REQ-PSTORE-028`, `REQ-PSTORE-029`, `REQ-PSTORE-034`, `REQ-CONFIG-056`, `REQ-VTOKEN-044`, `REQ-ORACLE-099`, `REQ-ROUTE-046`, `REQ-HELPER-011`, `TEST-295`, `TEST-296`, `TEST-250`, `TEST-062`, `TEST-024` |
| `Q-327` | An oracle switch never stops liquidation | A market's LPOracle proxy address is the Aave price source and the vault token's `lpOracle()` for life; every pricing change is an upgrade of that proxy that keeps every consumed view. No LPOracle view depends on the AaveOracle source, so an Aave-side source change alters neither state nor `liquidationPaused`. LPVaultSwap keeps gating and bounding seizure by this LPOracle (no revert on a source mismatch), and the RiskModule drift check trips a breaker that stops new activity, never liquidation. Repointing the Aave reserve source is a joint operation: the Aave payload goes only with the matching Avana timelock operation. | `LIQ-006(a)` reverted every liquidation of a market whose Aave source changed, with no Avana remedy. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-20 |
| `Q-328` | Gas guard derived from the 63/64 rule | Every external read of the oracles (and every gas-limited `try` in Avana, including LPVaultSwap's breaker reports and in-kind transfers) passes explicit gas `G_k` and is preceded by `gasleft() ≥ ⌈64·G_k/63⌉ + POST_CALL_GAS`, else `InsufficientGas`. `FEED_CALL_GAS = 50,000`, `POOL_CALL_GAS = 100,000`, `CONFIG_CALL_GAS = 40,000`, `POST_CALL_GAS = 5,000`. `POKE_MIN_GAS_FLOOR = W_poke + ⌈64·G_max/63⌉`, with `W_poke` the measured worst-case poke on each chain's fork. The old after-the-catch check is replaced. Amends `Q-056`, `Q-154`. | The EVM forwards at most 63/64 of the remaining gas, so a caller who chooses the transaction gas can starve a call; a starved call still leaves 1/64 behind, so a check after the catch cannot tell starvation from failure. With the pre-call check every read receives exactly `G_k` whatever the gas limit. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-05, AMMN-GAS-04 (rule) |
| `Q-329` | n-token USD band on the geometric mean, Stable markets only | Band test `usdBandLow^⊗n ≤ ⊗ q_i` and `⊗ v_i ≤ usdBandHigh^⊗n` with `x ⊗ y = ⌊x·y/10^8⌋`, left to right; `usdLevel = ⌊(⊗ q_i)^(1/n)⌋`. It applies to markets whose tokens are all USD stables; other markets carry `usdBandLow = 0`, `usdBandHigh = max`. Amends `Q-176`, `Q-195`, `Q-275`. | It reduces exactly to the two-token test up to a floor; the fixed-point product stays below 10^40 for n ≤ 8 where the raw product would overflow 256 bits; a USD band means nothing for Correlated or Volatile pairs. | Generalizes the band to n-token vectors |
| `Q-330` | Oracle ratio outside the range is Drifting (`PRICE_CLAMPED`) | When the clamp changes the sqrt price (or `SPEC-ORC-009` clamps), the market is Drifting: new pledges and borrowing blocked, top-ups and liquidation allowed, served at `min(fresh, accepted)`. | For a Stable-tier narrow range, an oracle ratio outside the range means a depeg; the composition values the one-token holding correctly, but new debt against it at a moving price should wait. | Resolves AMMN-RT-02 (operational side) |
| `Q-364` | Feed freshness and second source in the invariants | A Chainlink leg is fresh while `now − updatedAt ≤ heartbeat + heartbeatMargin`; both values are recorded per feed at listing, in seconds, compared as integers. There is no global age cap, and the ≤ 3,600 s listing rule is removed (`Q-322`). Borrow and withdraw-with-debt also need an independent second source (a TWAP of a deep pool or a second oracle network) to agree with the feed within its deviation band (`Q-323`). Liquidation keeps its stress rules: stale collateral counts as 0 from Expired, and stale debt uses its stress price. Example: USDT/USD with a heartbeat of 86,400 s and a margin of 1,800 s has a limit of 88,200 s; a round 67,704 s old is fresh, one 88,201 s old is stale. | Chainlink stablecoin feeds update on a 0.25% deviation or on a heartbeat of 82,800–86,400 s (every Base stablecoin feed is 86,400 s), so a 4,200 s limit would keep the launch markets out of Live most of the day. While a feed is alive, its price risk is deviation plus drift, which the listing margin `(1 − CF) > deviation + drift` covers; age only shows the feed has stopped. The second source restores the check a short age limit used to give. | Resolves AMMN-INT-01, AMMN-FLOW-03 (heartbeat part) |
| `Q-372` | Oracle configuration: per-feed heartbeat, second source, sequencer, consistency check | `OracleLeg` carries `heartbeat` and `heartbeatMargin`; a leg is fresh while `now − updatedAt ≤ heartbeat + heartbeatMargin`. `OracleConfig` holds one `TokenOracleConfig` per token (primary and alternate routes, agreement tolerance, `SecondSource`), a generic consistency check (`twapWindow`, `twapMinObservations` sized to the chain's block time, `consistencyToleranceBps`), a per-block `maxQuoteChangeBps` cap for every AMM protocol, and the chain's `SequencerConfig`. Borrow and withdraw-with-debt additionally need the sequencer up and past its grace period and every token's second source within tolerance (`PriceStateView.borrowAllowed`); liquidation reads neither. `ILPOracle` has no pool, token0, token1 or tick field: it exposes `marketId`, `integration`, `tokens`, `getTokenPrices() → uint256[]`; the concentrated-liquidity detail is `ILPOracleCL.getClPriceState()`. | Chainlink stablecoin feeds publish 82,800–86,400 s heartbeats, so one global age limit is wrong for some feed. A 1,800 s TWAP needs 151 observations at 12 s blocks and 901 at 2 s blocks on Base (`Q-326`). A second source of another network or a deep pool cannot fail with the Chainlink routes. A sequencer outage stops top-ups, so borrowing waits out the grace period. | Resolves AMMN-INT-01, AMMN-RT-08, AMMN-ARCH-05 (sequencer), the per-block quote-change listing guard |

## Aave V4 configuration

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-025` | Direct liquidation path | Superseded by `Q-116`. Was: whether a direct `Spoke.liquidationCall` could seize vault-token collateral outside LPVaultSwap; it is now a deployment proof obligation in `Q-116`. | — | — |
| `Q-026` | Aave liquidation fee shares | Superseded by `Q-068`. Was: how Aave liquidation fee shares relate to LP backing; the native liquidation fee is 0, so none exist. | — | — |
| `Q-059` | Restricted liquidation Spoke | Superseded by `Q-116`. Was: a modified Spoke where only LPVaultSwap could call liquidation. **Amended by `Q-222`:** the restriction is adopted in the form Aave Labs wrote and maintains, the liquidation-manager Spoke variant with LPVaultSwap as the manager; Avana writes no Spoke code. **Amended by `Q-281`:** the liquidation-manager variant of `Q-222` is not used; the CDP Spoke is the standard Spoke and exclusivity comes from the vault token's transfer rule. | — | — |
| `Q-080` | Aave configuration drift | The Adapter checks the critical Aave settings on every new-account pledge and every borrow and blocks new pledges and borrows if they drift: vault-token reserve `receiveSharesEnabled` false, native liquidation fee 0, not borrowable, expected price source; one Spoke on the vault-token Hub asset besides its fee receiver; `drawCap` = 0 for every Spoke and zero drawn shares on that asset (`Q-181`); no reinvestment controller; collateral risk ≤ `riskPremiumThreshold` − 1 bps (`Q-178`); the reserve's underlying is the market's vault token on the Avana Hub and the debt reserve's underlying is GHO. **Amended by `Q-222`:** the check also reads the Borrow Spoke's implementation through `Spoke.extSload` at the EIP-1967 implementation slot and its `LIQUIDATION_MANAGER()`, and treats any difference from the approved implementation or from LPVaultSwap as drift. Top-ups of indebted accounts, repay, fee collection, withdrawals and liquidation continue. Monitoring alerts on drift; the deployment AIP lists the required settings. **Amended by `Q-229`:** the RiskModule runs the drift check. **Amended by `Q-237`:** the drift check also covers every approved debt reserve (identity, price source, borrowable) and the Spoke's approved debt set. **Amended by `Q-238`:** each debt reserve's Aave price source must match the one AvanaConfig records. **Amended by `Q-281`, `Q-309`:** the drift check no longer reads `LIQUIDATION_MANAGER` or an approved debt-reserve set; it checks the standard implementation and recorded AaveOracle, and that the Spoke's borrowable reserves equal the registry's enabled debt reserves, whose underlyings are their recorded assets. | The DAO owns these settings; a change to any of them could let vault tokens be borrowed, paid out as shares or seized outside Avana. Checking on every new-risk action stops new exposure as soon as drift happens. | — |
| `Q-100` | GHO debt price | The Borrow Spoke uses AaveOracle exactly as Aave V4 does, with Aave's standard fixed GHO price source. **Amended by `Q-238`:** GHO keeps the fixed source; every other debt reserve uses its approved Aave oracle feed. | Pricing GHO debt as the rest of Aave does keeps debt valuation identical to the Core Hub and outside Avana's control. | — |
