| `Q-103` | Hub shares ≠ assets | If the vault token's Hub shares stop equalling assets: new-account pledges, borrowing, withdraw-with-debt and liquidation in that market stop, with an alarm; repay, fee collection, top-ups of indebted accounts and debt-free withdrawal continue. The drift checks (`Q-181`) keep it from occurring; the DAO corrects it. | The 1:1 backing and the liquidation debit check (`Q-189`) assume one share is one unit; without that, seized units cannot be matched to backing, so everything that depends on the equality stops. | — |
| `Q-110` | Collateral factor cuts | A collateral factor cut edits the existing dynamic configuration keys so every position sees it at once, paired with an Avana market freeze; the risk reviewer sizes the step. Cuts go through Aave governance (AIP; no risk steward). There is no third-party refresh call. **Amended by `Q-246`, `Q-270`:** Avana first tightens `poolRiskFactor` at the short delay and the drift check stops new borrowing while Aave's factor is above the expected one; the Avana market freeze no longer exists (`Q-227`). | Editing the existing key applies the cut to every account immediately, with no period where old positions keep the higher factor; the freeze stops new exposure while the AIP passes. | — |
| `Q-111` | Risk premium threshold | Superseded by `Q-141`. Was: threshold set to the highest collateral risk in use and never lowered while debt is outstanding. | — | — |
| `Q-116` | Liquidation entry | Unmodified Aave V4 Borrow Spoke. LPVaultSwap, called by approved keepers only, calls the standard `liquidationCall` with `receiveShares = false`. Vault-token reserves: collateral-share payouts disabled, native liquidation fee 0; vault-token transfers to unauthorized recipients revert. Deployment requires proof that no vault-token movement and no deficit report happens except through LPVaultSwap, including calls routed through any allowed holder that receives ordinary withdrawals; proxies have no liquidation path. The indexer accepts liquidation events for foreign accounts that hold no vault-token collateral. **Amended by `Q-222`:** the Borrow Spoke is the liquidation-manager Spoke variant written by Aave Labs, not the unmodified Spoke; only LPVaultSwap can call `liquidationCall`, so no foreign liquidation event can occur. **Amended by `Q-213`:** any address may also call LPVaultSwap's permissionless entry point; `Q-222` moves the restriction on direct `liquidationCall` callers into the Borrow Spoke's code. **Amended by `Q-237`:** the Spoke's borrowable set is the approved debt-reserve set; LPVaultSwap calls `liquidationCall` with the debt reserve named in the call. **Amended by `Q-281`:** `Q-222` and `Q-237` no longer apply: the CDP Spoke is the unmodified standard Spoke this decision chose, a foreign `liquidationCall` reverts on the vault token's transfer rule (`Q-300`), and LPVaultSwap calls the 5-argument `liquidationCall` with `receiveShares = false`. | Unmodified Aave code avoids a custom Spoke that Aave would have to maintain and audit. A direct liquidator's call reverts because it cannot receive the seized vault tokens. | `Q-025`, `Q-059` |
| `Q-141` | Risk premium threshold margin | `riskPremiumThreshold` = highest collateral risk in use + at least 1 bps; never lowered while debt exists; a fork test with many accounts at the top risk proves repay and liquidation pass. | If a collateral risk reaches the threshold, Aave reverts borrow, withdraw, non-deficit liquidation and refresh with `InvalidPremiumChange`; the margin keeps every account below that point. | `Q-111` |
| `Q-144` | Rule cleanups | PositionProxies are fixed clones; the LPVault pulls and releases NFTs with `transferFrom` and calls the ProxyFactory's get-or-create; stray NFTs follow `Q-104`. Top-ups follow `Q-132`; Aave configuration drift and Hub shares ≠ assets block new-account pledges but spare top-ups of indebted accounts; LPOracle never reverts on a price read; there is no third-party refresh. The FlashMinter-unavailable case uses keeper GHO only; repay pulls GHO through the borrower's proxy. The seizure proof reads "no vault-token movement and no deficit report except via LPVaultSwap" (`Q-116`). **Amended by `Q-225`:** proxies are beacon proxies created by the ProxyManager for the Adapter; Collateralization takes custody through the LPVault, and neither calls a proxy factory. **Amended by `Q-306`, `Q-336`:** proxies are logic-free immutable contracts created by the immutable ProxyManager, and when flash liquidity is short the caller funds the debt asset itself. | Each cleanup removes a conflict between an earlier rule and a later decision, so the contract pages have one meaning. | — |
| `Q-172` | Housekeeping | No daily borrow limit in v1; Hub draw caps bound exposure. The drift checks (`Q-080`) include Spoke `drawCap` = 0 on the vault-token asset, a single Spoke on that asset, no reinvestment controller and the expected price source. Updating the public governance proposal and setting the credit-line growth metrics are governance tasks outside the spec. | A daily limit would need enforcement in the Spoke's borrow path; draw caps already bound total exposure. **Corrected by `Q-220`:** the Adapter's `checkBorrow` sees every borrow and could enforce a limit; v1 has none because the Hub draw caps and the caps set with the Aave service providers (`Q-197`) bound exposure. | — |
| `Q-178` | Collateral-risk increases | Any collateral-risk increase on a vault-token reserve must follow a threshold increase to the new top risk + 1 bps. The drift check (`Q-080`) asserts collateral risk ≤ threshold − 1 bps and blocks new borrows if violated. | Raising risk first would briefly put accounts at or above the threshold, where repay and liquidation revert (`Q-141`). | — |
| `Q-181` | Hub-side drift check | Deployment and drift checks also assert `drawCap` = 0 for every Spoke on the vault-token Hub asset and zero drawn shares for that asset. **Amended by `Q-270`:** the drift check also covers the collateral factor, the bonus bounds and the target health factor of every vault-token reserve. | Any draw on the vault-token asset would let someone borrow vault tokens out of the Hub and break shares == assets. | — |
| `Q-188` | GHO-leg collateral factor | Listing defines an explicit collateral factor for a GHO leg (`ghoLegCollateralFactorBps`, for example equal to USDC's) instead of the Core Hub value. **Amended by `Q-317`:** every token without a Core Hub collateral factor, GHO included, gets an explicit `tokenCollateralFactorBps` at listing. | GHO's Core Hub collateral setting reflects Core Hub policy, not its risk as half of a stable LP. | — |
| `Q-189` | Debit equality and share drift | The liquidation debit check compares the share drop with Aave's shares-to-liquidate and requires Hub shares == assets; the Hub drift checks (`Q-181`) keep it true. If shares ≠ assets, liquidation in that market stops with an alarm. | With shares ≠ assets, the units removed from the proxy no longer equal the vault tokens delivered, so a debit could release the wrong amount of liquidity. | — |
| `Q-202` | Aave-side reserve pauses | An Aave Guardian pause of a vault-token or GHO reserve is an Aave-governed risk with no Avana expiry. The deployment AIP asks the DAO to pause Avana reserves only for exploits and to unpause within `incidentDuration`; caps are sized for it through the pause-loss bound (`Q-192`). Documented in 1.3 and 4.3. **Amended by `Q-227`:** the AIP asks the DAO to unpause within the 48-hour incident window that caps are sized for (`Q-192`); the request covers every debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-290`, `Q-351`:** containment of a CDP Spoke uses the reserve freeze and `drawCap = 0` before any pause or halt. | Avana cannot override an Aave pause; sizing caps for it keeps the accepted loss bounded. | — |
| `Q-220` | Daily borrow limit | No daily borrow limit in v1. The Aave Adapter's `checkBorrow` sees every borrow and could enforce one, so the reason is not the unmodified Spoke. Exposure is bounded instead by the Hub draw caps on the credit line and by the per-market, per-token and per-depositor caps set with the Aave service providers (`Q-197`); the immutable proxy's health-factor floor and price-age checks bound each borrow. Corrects the reasoning of `Q-172`. | A rate limit would slow how fast a pricing error turns into debt, but it adds state to an upgradeable contract and a parameter to govern, while the caps already bound the largest loss such an error can cause. | — |
| `Q-221` | Liquidator restriction | Superseded by `Q-222`. Was: keep the unmodified Borrow Spoke and rely on the listing configuration of each vault-token reserve (native liquidation fee 0, not borrowable, `receiveSharesEnabled` false), the vault token's recipient restriction, the drift checks and the deployment proof to make a direct `liquidationCall` fail, accepting that a DAO payload changing one of those settings would let a third party liquidate an Avana account outside LPVaultSwap. **Amended by `Q-281`:** `Q-222` is itself superseded; the standard-Spoke design described here is the one Avana uses, with the drift check and the activation checks of `Q-309` as its controls. | — | — |
| `Q-222` | Liquidation-manager Borrow Spoke | Every Avana Borrow Spoke is the liquidation-manager Spoke: a variant written by Aave Labs and maintained in `aave/aave-v4`, built on the overridable `Spoke.sol` of commit `455a40659431a512aa541fe62d18cb0ae918c9d4`, deployed by Aave and approved by the Aave DAO in the listing AIP; the Hub stays the unmodified Aave V4 Hub. Its constructor immutable `LIQUIDATION_MANAGER` is the LPVaultSwap proxy address. The canonical 5-argument `liquidationCall` is its only liquidation entry point; it reverts with `Unauthorized()` for any caller other than the manager and with `UnsupportedLiquidationCall()` when `receiveShares` is true, and otherwise runs canonical Aave V4 liquidation (target health factor sizing, dynamic bonus, dust rule, deficit reporting) unchanged. A non-zero liquidation fee is rejected on every key (`UnsupportedLiquidationFee()`), and every reserve outside the Spoke's set of approved debt reserves (`APPROVED_DEBT_RESERVES`, which at launch holds the GHO reserve) is rejected as borrowable on `addReserve`, `updateReserveConfig` and the `initialize` run on every upgrade (`UnsupportedBorrowableCollateral()`). Everything else is canonical Aave V4 at the pin. Avana lists only on such a Spoke. AvanaConfig records the approved implementation; the Adapter's drift check compares it on-chain with `Spoke.extSload` at the EIP-1967 implementation slot and checks `LIQUIDATION_MANAGER() == LPVaultSwap`, and a mismatch closes new pledges and borrows the way configuration drift does until Avana re-approves the new implementation. The Hub implementation is checked off-chain. Changing the manager needs a new Spoke implementation. Both the approved-keeper path and the permissionless fallback (`Q-213`) enter through LPVaultSwap and keep working. Supersedes `Q-221`; amends `Q-003`, `Q-059`, `Q-080` and `Q-116`. **Amended by `Q-237`:** the single `DEBT_RESERVE_ID` is replaced by the Spoke's set of approved debt reserves. **Amended by `Q-240`:** the MarketRegistry records the approved implementation and the RiskModule runs the drift check (`Q-229`). **Superseded by `Q-281`:** every CDP Spoke is a standard, unmodified Aave V4 Spoke whose 5-argument `liquidationCall` LPVaultSwap calls with `receiveShares = false`, and exclusivity comes from the vault token's transfer rule plus the drift-checked settings, because Aave's liquidation-manager variant takes a 4-argument call and one collateral per Spoke. | Aave enforces in code what Avana otherwise relied on listing flags for: no third party can liquidate an Avana account or receive vault-token shares, even if a DAO payload later changes `receiveSharesEnabled`, the liquidation fee or `borrowable`, so the accepted risk of `Q-221` disappears instead of being monitored. The variant is Aave Labs code in Aave's own repository, so it stays inside Aave's maintenance and audit process and Avana still writes no Aave code. Canonical liquidation sizing stays because Avana's LP collateral is divisible and partial seizure depends on Aave sizing each liquidation toward the target health factor. Code guarantees hold only for the deployed implementation, so the implementation itself becomes a drift item. | `Q-221` |
| `Q-237` | Debt-reserve set on the liquidation-manager Spoke | The liquidation-manager Borrow Spoke (`Q-222`) holds a set of approved debt reserves instead of one `DEBT_RESERVE_ID`: a constructor-immutable bitmap `APPROVED_DEBT_RESERVES` over reserve IDs, read through `isApprovedDebtReserve(reserveId)`. Every reserve outside the set, including every vault-token reserve, is rejected as borrowable on `addReserve`, `updateReserveConfig` and the `initialize` run on every upgrade (`UnsupportedBorrowableCollateral()`), and a non-zero liquidation fee is rejected on every key, so collateral reserves stay non-borrowable with liquidation fee 0 in code. Adding a debt reserve needs a new Spoke implementation, which Avana re-approves through the drift check of `Q-222`. The MarketRegistry's approved debt set for the Spoke must equal the Spoke's set; any difference is configuration drift. Amends `Q-080`, `Q-116`, `Q-222`. **Superseded by `Q-281`:** a CDP Spoke's borrowable reserves are those `enableDebtReserve` recorded, and the drift check requires the Spoke's borrowable set to equal them. | The set keeps Aave's code guarantee that no vault token can be borrowed while letting several assets be lent against the same collateral. Making it an immutable, like the liquidation manager, means a reserve becomes borrowable only through a reviewed implementation that Avana's drift check sees. | — |
| `Q-270` | Drift check on Aave risk configuration | The drift check compares Aave's live risk configuration of every vault-token reserve with the values Avana's sizing assumes. `DriftReason` gains `CollateralFactorAboveExpected` (a dynamic configuration key's collateral factor above `expectedCollateralFactorBps(m)`), `BonusBoundViolated` (`⌈maxLiquidationBonus × CF / 10⁴⌉ ≥ 10⁴`, or the minimum bonus below `minLiquidationBonus`) and `LiquidationConfigChanged` (target health factor different from `aaveTargetHealthFactor`). New-account pledges and the borrow-side checks revert `ConfigDrift` on any of them; top-ups, exits and liquidation are unaffected. Amends `Q-181`. **Amended by `Q-393`:** the expected values are the ones the Aave DAO set in the listing AIP, from Avana's recommended guidance. | Avana's caps, listing margin and two-leg slippage bound all assume a collateral factor and a bonus curve; an Aave payload that raised the factor or cut the bonus after activation left Avana opening loans with a smaller margin than every sizing rule assumed. Treating those changes as drift stops new exposure the moment they apply, and leaving liquidation open keeps the accounts already open protected. | — |
| `Q-281` | Standard Spoke with three required settings | Every CDP Spoke is a standard, unmodified Aave V4 Spoke. No liquidation-manager variant is used: the only one in Aave's code replaces `liquidationCall` with a collateral-capped call that has no collateral-reserve argument and allows 1 collateral reserve per Spoke. The listing AIP sets, and Avana's drift check enforces on every Avana collateral reserve: `receiveSharesEnabled = false`; `liquidationFee = 0` (or a fee paid to Avana's fee receiver); no reinvestment controller. Liquidation exclusivity comes from the vault token's transfer rule (`Q-300`), which allows supply from a registered proxy into the Hub, withdrawal from the Hub to a registered proxy and seizure from the Hub to LPVaultSwap; any other liquidator's `liquidationCall` reverts on the token transfer. Each vault-token Hub asset has exactly one Spoke with non-zero caps, its `vaultToken.spoke()`, checked at listing and by the drift check. Drift in any of these trips a breaker, which stops new activity and never liquidation. LPVaultSwap calls the standard 5-argument `liquidationCall(collateralReserveId, debtReserveId, user, debtToCover, receiveShares)` with `receiveShares = false`. Supersedes `Q-222` (the liquidation-manager Spoke) and `Q-237` (the immutable approved debt-reserve set: the borrowable reserves are those `enableDebtReserve` recorded, checked by the drift check). | A drift alarm cannot stop a seizure in the same block, so the three settings are listed in the AIP, checked at activation, and checked again on every pledge and borrow; while they hold, the transfer rule is the only gate needed. A second Spoke on a vault-token asset could draw vault tokens out of the Hub and leave proxy withdrawals and seizures unable to settle. | `Q-222`, `Q-237`. Resolves AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-ARCH-07, AMMN-RT-03, AMMN-SM-04, AMMN-INT-07, AMMN-RT-12 |
| `Q-290` | Aave Hub halt scope | A Hub halt of a Spoke on an asset stops `add`, `remove`, `draw`, `restore` and share transfers, so supply, withdraw, borrow, repay and liquidation of that Spoke on that asset stop. Deficit reporting and elimination, premium refresh and fee-share payment continue, so a Coverage Spoke that is not halted can still cover the halted Spoke's deficit. Avana treats a halt as an Aave-side containment: the playbook asks the DAO to lift it within the 48-hour incident window. Amends `Q-140`, `Q-202`. | The pinned Hub checks only `active` in `reportDeficit`, `eliminateDeficit`, `payFeeShares` and `refreshPremium`; stating the real scope keeps coverage planning correct during a halt. | Resolves AMMN-INT-08 |
| `Q-382` | Exposure window of a standard-Spoke setting change | The drift check (`checkAaveConfig`) runs on every Avana entry point that opens new risk and on a per-block keeper poke, `RiskModule.pokeConfig(spoke)`, which anyone may call and Avana's keeper simulates every block and sends only when the result changes state; a failure emits `ConfigDriftDetected(spoke, reasons)` and starts the 3.6 playbook (monitoring alarm, request to the Aave Protocol Guardian to freeze the reserve and set the Spoke's `drawCap`s to 0). Avana's exposure to a changed setting (`receiveSharesEnabled`, `liquidationFee`, reinvestment controller, borrowable vault-token reserve, extra Spoke, implementation, oracle) lasts from the Aave change until the drift check trips `CONFIG_DRIFT`: at most the block of the change and the next block. From then on Avana opens no new account and adds no debt on the Spoke, and liquidation through LPVaultSwap continues. Seizures outside LPVaultSwap while the setting stays changed, for example a third party's `liquidationCall(…, receiveShares = true)` followed by a borrow against those shares on the Spoke, are not Avana calls, so no Avana check can stop them: they are an accepted residual risk, bounded by the Spoke's draw caps, and they end when Aave reverts the setting. | The Spoke is unmodified Aave code and the settings are Aave DAO configuration, so Avana can detect a change and stop its own flows but cannot gate a third party's call to the Spoke. The poke keeps detection within a block even when no user calls Avana. A third party holding seized shares can borrow at most what the Spoke's draw caps allow, which the DAO sized for the Spoke. | Amends `Q-309`, `Q-362` (`INV-093`). Resolves AMMN-RT-03, AMMN-SM-04, AMMN-INT-07 |
| `Q-309` | Drift check of the required Spoke settings | `deployCdpSpoke`, `listCollateralMarket` and activation refuse a Spoke or market unless, on every key: `receiveSharesEnabled == false`; `liquidationFee == 0`; no reinvestment controller; the vault-token reserve is not borrowable; no other Spoke is on the vault-token asset; and the Spoke runs the approved standard implementation with the recorded AaveOracle. After listing, any drift trips the Spoke's `CONFIG_DRIFT` condition breaker, which stops new-account pledges, borrows, withdraw-with-debt and removal with debt, and never stops liquidation, repay, top-ups, debt-free exits or fee collection. Nothing checks a liquidation-manager address or an approved debt-reserve set. The Spoke's borrowable-reserve set must equal the registry's enabled debt reserves. Residual risk: a configuration change and an attack in the same block are bounded only by the Aave DAO's governance delay and the listing AIP's commitments. Same rule as `Q-352`, stated for the RiskModule and the MarketRegistry. Amends `Q-080`. **Amended by `Q-382`:** the exposure window ends when the drift check trips, within a block through the keeper poke; seizures outside LPVaultSwap are an accepted residual risk bounded by the Spoke's draw caps. | The liquidation-manager variant takes 4 arguments and allows 1 collateral, so it does not fit. On a standard Spoke these settings are DAO configuration, so a field check is the main control rather than a duplicate of code. | Resolves AMMN-RT-03, AMMN-SM-04, AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-INT-07, AMMN-ARCH-10 |
| `Q-346` | Aave-side halts in liquidation | An Aave halt of a CDP Spoke or a pause of a vault-token or debt reserve stops repay, withdraw and liquidation of that market; Avana cannot override it. The listing AIP asks Aave to contain a CDP Spoke with a reserve freeze and `drawCap = 0`, never `haltSpoke` or `pauseReserve`, while debt exists. The RiskModule reports a halted Spoke as drift, monitoring pages on it, and the keepers' playbook liquidates every eligible account of the market in the first block after an unpause. | The "liquidation never paused" rule binds Avana's switches only; the Aave-side exception must be stated with its containment path. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-12 |
| `Q-351` | Aave-side halts are containment, never the first tool | A Hub `haltSpoke` of a CDP Spoke, a Hub deactivation, or an Aave reserve pause of a vault-token or debt reserve blocks repay, debt-free withdrawal and liquidation of that market, because the Hub refuses `restore`, `remove` and the seizure transfer. The listing payload agreement states that containment of a CDP Spoke uses, in order: the vault-token reserve freeze, `drawCap = 0` on every debt asset for the Spoke, and Avana's own pause; `haltSpoke`, `deactivateSpoke` and `pauseReserve` are used only when the Spoke itself is faulty and no debt can be served safely. The RiskModule's drift check carries reason `SpokeHalted` (Hub `halted`, Spoke inactive, or a paused Avana reserve), monitoring alarms on it within one block, and playbook P-16 covers the halt: Avana keepers prepare liquidations of every account below health factor 1 at the halt's start prices, the Guardian asks the Aave Guardian to lift the halt for liquidation and repay (or to convert it to freeze plus `drawCap = 0`), and keepers run the prepared liquidations in health-factor order in the first block after the lift. | The rule that pause states never block liquidation, repay, debt-free withdrawal or fees binds Avana's states only. With several markets per Spoke, a halt also stops healthy markets. Freeze plus `drawCap = 0` stops all new risk while leaving every exit open, so it is the containment step that keeps the guarantee. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-12 |
| `Q-352` | Liquidation exclusivity on a standard Spoke | CDP Spokes are standard, unmodified Aave V4 Spokes. Exclusivity comes from the vault token's transfer rule (`Q-300`): a vault-token transfer is accepted only from a registered proxy to the home Hub (supply), from the home Hub to a registered proxy (withdrawal, after which the Adapter burns from the proxy) and from the home Hub to LPVaultSwap (seizure); zero-amount transfers to any other recipient revert too. A foreign `liquidationCall` therefore reverts on the seizure transfer. The drift check covers every Aave setting that could move vault tokens elsewhere: `receiveSharesEnabled = false`, `liquidationFee = 0` (or the fee receiver is Avana's), `reinvestmentController = address(0)` on the vault-token asset, `borrowable = false`, the vault-token asset listed on no other Spoke, `drawCap = 0` on it everywhere, and no position-manager approvals on any proxy. Drift trips the market breaker `CONFIG_DRIFT`, which stops new-account pledges, borrows and withdraw-with-debt; liquidation, repay, top-ups, debt-free withdrawal and fee collection continue. | A liquidation-manager Spoke variant whose call names a manager exists in no Aave source, and Aave's liquidation-manager design takes one collateral per Spoke. The standard Spoke's 5-argument `liquidationCall(collateralReserveId, debtReserveId, user, debtToCover, receiveShares)` lets anyone call it, so LPVaultSwap calls it with `receiveShares = false`, `receiveSharesEnabled = false` blocks the shares path for everyone, and the vault token's transfer rule makes every other caller's seizure transfer revert. A transfer rule plus a checked configuration gives exclusivity with an unmodified Spoke. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-13, AMMN-INT-04, AMMN-RT-03, AMMN-SM-04 |
| `Q-393` | Aave Spoke liquidation settings are guidance | `aaveTargetHealthFactor` 1.10 and `minLiquidationBonus` ≥ 2 % per CDP Spoke are Avana's recommended guidance for the listing AIP; the Aave DAO decides the live values. Avana records the values the AIP set (`setLiquidationExpectations`), and the drift check compares the live configuration with them. Amends `Q-179`, `Q-185`, `Q-270`. | The liquidation configuration of an Aave Spoke belongs to the Aave DAO. Avana sizes its caps, margins and liveness bound from what the DAO set, and stops new activity if a live value later differs. | — |

## Borrower operations

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-029` | Debt-free withdrawal | An account with zero debt can withdraw its NFT in every price state and under every Avana restriction; the proxy reads zero debt from the Spoke's debt balances and never consults the policy. Only an Aave reserve pause or Hub halt can block it, and a zero-credit record is always releasable. **Amended by `Q-223`:** debt-free withdrawal goes through the Adapter, which never applies the pause state or any gate to it; zero debt means zero on every debt reserve (`Q-234`). | A borrower with no debt poses no risk; letting an Avana condition hold the NFT would turn an outage into a custody lock. | — |
| `Q-051` | Repay availability | No Avana pause, freeze, restriction or price state can block repay, fee collection or debt-free withdrawal; only Aave can. Avana pauses are per function and per market (pledge, borrow, withdraw-with-debt, liquidation), never one global switch. **Amended by `Q-227`:** Avana has one pause state (None, Frozen, Paused) instead of per-function, per-market pauses; repay, fee collection and debt-free withdrawal stay open in every state. Under `Q-223` a faulty Adapter implementation could also block repay and withdrawal, which the timelock bounds. | Blocking repayment during an incident would push borrowers into the liquidation they are trying to avoid. | — |
| `Q-087` | Adding collateral | Superseded by `Q-132`. Was: top-ups allowed in every price state. | — | — |
| `Q-105` | Receiver | The explicit receiver applies to borrowed GHO only, defaulting to the borrower's wallet. Withdrawn NFTs and collected fees always go to the depositor of record. **Amended by `Q-233`:** the receiver applies to whichever debt asset is borrowed. | A receiver field on withdrawals would let a phishing signature send the NFT elsewhere; GHO needs a receiver for integrations. | — |
| `Q-106` | Duplicate borrows | Per-proxy borrow nonce: the wallet-signed call carries the expected nonce; the proxy checks and increments it, after authorization and deadline, before calling `Spoke.borrow`; every borrow path passes this check. The app keeps one pending borrow per proxy across tabs, reuses the prepared intent and reads the nonce on-chain when preparing. `maxTotalDebtAfter` is an optional extra bound. **Amended by `Q-229`:** the nonce is kept per proxy in the Adapter. | A resubmitted or double-clicked borrow would otherwise draw GHO twice. | — |
| `Q-132` | Top-up states | Adding collateral to an account with debt is allowed only into markets that are Live or Drifting, never Cached or Expired; a market freeze blocks it; a pledge pause does not (`Q-164`). A new account requires Live. | A top-up priced on a stale feed could be credited at the wrong value; Drifting still has fresh Chainlink prices, so rescue top-ups stay possible. | `Q-087` |
| `Q-146` | Repay floor | Superseded by `Q-153`. Was: a partial repay could not leave debt between 0 and 1,000 GHO. | — | — |
| `Q-153` | Partial repay | Any partial repay is allowed. **Amended by `Q-311`:** the per-chain minimum debt applies to borrows only, so any partial repay is still allowed. **Amended by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt at all. | With no minimum debt (`Q-169`), a repay floor would only stop borrowers reducing risk. | `Q-146` |
| `Q-169` | No minimum debt | There is no minimum debt and no minimum position value. Dust positions are accepted; Aave's dust rule forces full liquidation of small debts, and keeper reimbursement (`Q-098`) covers unprofitable liquidations. Accepted risk: many tiny loans can become bad debt or reimbursement cost during gas spikes. **Reaffirmed by `Q-215`**, which restates the accepted risk and its controls. **Amended by `Q-230`:** each pledge must be worth at least `minPledgeUsd`; there is still no minimum debt. **Reaffirmed by `Q-277`.** **Superseded by `Q-287`:** a minimum debt per chain, derived from liquidation gas ($2,000 on Ethereum, $50 on Base at launch), applies to every borrow, while repay and liquidation never read it. **Restored by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt on any chain; Avana's own keepers liquidate every account and pay its gas. | A minimum debt blocks partial repayment and small borrowers without solving the gas cost of small liquidations, which the dust rule and reimbursement address directly. | — |
| `Q-180` | Repay-all | The proxy's repay takes a `repayAll` flag and a signed `maxRepaySpend`. At execution it reads `getUserTotalDebt(ghoReserveId, address(this))`, including interest and premium; if zero it returns 0 without moving tokens; if above the cap it reverts before pulling GHO; otherwise it pulls and approves the exact live amount, calls `Spoke.repay`, and requires remaining GHO debt to be zero. **Amended by `Q-234`:** repay-all is per debt reserve through the Adapter, and `repayAllAndWithdraw` clears every named debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-269`:** repay-all pulls `maxRepaySpend` and refunds the unused part in the same call. | Debt grows every block, so an amount fixed at signing either leaves dust or overpays; reading the live amount under a signed ceiling clears the debt exactly. | — |
| `Q-212` | Third-party repay | `PositionProxy.repay(maxRepaySpend, repayAll, deadline)` accepts any caller. The payer is `msg.sender`: the proxy pulls GHO from `msg.sender` and never from the borrower unless the borrower is the caller, approves the Spoke for the exact amount, repays the proxy's own account and refunds any GHO the Spoke did not consume to `msg.sender`. The deadline, the `repayAll` cap against the live debt and the zero-debt return of 0 (`Q-180`, `Q-209`) apply to every caller. `borrow`, `withdraw` and `repayAllAndWithdraw` stay borrower-only, so a third party can lower the debt but never move collateral or GHO out of the account. A third-party repay that lands first costs the borrower nothing: the borrower's later repay reads the lower live debt, a repay-all pulls only what remains under its cap, and at zero debt the call returns 0 and pulls nothing. `Repaid` names the payer and the borrower. **Amended by `Q-223`:** the payer calls `AaveAdapter.repay` and approves the Adapter, which pulls from `msg.sender` to the proxy; the repay names the debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-260`:** repay no longer checks the registry's debt-reserve list; the Spoke rejects reserves it does not lend. **Amended by `Q-349`:** Aave V4's `Spoke.repay` is `onlyPositionManager` and proxies approve no position manager, so every repay, a third party's included, goes through `AaveAdapter.repay` and the proxy's `execute`. | Repaying only lowers an account's risk and makes no price or policy call, so admitting any payer opens no path to harm the account; it lets the borrower's other wallets, a rescue service or the protocol save a position near liquidation, including one whose key is lost. The proxy is immutable, so who may repay is fixed at deployment. | — |
| `Q-215` | No minimum debt reaffirmed | `Q-169` stands after review of small-loan liquidation profitability: there is no minimum debt, no minimum position value and no borrow-side minimum. Accepted risk: a small debt can cost more gas to liquidate than its bonus pays, so during gas spikes many small accounts can turn into keeper-reimbursement cost or, if nobody liquidates them, into bad debt that the First-Loss Reserve covers. Controls: Aave's dust rule forces a small debt to be liquidated in full in one call; approved keepers must liquidate every account below HF 1 and are reimbursed for loss-making liquidations (`Q-098`, `Q-185`); the permissionless fallback (`Q-213`) admits other callers when keepers stall; and the SettlementBudget closes the rounding gaps of dust liquidations (`Q-198`). **Reaffirmed by `Q-277`.** **Superseded by `Q-287`:** a minimum debt per chain, derived from liquidation gas, keeps every account worth liquidating at that chain's gas cost. **Restored by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt; Avana's keepers liquidate every account at Avana's own gas cost. | A minimum debt or a borrow-side minimum would only stop borrowers opening small loans; small debts still arise from partial repays, partial liquidations and price moves, and the dust rule, reimbursement and the fallback handle them wherever they come from. | — |
| `Q-252` | Rescue top-ups | A rescue top-up is a pledge into an existing account that has debt (`borrowCount > 0`), whose health factor before the pledge is below `minOpeningHealthFactor`, and whose booked charge is at most the restore amount of the top-up slice rule. Rescue top-ups are exempt from the pool-share cap, the application Frozen state, `Paused` with reason `General`, circuit breakers (`Q-241`) and the address freeze (`Q-242`), and must reach `min(minPledgeUsd, $10)` instead of `minPledgeUsd`. Every other pledge check applies: full range, the market's price state Live or Drifting, market not offboarding, the regular room or slice of the cap test, token and depositor caps for the regular part, the Hub `addCap` and the per-account reserve limit. `pause(reason)` carries a reason: under `General` rescue top-ups are the only pledges; under `MintPathSuspect` no pledge, top-up or re-credit runs. The pause guardian or the admin can change the reason; raising it to `MintPathSuspect` is immediate for both guardians. Frozen still admits every top-up of an indebted account. Units admitted through an exemption are rescue units (`Q-253`). Amends `Q-054`, `Q-157`, `Q-227`, `Q-230`. **Amended by `Q-293`:** the shape check is the exact-shape match of the market instead of full range. **Amended by `Q-398`:** eligibility uses `rescueHealthFactor` (1.10) in place of `minOpeningHealthFactor`. | Adding collateral is the cheapest way out of liquidation, and incidents (a depeg that drains the pool, a pause, a tripped breaker) are when borrowers need it most; refusing it then turned a containment step into liquidations. A rescue top-up adds collateral to an account that already has debt, is capped at what restores the opening health factor and cannot be borrowed against, so it cannot grow exposure. The one case where it stays refused is a pause because the minting code itself is suspect, since every top-up mints vault tokens. | — |
| `Q-253` | Rescue units do not support new debt | Collateralization books on each record its rescue units: the part of a top-up admitted through the slice (`ceil(L × sliceUsd / usdCharge)`) or the whole pledge when it needed a whole-pledge exemption of `Q-252`. After every debit of the record they are at most its credited units, and Collateralization reports each account's total per market. The RiskModule's health check after borrowing, withdraw-with-debt and partial removal with debt computes the health factor with rescue units valued at zero (the excluded value rounded up) and requires `max(minOpeningHealthFactor, PROXY_HEALTH_FACTOR_FLOOR, minHealthFactor)` of it. Aave's health factor, liquidation and seizure order count rescue units in full. Rescue units leave with their record; a withdrawn rescue NFT can be pledged again as an ordinary top-up. Amends `Q-157`, `Q-229`. **Amended by `Q-398`:** the requirement is `hfEx ≥ max(1, hardHealthFloor[m], minHealthFactor)`; `minOpeningHealthFactor` and `PROXY_HEALTH_FACTOR_FLOOR` no longer exist, and the optional hard floor is off by default. | The slice and the rescue exemptions exist to keep indebted accounts out of liquidation. If the collateral they admit could support new debt, a borrower could top up from the slice on every dip and borrow back to 1.10, turning the rescue budget into growth past the caps. Valuing it at zero only for new debt keeps it fully protective in liquidation while denying it as borrowing power. | — |
| `Q-255` | Repay with collateral | A borrower can repay debt with liquidity from one of its pledged NFTs, with no liquidation bonus: `AaveAdapter.repayWithCollateral(params)` names one record, `units`, one debt reserve, `minDebtRepaid`, `maxGhoSpend` (non-GHO debt), a deadline and `minHealthFactor`. In one call LPVaultSwap flash-mints GHO and, for a non-GHO debt, buys the debt asset within the oracle bound and `maxGhoSpend`; the proxy repays `x` of the debt; the proxy withdraws `units`, the Adapter burns them, Collateralization debits the record and the LPVault removes that liquidity to LPVaultSwap; LPVaultSwap converts it to GHO over the market's routes with oracle-derived minimums on every leg (`Q-250`, slippage `slippageAllowanceBps`), sells back unspent debt asset, repays the flash loan and fee and sends the rest to the wallet in GHO. `x` is at most the oracle value of `units` after slippage, the one-unit-per-leg allowance and the flash fee, and at least `minDebtRepaid`. The call requires the market's and the debt reserve's prices at liquidation freshness and Hub shares == assets, and reverts if conversion does not cover the flash loan and fee, if debt remains and Aave's health factor after the call is below its value before the call or below `minHealthFactor`, or if Aave refuses the withdrawal. Paused and the address freeze refuse it; Frozen and circuit breakers do not. No Avana fee, SettlementBudget draw or keeper reimbursement applies; the borrower pays the flash fee and the conversion cost. Withdrawing every credited unit of the record returns the NFT to its depositor. Amends `Q-223`, `Q-224`. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-334`, `Q-356`:** LPVaultSwap flash-borrows the debt asset itself through `IFlashSource`, with no buy or sell-back leg and no `maxGhoSpend`, and pays the remainder in the debt asset; the call runs under `Paused(General)` and for a frozen wallet when it only reduces debt, and stays refused under `Paused(MintPathSuspect)`, at a Cached or Expired price and while the sequencer is Down. | A borrower near liquidation who holds no spare debt asset could only wait for a keeper and pay the bonus. Converting part of the collateral to repay debt performs the same unwinding a liquidation does, at oracle-bounded prices and without the bonus. Repaying before withdrawing inside one flash-funded call keeps the health factor from dipping during the step, and requiring it not to fall means the call can only reduce risk; it is not a loop, since nothing is borrowed (`Q-031`). It runs the Adapter's withdrawal and health checks, so it stays closed while that code is suspect (Paused) and for a frozen address, which keeps plain repay. | — |
| `Q-269` | Repay-all quote and refund | For `repayAll` the Adapter pulls `maxRepaySpend` from the payer to the proxy, repays the live debt and refunds the unused part to the payer in the same call, so the payer's allowance ends at zero; the app sets `maxRepaySpend` and the approval to the Lens `quoteRepayAll` for the transaction's deadline (`Q-268`). A partial repay still pulls exactly `min(maxRepaySpend, debt)`. Amends `Q-180`. | Debt accrues every second, so a cap and approval equal to the debt quoted at signing always fell short at inclusion and every first repay-all reverted. Quoting to the deadline and refunding the unused part makes the first attempt succeed and still leaves no standing allowance. | — |
| `Q-347` | Repay with collateral under `Paused(General)` | `deleverage` runs under `Paused(General)` when it only reduces debt and does not lower the health factor, and for a frozen wallet; it stays refused under `Paused(MintPathSuspect)` (`Q-356`). Amends `Q-255`. | A borrower without outside funds could not deleverage during a crash, while keepers could still liquidate the same account at a bonus. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-26 |
| `Q-356` | Repay with collateral that only reduces debt | `repayWithCollateral` runs under `Paused` with reason `General`, for a frozen wallet and while a market breaker is tripped, when it only reduces debt: the Adapter requires the account's debt on the repaid reserve to fall by at least the repaid amount, no new debt, the health factor after the call at least the health factor before (`hfAfter ≥ hfBefore`), and the unwound value sent nowhere except to repay that debt and to the borrower's surplus. It stays refused under `Paused(MintPathSuspect)` (it burns vault tokens through the suspect path), when the market's price state is Cached or Expired (the swap floor needs a fresh price), and while the sequencer gate is Down. Amends `Q-242`, `Q-255`. | In a crash with `Paused(General)`, a borrower without outside funds could otherwise only wait to be liquidated at a bonus, while keepers can still liquidate. Deleveraging that strictly lowers risk must stay open. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-26 |

## Liquidation and settlement

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-008` | Liquidation funding model | Liquidations are funded in GHO: the keeper's own GHO or a flash loan from the GHO FlashMinter (`Q-063`). LPVaultSwap has no Hub draw capacity. A settlement shortfall draws from the SettlementBudget within its caps (`Q-117`); if flash repayment still cannot be completed, the whole transaction reverts. Recorded Hub deficits are covered by the First-Loss Reserve (`Q-101`). Only approved keepers can call LPVaultSwap (`Q-053`). **Amended by `Q-213`:** any address may also liquidate through `liquidatePermissionless`, which never draws the SettlementBudget. **Amended by `Q-235`:** every liquidation still settles in GHO; a non-GHO debt is bought with flash-minted or caller GHO within `maxGhoSpend` before `liquidationCall`. **Superseded by `Q-334`:** liquidations are funded by a flash loan of the debt asset itself from the chain's Aave V3 Pool through `IFlashSource`, or by the caller's own debt asset when flash liquidity is short, and settle in that asset (`Q-333`). | Flash funding needs no idle protocol capital; with no draw capacity, a fault in LPVaultSwap cannot borrow from the Hub. | — |
| `Q-010` | Partial or whole-NFT settlement | Superseded by `Q-060`. Was: which v3 markets use partial seizure and which release the whole NFT. | — | — |
| `Q-016` | Keeper set management | Superseded by `Q-155`. Was: who adds and removes approved keepers and with what delay; keeper failure is `Q-082`. | — | — |
| `Q-022` | CoW Swap inside a liquidation | Superseded by `Q-062`. Was: how a CoW Swap order could settle inside the liquidation transaction; CoW Swap is not used. | — | — |
| `Q-023` | Whole-NFT settlement sizing | Superseded by `Q-060`. Was: how to size repayment when a whole NFT is released from an account holding other collateral. | — | — |
| `Q-034` | LPVaultSwap's use of LPOracle | LPVaultSwap uses LPOracle to compute the minimum GHO a liquidation must recover (`Q-085`, `Q-198`), to value in-kind settlement (`Q-133`) and to check price age and state before seizing (`Q-128`, `Q-182`). **Amended by `Q-333`:** the floor is the minimum amount of the repaid debt asset a liquidation must recover. | An oracle-based floor stops a keeper or route from selling seized collateral far below its value. | — |
| `Q-045` | Flash-loan callback checks | LPVaultSwap's flash-loan callback reverts unless `msg.sender` is the GHO FlashMinter and the initiator is LPVaultSwap itself. **Superseded by `Q-335`:** the flash source accepts a callback only from the Aave V3 Pool with itself as initiator, and LPVaultSwap accepts one only from the flash source inside an active settlement context. | Without both checks, anyone could call the callback directly or start a flash loan naming LPVaultSwap and run settlement steps outside a keeper liquidation. | — |
| `Q-053` | Keeper access | Only approved keepers can liquidate at launch: Avana Labs, at least one independent operator and an independent standby keeper (`Q-082`), never fewer than `keeperFloor` = 3. Letting any caller liquidate is a later governance decision after a track record. **Amended by `Q-213`:** approved keepers keep the full liquidation path, and any address may liquidate through the permissionless fallback (primary-then-backup routes only, no SettlementBudget draw, no in-kind settlement, no reimbursement). **Superseded by `Q-404`:** there is one entry point, `liquidate`, open to every address that is not frozen with both route modes; the keeper list is a registry of committed operators that gates no call. | Approved keepers are bound by the keeper agreement (liquidate every account below HF 1, never their own); permissionless callers could take only profitable accounts and leave small ones to become bad debt. | — |
| `Q-060` | Liquidation route | Partial seizure only, through the standard Spoke `liquidationCall` (`Q-116`): Aave sizes the repayment; Avana removes exactly the matching liquidity; the borrower keeps the rest of the NFT and its fees. There is no whole-NFT (repay and withdraw) route in v1, so PositionProxies carry no settlement authority. **Amended by `Q-235`:** the seizure is still partial and Aave-sized; for a non-GHO debt the debt asset is bought first and any unspent amount is sold back to GHO. **Amended by `Q-333`:** no debt asset is bought or sold back; LPVaultSwap flash-borrows the debt asset (`Q-334`). | Aave's own sizing keeps liquidation amounts identical to any Aave market; without the whole-NFT route, proxies need no settlement powers and no repayment sizing against other collateral is needed. | `Q-010`, `Q-023` |
| `Q-061` | Fees during liquidation | Principal-capped collection: after removing the seized liquidity, the LPVault collects at most the principal amounts that removal returned; accrued fees stay claimable by the depositor. Swap and flash-repayment checks use the amounts actually received. The depositor's fee claim survives removal of the last liquidity. **Amended by `Q-254`, `Q-271`:** a partial removal collects only the principal it removed, to the depositor of record; the received amount is measured by balance delta and may be below the principal for a fee-taking token. | Uniswap `collect()` returns principal and fees together; capping at principal keeps the borrower's fees out of liquidation proceeds (`Q-009`). | — |
| `Q-062` | Converting proceeds to GHO | Fixed on-chain routes per market and token, set at listing: the GHO Stability Module for USDC and USDT, Sky PSM then GSM for DAI and USDS, a fixed Uniswap or Curve route for other listed stablecoins; GHO needs no conversion. Each token has a primary and a backup route (`Q-084`, `Q-187`); route changes follow `Q-151`. Minimum GHO recovered is computed on-chain from the oracle (`Q-085`). CoW Swap is not used. **Amended by `Q-235`:** each non-GHO debt reserve also has fixed primary and backup buy routes from GHO to the debt asset. **Superseded by `Q-339`:** each pool token has a primary and an independent backup swap route into each approved debt asset of the Spoke, and the GSM is never a primary route (`Q-341`). | The flash loan must be repaid in the same transaction, and CoW orders settle later in solver batches. Routes fixed at listing cannot be replaced by a keeper-chosen swap route that extracts value. | `Q-022` |
| `Q-063` | Liquidation funding sources | The keeper may bring its own GHO, or LPVaultSwap flash-mints GHO from the GHO FlashMinter (callback checks `Q-045`, fee limit `Q-135`). **Amended by `Q-235`:** for a non-GHO debt the flash-minted or caller GHO buys the debt asset; keepers always fund in GHO. **Superseded by `Q-334`:** the caller brings its own debt asset or LPVaultSwap flash-borrows it from the Aave V3 Pool through `IFlashSource`; the GhoFlashMinter is not used. | Flash minting means keepers need no capital; keeper GHO keeps liquidations running when the FlashMinter is unavailable. | — |
| `Q-082` | Keeper failure | An independent standby keeper is approved on LPVaultSwap before launch, with separate signing, monitoring and submission infrastructure; it liquidates at the normal HF < 1 threshold when the primaries stall (`Q-147`). Liquidation stays keeper-only, with no health-factor level at which it becomes permissionless. **Amended by `Q-213`:** a permissionless fallback now lets any address liquidate at the normal HF < 1 threshold; the standby keeper and its separate infrastructure stay. **Amended by `Q-404`:** `liquidatePermissionless` is removed and `liquidate` is open to every address; the standby keeper, a committed operator on separate infrastructure, stays. | A keeper outage during a price drop is when bad debt builds; a standby on separate infrastructure removes the shared point of failure without admitting arbitrary callers. | — |
| `Q-084` | Route failure | Each market has a pre-approved backup conversion route per token. If both fail, in-kind settlement can be enabled (`Q-133`, `Q-163`): a keeper repaying with its own GHO takes the unwound tokens; Aave records any deficit normally. Listing caps are sized to GSM capacity, flash capacity and route depth (`depthThresholdGho`). **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-340`, `Q-341`:** in-kind settlement uses the keeper's own debt asset, and listing caps are sized to flash liquidity and one-sided route depth, not GSM capacity. **Amended by `Q-399`, `Q-401`:** in-kind settlement no longer exists; swap-route failures raise an alert, and a guardian or the timelock freezes new borrowing only once the approved routes are confirmed unable to settle valid liquidations at the required size. | A stablecoin facility outage must not stop liquidation; in-kind settlement lets keepers with their own GHO still clear debt. | — |
| `Q-085` | Liquidation recovery floor | No standalone spot-price check anywhere in the liquidation path. Once the seized vault-token amount is known, the whole unwind-and-convert must recover at least the recovery floor of `Q-198`. GHO actually recovered is measured; flash repayment is checked separately, so unused flash funds or prefunding cannot hide a poor recovery. **Amended by `Q-235`:** the floor and the flash-repayment check include the GHO spent on the buy leg. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-350`:** the floor is measured in the debt asset repaid, and there is still no spot-price check: the unwind runs with zero per-token minimums, value is checked at oracle prices after it, and every swap leg carries its oracle `minOut` (`REQ-SWAP-025`). | A spot check can be moved by pool manipulation to block liquidation; a floor on GHO recovered measures what matters, the value received for the collateral given up. | — |
| `Q-098` | Small liquidations | The minimum liquidation bonus follows `Q-179`. The keeper agreement requires submitting a liquidation for every account below HF 1 within an agreed number of blocks and never liquidating own or affiliated accounts; Avana reimburses loss-making liquidations from `keeperReimbursementBudget` (`Q-185`, `Q-197`). **Amended by `Q-251`:** the keeper agreement adds a minimum call size and the caller's shortfall top-up; reimbursement is sized per `Q-249`. | Aave's dust rule forces full liquidation of small debts, which can cost more gas than the bonus pays; reimbursement keeps keepers liquidating them before they become bad debt. | — |
| `Q-108` | Seizure order | Superseded by `Q-194`. Was: largest credited record first, with at most N NFTs per account per market. | — | — |
| `Q-125` | Liquidation debit check | Superseded by `Q-160`. Was: the LPVault checks the proxy's supplied vault tokens fell by exactly the units debited (the audit-test extension is kept in `Q-092`). | — | — |
| `Q-127` | Settlement budget scope | The SettlementBudget covers rounding gaps only, within its per-call and rolling 24-hour caps (`Q-167`), and never refunds keeper GHO. The live slippage bound of `Q-159` keeps the recovery floor alone sufficient for flash repayment. **Amended by `Q-236`:** caps are per debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-235`:** the slippage bound includes the buy leg. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-343`:** there is no buy leg, and the budget pays rounding gaps in the repaid debt asset within the market's window. **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed. | If slippage could exceed the bonus, every liquidation at the limit would need budget money and the budget would drain; with the bound, the budget only covers dust. | — |
| `Q-131` | Unwind minimums | Liquidity is removed with zero per-token minimums; the only floor is total GHO recovered across unwind and conversion (`Q-085`). | Per-token minimums fail when the pool's composition shifts even if total value is fine; one value floor accepts any composition that recovers enough GHO. | — |
| `Q-133` | In-kind settlement | Enabled per market only by the Guardian or Recovery (`Q-163`), never by a keeper, for a market whose routes are disabled or failing. The keeper repays from its own GHO; the unwound tokens, valued at the oracle price rounded up, must not exceed debt repaid × (1 + bonus); the keeper's share rounds down and the remaining tokens go to the borrower; no Avana fee and no SettlementBudget draw. **Amended by `Q-214`:** a remainder transfer to the borrower that fails goes to the PayoutEscrow for the borrower to claim, so the liquidation does not revert. **Amended by `Q-228`:** enablement is a short-delay timelock operation (`Q-228`). **Amended by `Q-235`:** for a non-GHO debt the keeper's GHO buys the debt asset first. **Amended by `Q-251`:** a market whose listing approved the trigger enables in-kind settlement automatically after reported recovery shortfalls persist for `inKindAutoDelay`. **Amended by `Q-333`:** the keeper repays with its own debt asset and no debt asset is bought. **Superseded by `Q-401`:** in-kind settlement is removed. | A keeper able to switch to in-kind could take tokens instead of converting them whenever routes are briefly worse; capping value at debt plus bonus keeps the borrower's loss equal to a normal liquidation. | — |
| `Q-135` | Flash fee | Liquidation reverts only if the FlashMinter fee exceeds `maxFlashFeeBps` (Structural change class, launch value 0); the fee is a settlement cost paid before Avana's share. **Superseded by `Q-335`:** LPVaultSwap checks the flash source's premium against `maxFlashFeeBps` before the loan and re-checks the premium charged in the callback. | A fee introduced by the FlashMinter's governance must not quietly reduce recovery; accepting any fee is a reviewed, delayed decision. | — |
| `Q-147` | Standby keeper trigger | The standby keeper is on the keeper list like the others; "primaries stalled" is defined by the keeper agreement and monitoring, not by an on-chain rule. | An on-chain stall detector would need its own trusted inputs; an operating agreement gives the same coverage without new attack surface. | — |
| `Q-151` | Route changes | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: route additions at `upgradeDelay`, immediate disabling by the multisig or risk reviewer, Guardian route suspension and Recovery restores. | — | — |
| `Q-159` | Slippage bound | Each liquidation reads Aave's actual bonus for the account, net of any liquidation fee, before calling, and uses effective slippage = min(`slippageAllowanceBps`, 1 − (1 + costs)/(1 + bonus)); in bps, `floor(10000·(B − 10000 − C)/B)` with B the bonus multiplier and C the costs, both in bps (`Q-190`). **Amended by `Q-235`:** costs include `buySlippageAllowanceBps` for a non-GHO debt. **Amended by `Q-261`:** listing bounds keep the effective slippage above zero at the minimum bonus, and a zero result allows no budget draw. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-317`:** the costs are the flash premium only; there is no buy leg and no `buySlippageAllowanceBps`. | Aave's bonus varies with health factor; bounding slippage by the live bonus keeps the recovery floor at or above debt repaid plus costs, so flash repayment needs budget money only for rounding. | `Q-127` (additive bound) |
| `Q-160` | Debit check | The LPVault snapshots the proxy's supplied shares itself under the Lock and requires the share drop to equal Aave's shares-to-liquidate and the vault tokens it pulls from LPVaultSwap, with Hub shares == assets (`Q-189`); otherwise the call reverts. **Amended by `Q-224`:** the LPVault no longer pulls or burns; Collateralization keeps the snapshot and requires the proxy's share drop and the token's supply drop between `beginSeizure` and `settleSeizure` to equal the debited units, after the Adapter burns them from LPVaultSwap. | Debiting by the LPVault's own measurement means a hostile LPVaultSwap cannot claim a larger seizure and release liquidity from another record. | `Q-125` |
| `Q-163` | In-kind enabling | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: in-kind settlement enabled at once by the Guardian or Recovery, lapsing after `inKindExpiry` unless Recovery extended it. **Superseded by `Q-401`:** in-kind settlement is removed. | — | — |
| `Q-165` | Liquidation burn | Superseded by `Q-224`. Was: LPVaultSwap transferring seized vault tokens to the LPVault, which burned them while debiting records. | — | — |
| `Q-170` | Seizure tie-break | Superseded by `Q-194`. Was: records ordered by credited units descending, ties by lowest record ID, with the order computed at the start of each debit. | — | — |
| `Q-179` | Minimum liquidation bonus | The risk service providers set Aave's bonus curve so the bonus just below HF 1, (maxLiquidationBonus − 1) × liquidationBonusFactor, is at least `minLiquidationBonus` (2%) and covers gas, conversion cost and Avana's share; market activation checks it on-chain. Worked examples use Aave V4's real bonus curve and dust rule. **Amended by `Q-393`:** `minLiquidationBonus` ≥ 2 % is Avana's recommended guidance for the listing AIP; the Aave DAO decides. | If the smallest bonus did not cover costs, liquidating marginal accounts would lose money and every such liquidation would depend on reimbursement. | — |
| `Q-185` | Keeper cost control | Aave target health factor for liquidation sizing (`aaveTargetHealthFactor`) 1.10, plus a funded keeper-reimbursement budget sized from the simulations (about $60k per 2M GHO at 20 gwei, more for gas spikes), set per `Q-197`. **Amended by `Q-249`, `Q-251`:** the reimbursement budget assumes keepers receive only loss-making liquidations, and keepers follow the minimum-size rule. **Amended by `Q-393`:** `aaveTargetHealthFactor` 1.10 is Avana's recommended guidance for the listing AIP; the Aave DAO decides. | A target above 1.0 restores a buffer with each liquidation, so an account is not liquidated again on the next small move, reducing gas-paying calls. | — |
| `Q-187` | Independent backup route | Each listed token has a backup conversion route to GHO that does not pass through the GSM or PSM of a pool token, with a published depth threshold (`depthThresholdGho`). **Amended by `Q-235`:** each non-GHO debt reserve also has an independent backup buy route. **Amended by `Q-273`:** a backup DEX route never trades through the market's own pool. **Amended by `Q-339`, `Q-342`:** each pool token has a primary and a backup route into each approved debt asset, and the two share no intermediate token or contract. | If both routes used the same stablecoin facility, one facility outage would disable both. | — |
| `Q-190` | Settlement formulas | surplus = max(0, recovered − debt − flash fee); budget draw = max(0, debt + fee on debt − recovered − keeper GHO), so oversizing a flash loan costs only the keeper. The Coverage Spoke calls `eliminateDeficit` only for Borrow Spokes on a DAO-set allowlist. The live slippage bound uses Aave's bonus net of any liquidation fee. Budget caps use a rolling 24-hour window in 1-hour buckets, never looser than a true rolling window. In-kind: oracle value rounds up, keeper share rounds down, the borrower gets the remainder, no Avana fee. **Amended by `Q-235`:** with `Gnet` the net GHO spent on the debt: surplus = max(0, recovered − Gnet − flash fee); budget draw = max(0, Gnet + fee − recovered − keeper GHO). **Amended by `Q-358`:** with `X = R − D − φD` in the repaid debt asset, `A + K + B = X` pays Avana's fee, the keeper reward and the borrower's surplus in that order; there is no `Gnet`. **Amended by `Q-397`:** there is no budget draw; a shortfall below the flash repayment is paid by the caller's shortfall top-up or the liquidation reverts. | Fixing each formula and its rounding direction makes the economics checkable and makes every rounding step favour the protocol and the borrower over the keeper. | — |
| `Q-194` | Records per market | No limit on pledged NFTs per account per market. The LPVault keeps each account-market's records in largest-first order at insertion (credited units descending, ties by lowest record ID; the pledger pays the insertion cost); seizure walks from the top and stops once the seized units are covered, so a liquidation touches only the records it debits; keepers size `debtToCover` per call to stay within gas. **Amended by `Q-240`:** Collateralization keeps the records and their order. **Amended by `Q-294`:** an account holds at most 16 open records per market; the largest-first order is unchanged. | Seizing largest records first touches the fewest NFTs, and keeping the order at insertion means a liquidation never sorts, so many small NFTs cannot make liquidation run out of gas. | `Q-108`, `Q-170` |
| `Q-198` | Recovery floor | Recovery floor = oracle value of the seized backing in GHO × (1 − effective slippage, `Q-159`) − one raw token unit per unwound token leg (valued in GHO) − `floorRoundingAllowanceGho` (1 GHO). The SettlementBudget covers any gap within its caps, so dust liquidations always settle and deficits get recorded. **Amended by `Q-235`:** for a non-GHO debt the floor also covers the GHO spent on the buy leg. **Amended by `Q-250`, `Q-251`:** every leg has an oracle-derived minimum, and the floor is tested on recovered GHO plus the caller's shortfall top-up. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-317`:** the floor is in the repaid debt asset with `floorRoundingAllowance(asset)`, and there is no buy leg. **Amended by `Q-397`:** no SettlementBudget covers the floor gap; the caller pays it through the shortfall top-up. | Uniswap's liquidity math rounds down by up to one raw unit per token; without the allowance, tiny liquidations would fail the floor and their bad debt would never be recorded. | — |
| `Q-201` | Reconcile on exit | When a proxy's Aave vault-token supply in a market is below its credited units (a seizure that was never settled), the LPVault treats the missing units as seized: on the borrower's next withdrawal or a liquidation settlement it debits those units from the records largest-first, moves the matching liquidity into a pending-settlement balance that keeps the outstanding vault tokens backed, and releases the rest of the NFT. A later LPVaultSwap `settlePending` call settles that balance against the tokens it holds (`Q-211`). **Amended by `Q-224`:** the Adapter burns the vault tokens that settle a pending balance. **Amended by `Q-240`:** Collateralization holds the records and the pending balance; the LPVault removes the liquidity. | A faulty or hostile LPVaultSwap that seized and never settled would otherwise leave the borrower's remaining NFTs stuck and vault tokens circulating without known backing. | — |
| `Q-213` | Permissionless liquidation fallback | Any address may liquidate through `LPVaultSwap.liquidatePermissionless`, a separate entry point beside the approved-keeper `liquidate`. It converts over the market's primary route, then its backup (the `PrimaryThenBackup` mode only); it never draws the SettlementBudget (a liquidation that would need a draw reverts with `InsufficientProceeds`), never settles in kind (with both routes failing it reverts with `NoRouteAvailable`, even while in-kind is enabled for the market) and earns no keeper reimbursement. The caller funds the repayment with its own GHO, a GHO flash mint or both, as keepers do (`Q-063`), and is paid by the keeper surplus rules: Avana's fee on realized surplus (`Q-097`), then the remainder to `msg.sender`. The recovery floor, the price-state and grace gates, Guardian liquidation pauses, the Hub share check, the deadline and the minimum payout (`Q-216`) and every other liquidation check apply unchanged; keeper suspension and quarantine restrict only the keeper path. Approved keepers keep every route mode, in-kind settlement, the SettlementBudget and reimbursement. The Aave Adapter still treats zero active keepers as a liquidation halt for new borrowing and withdraw-with-debt, because the permissionless path carries no duty to clear small or unprofitable accounts. **Amended by `Q-227`:** Guardian liquidation pauses, keeper suspension and quarantine no longer exist, and neither application pause state stops either entry point. **Amended by `Q-235`:** the fallback takes `debtReserveId` and `maxGhoSpend` like the keeper path. **Amended by `Q-251`:** the permissionless caller may also cover a shortfall with its own GHO. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-334`:** the caller funds the repayment with its own debt asset or a flash loan of it through `IFlashSource`, and is paid in that asset. **Superseded by `Q-404`:** `liquidatePermissionless` is removed; `liquidate(params)` is open to every address that is not frozen with `PrimaryThenBackup` or `BackupOnly`, every check applies to every caller, and the caller funds the call itself, directly or through the public flash liquidation helper. | Liquidation must not stop when no keeper acts. Avana already fixes the caller's economics (oracle recovery floor, fixed routes, payout to `msg.sender`), so the keeper gate protects only the SettlementBudget, the in-kind payout valued by the oracle alone, and the keeper agreement's duties; the permissionless path has neither of the first two, and keepers stay bound by the third. | — |
| `Q-214` | In-kind remainder escrow | In in-kind settlement LPVaultSwap sends the borrower's remainder of each pool token (`aᵢ − kᵢ`) to the borrower's wallet with a call limited to `payoutTransferGas` (100,000 gas) whose failure does not revert the liquidation. If the transfer reverts, returns `false` or runs out of that gas, LPVaultSwap deposits the amount in the immutable `PayoutEscrow`, credited to that borrower and token; the borrower claims it later to a recipient of its choosing. The escrow accepts deposits only from LPVaultSwap, has no admin, no sweep and no upgrade, and pays only the credited borrower. LPVaultSwap still ends every call with no balance change. **Amended by `Q-401`:** in-kind settlement is removed; the PayoutEscrow holds failed payout transfers only. | A borrower whose address a token blocks, or a borrower contract that reverts on receipt, could otherwise make every in-kind liquidation of its own account revert, during the route outage when in-kind is the only settlement path. The gas limit stops a token from consuming the liquidation's gas through the borrower transfer. | — |
| `Q-216` | Liquidation deadline and minimum payout | `ILPVaultSwap.LiquidationParams` and `PermissionlessLiquidationParams` carry `deadline` (last valid `block.timestamp`) and `minPayout` (GHO wei). `liquidate` and `liquidatePermissionless` revert with `DeadlineExpired(deadline)` as their first check if `block.timestamp > deadline`, and with `PayoutTooLow(keeperOut, minPayout)` after the waterfall if the caller's GHO payout is below `minPayout`. In `InKind` mode the keeper's share is fixed by the in-kind cap and paid in pool tokens, so `minPayout` must be 0 there (a non-zero value reverts with `InvalidAmount`). Both inputs can only make the call revert; neither changes `D`, `c`, `minGho`, `Bd`, `A`, a route or a recipient. The LPVault's internal Uniswap `decreaseLiquidity` deadline stays `block.timestamp`, because the outer call carries the caller's deadline. **Amended by `Q-235`:** both parameter structs also carry `debtReserveId` and `maxGhoSpend`. **Amended by `Q-333`:** `minPayout` is in the debt asset repaid, and `maxGhoSpend` is removed. **Amended by `Q-397`:** there is no budget draw `Bd`; a rounding gap is paid by the caller inside its shortfall top-up. | A liquidation transaction left pending, for example after a fallback to the public mempool, must not execute at a stale price or at a worse payout than its caller accepted; the protocol's own take is already protected at execution by the recovery floor. | — |
| `Q-232` | Liquidation preview | The immutable Lens returns ready-to-send liquidation parameters: `previewLiquidation(proxy, vaultToken, debtReserveId, deadline)` returns the `LiquidationParams` a caller can submit unchanged in the same block (account, market, debt reserve, the `debtToCover` Aave's sizing will consume, flash amount, `maxGhoSpend`, route mode, deadline, `minPayout` 0) together with every gate result, the expected units seized, the recovery floor and the expected payout. It sizes `debtToCover` with Aave V4's liquidation formulas at the pinned version (target health factor, dynamic bonus, dust rule) from on-chain reads. It is advisory: LPVaultSwap recomputes and enforces every check. **Amended by `Q-268`.** **Amended by `Q-319`:** the Lens is a periphery contract, and the preview sizes a flash loan of the debt asset with no `maxGhoSpend`. | A caller that builds parameters off-chain can drift from Aave's sizing and over-buy the debt asset or fail; one on-chain preview that matches execution, tested against fork execution, removes that source of failed or loss-making liquidations, including for permissionless callers without keeper tooling. | — |
| `Q-235` | Liquidation of non-GHO debt | Every liquidation settles in GHO. `LiquidationParams` and `PermissionlessLiquidationParams` gain `debtReserveId` (from the Spoke's approved debt set) and `maxGhoSpend`; keepers and permissionless callers always fund in GHO. For a GHO debt reserve the flow is unchanged and `maxGhoSpend` bounds the GHO repaid. For a non-GHO debt reserve, LPVaultSwap: flash-mints GHO or uses the caller's GHO; buys exactly `debtToCover` of the debt asset through the reserve's fixed primary buy route, then its backup, spending `G` GHO, and reverts `GhoSpendTooHigh` if `G` exceeds `maxGhoSpend` or the oracle bound `ceil(debtToCover × Pdebt ÷ Pgho × (1 + buySlippageAllowanceBps ÷ 10⁴))`; calls `Spoke.liquidationCall(collateralReserveId, debtReserveId, proxy, debtToCover, false)`, in which Aave pulls `D ≤ debtToCover`; sells any unspent debt asset back to GHO through that asset's sell route, giving the net GHO cost `Gnet`; has the Adapter burn the seized units and Collateralization debit and unwind (`Q-224`); converts proceeds to GHO; repays the flash principal and fee; and runs the waterfall with `S = max(0, R − Gnet − φ)`, Avana fee 20% of `S` and budget draw `max(0, Gnet + φ − R − K)` within caps. The effective slippage bound includes the buy leg: `(1 + bNet)(1 − s) ≥ (1 + flash fee)(1 + buySlippageAllowanceBps)`. Each call liquidates one collateral reserve against one debt reserve, as Aave's `liquidationCall` does. In-kind settlement of a non-GHO debt still buys the debt asset with the keeper's GHO. Amends `Q-008`, `Q-060`, `Q-062`, `Q-063`, `Q-085`, `Q-127`, `Q-133`, `Q-159`, `Q-187`, `Q-190`, `Q-198`, `Q-213`, `Q-216`. **Amended by `Q-250`, `Q-256`, `Q-261`:** the sell-back has a floor, every leg has an oracle-derived minimum, and the buy allowance is bounded below at listing. **Superseded by `Q-333`:** every liquidation settles in the debt asset of the reserve it repays, flash-borrowed through `IFlashSource`, with no GHO buy or sell-back leg. | Settling everything in GHO keeps one flash source, one recovery-floor unit and one budget; the buy leg is the only new step and it is bounded twice, by the caller's `maxGhoSpend` and by an oracle bound, so a sandwiched or thin buy cannot shift cost onto the SettlementBudget or the borrower. Selling back unspent debt asset makes a caller who over-sizes `debtToCover` pay for it instead of the budget, and putting the buy cost into the slippage bound keeps flash repayment needing budget money only for rounding. | — |
| `Q-250` | Oracle-derived minimum on every swap | Every swap on Avana's settlement paths runs with a minimum output derived from the oracle, never zero. Each sell-route leg converting a pool-token amount gets `minOut = max(1, ⌈Vleg × (10⁴ − sLeg) / 10⁴⌉ − Uleg − ⌊floorRoundingAllowanceGho / n⌋)`, with `Vleg` the amount's value in GHO at the LPOracle's capped leg price, `sLeg` the effective slippage of the call plus the caller's shortfall allowance (`Q-251`), `Uleg` one raw unit of the token in GHO and `n` the number of legs converted; the route passes it to the AMM or facility it calls (Uniswap `amountOutMinimum`, Curve `min_dy`, a balance check after a GSM or PSM call). The buy leg keeps `maxGhoIn = min(maxGhoSpend, bound)`; the sell-back of unspent debt asset keeps the floor of `Q-256`; pending settlement uses `s = min(slippageAllowanceBps, 30)`; repay with collateral uses `slippageAllowanceBps`. The aggregate recovery floor stays. Removing liquidity keeps zero minimums. Amends `Q-198`, `Q-211`, `Q-235`. **Amended by `Q-333`:** every leg's minimum is valued in the repaid debt asset with `floorRoundingAllowance(asset)`; the buy and sell-back legs no longer exist. | The aggregate floor protects lenders but left each leg open to sandwiching down to it: about the slippage allowance of the seized value per liquidation, taken from the caller's payout and Avana's fee, and on pending settlement taken from the reserve. A minimum on each leg from the oracle limits a searcher to the same allowance per leg. Removing liquidity needs no minimum, because at any pool price the tokens removed are worth at least the oracle value at oracle prices; moving the pool price before a removal shifts value to Avana, not from it. | — |
| `Q-251` | Caller shortfall top-up and automatic in-kind settlement | `LiquidationParams` and `PermissionlessLiquidationParams` gain `maxShortfallGho`, the caller's own GHO that may cover conversion below the recovery floor. The floor test becomes `R + shortfall ≥ minGho` with `shortfall = max(0, minGho − R) ≤ maxShortfallGho`, pulled from the caller in the same call; each leg's slippage is raised by at most `min(maxShortfallBps, ⌊maxShortfallGho × 10⁴ / V⌋)` (`maxShortfallBps` 1,000 at launch); the waterfall, Avana's fee and the budget draw use `R + shortfall`, so the SettlementBudget never pays a shortfall. The keeper agreement adds a minimum size: no call repays less than the smaller of the reserve's whole debt and `keeperMinRepay` ($500), and a floor failure is answered by a shortfall top-up within the reimbursement budget or a shortfall report, never by repeated halving. An approved keeper reports a shortfall with `reportRecoveryShortfall(params)`: LPVaultSwap runs the liquidation inside a call that always reverts with its result and records a floor failure for the market when `R < minGho`. When failures have been reported for at least `inKindAutoDelay` (6 hours), with no gap between reports over one hour and no successful routed liquidation of the market in between, and the market's trigger was approved (`setInKindAutoTrigger`, normal delay), the next report enables in-kind settlement for `inKindExpiry`. Manual enablement at the short delay stays. Amends `Q-098`, `Q-133`, `Q-185`, `Q-198`, `Q-213`. **Amended by `Q-333`:** the shortfall top-up `maxShortfall` is in the repaid debt asset. **Amended by `Q-395`, `Q-397`, `Q-401`:** `keeperMinRepay` and automatic in-kind settlement are removed; the shortfall top-up stays and also pays any rounding gap at final settlement. | In a crash the routes can run several percent below the oracle for hours. The borrower's loss is fixed by Aave's bonus whatever the conversion, so the floor then protects only the caller's surplus, Avana's fee and the budget; letting the caller pay the gap from its own GHO keeps liquidations running at full size instead of in many small calls whose gas exceeds the debt they repay, while the floor still guarantees the repayment is covered. A reverted liquidation leaves nothing on-chain, so persistence is shown by a call that runs the liquidation and reverts with its result; hours of such evidence and a trigger approved at listing let in-kind settlement open without a fresh operation, and opening it only lets approved keepers settle within the in-kind cap. | — |
| `Q-256` | Avana fee from oracle value and a sell-back floor | Avana's fee on a routed liquidation is `A = min(⌊max(0, V − Gcov − φD) × avanaFeeShareBps / 10⁴⌋, max(0, R' − Gcov − φD))`: computed from the oracle value `V` of the seized units and never above the realized gain over the covered debt cost `Gcov`, so it never makes a liquidation without an oversized buy-and-sell-back round trip revert, and a caller cannot lower it by raising `Gnet` with a sell-back below value. The sell-back of unspent debt asset must return at least `⌈amount × Pdebt × 10¹⁸ / (10^dd × Pgho)⌉ × (10⁴ − buySlippageAllowanceBps) / 10⁴ − 1` GHO, else `RecoveryBelowFloor`. Amends `Q-097`, `Q-231`, `Q-235`. **Amended by `Q-358`:** with no buy leg the covered cost is the repaid debt `D`, so `A = min(⌊max(0, V − D − φD) × avanaFeeShareBps / 10⁴⌋, max(0, X))`, and the sell-back floor no longer applies. | Measured on realized proceeds, the fee shrank whenever the caller converted badly, so a caller sandwiching its own conversion, or oversizing `debtToCover` and selling the leftover back below value, kept Avana's share. Measured on oracle value, routing choices no longer move the fee while the surplus covers it, and the sell-back, the one swap with no floor, now has one. | — |
| `Q-261` | Two-leg liquidation liveness | Each non-GHO debt reserve stores `buySlippageAllowanceMinBps`, set in its listing to at least the larger of its two buy routes' fee plus price impact at `buyRouteDepthThresholdGho`, and changed only at the normal delay to a value not above the current allowance; `tightenBuySlippageAllowanceBps` reverts `LivenessBound` below it. `setBuySlippageAllowanceBps`, `approveDebtReserve` and `activateMarket` revert `LivenessBound` unless, for every market on the Spoke, `minLB × (10⁴ − slippageAllowanceMinBps) × 10⁴ ≥ 10⁴ × (10⁴ + maxFlashFeeBps) × (10⁴ + buySlippageAllowanceBps)`, with `minLB` the minimum liquidation bonus multiplier in bps. If a liquidation still computes an effective slippage of 0, the SettlementBudget pays nothing: a call that would need a draw reverts `InsufficientProceeds`.
