Amends `Q-159`, `Q-210`, `Q-235`. **Superseded by `Q-317`:** liquidation liveness is the single inequality `minLB × (10⁴ − slippageAllowanceMinBps) ≥ 10⁴ × (10⁴ + maxFlashFeeBps)` per (market, debt reserve), since there is no buy leg. | A buy allowance below the routes' real cost makes every buy revert, and one at or above the minimum bonus leaves no room for conversion loss; either stops liquidation of every account holding that debt while borrowing it continues. Bounding the allowance from both sides keeps the two-leg inequality satisfiable for every bonus Aave can pay, and refusing draws when it is not keeps the budget to rounding gaps. | — |
| `Q-271` | Amounts measured by balance delta | Every amount Avana receives (pool tokens from `decreaseLiquidity` and `collect`, route inputs and outputs, debt asset bought or sold back, funds a proxy receives for a repay) is measured by balance delta, and every later step uses the measured amount. The principal-capped collect requires the received amount to be at most the principal removed rather than equal to it. An issuer pause or a USDT fee switch trips the issuer breaker (`Q-241`); an issuer blocklisting LPVaultSwap, the LPVault or Collateralization is handled by the token-issuer playbook (`Q-243`), which ends in a migration of the affected markets. Amends `Q-061`, `Q-124`. **Amended by `Q-342`:** an issuer blocklisting LPVaultSwap is answered by an LPVaultSwap upgrade at the same address plus in-kind settlement for the markets holding that token. **Amended for Aave V4's repayment check:** balance-delta measurement keeps settlement of a fee-charging pool token working, but a debt asset with a transfer fee cannot be repaid on Aave V4 at all: the Hub's `add`, `restore` and `reclaim` require its balance to cover the amount booked and revert `InsufficientTransferred`, so liquidation, repay, repay with collateral and pending settlement into that asset revert until the fee returns to 0, for every Aave user of it; Avana adds no path around that check (2.11 `REQ-SWAP-123`). | Tether can switch on USDT's transfer fee at any time; with exact-amount checks every liquidation of a USDT market would have reverted. Using what actually arrived keeps settlement of USDT as a pool token working at the reduced amounts, the oracle-based floors still compare against full value, and the breaker stops new exposure to the token. Where USDT is the debt asset, Aave's own accounting refuses a short repayment, and a side path would bypass it. | — |
| `Q-273` | Backup routes avoid the market's own pool | A market's backup DEX route never trades through the market's own Uniswap v3 pool. Amends `Q-187`. **Amended by `Q-339`:** the rule holds for every AMM protocol: a `DexPath` route never trades through the market's own pool. | Each seizure removes Avana's liquidity from the market's own pool, so a route through that pool would see less depth after every liquidation and, in a crash, each liquidation would make the next one fail. | — |
| `Q-289` | Keepers per chain | Each chain's LPVaultSwap keeps its own keeper list and its own `keeperFloor` (3 active keepers); the floor is checked per chain, and every Hub of the chain halts new borrowing when the chain's active keepers fall to zero. The standby keeper is funded on each chain, in each debt asset of that chain, before any market there is activated. Amends `Q-053`, `Q-139`. **Amended by `Q-400`:** `keeperFloor` is 1 (Avana's own keeper); new borrowing halts only when 0 active keepers remain. | One LPVaultSwap per chain serves every Hub there, so the keeper floor is a per-chain property; a keeper holding no Base funds cannot liquidate Base debt. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-25 |
| `Q-316` | Keeper count is an O(1) counter | LPVaultSwap keeps `activeKeeperCount()` as a per-chain counter updated by `addKeeper`, `removeKeeper` and the keeper freeze. `addKeeper` reverts above `MAX_KEEPERS = 32` per chain. The RiskModule reads the counter in one slot. | Counting unfrozen keepers by iterating was O(K) cross-contract reads on every borrow pre-check. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-12 |
| `Q-333` | Settlement asset | Every liquidation, repay with collateral and pending settlement settles in the debt asset of the reserve it repays (or, for a pending settlement, the market's pending-settlement reserve). Caller funds, flash principal, recovery, the recovery floor, the SettlementBudget draw, Avana's fee, the keeper reward and the borrower surplus are all amounts of that asset. GHO is used only when the debt is GHO. Supersedes `Q-235`. | Removes the buy-debt-with-GHO leg and the sell-back leg, so no liquidation needs GHO depth on a chain where GHO is thin (Base holds about 0.13M idle GHO in the V3 Pool). One unit for floor, fee and payout per call. | `Q-235`. Resolves AMMN-FLOW-02, AMMN-INT-05, AMMN-FLOW-14 |
| `Q-337` | Payout order and borrower surplus | After the flash principal and premium are repaid, the leftover debt asset is paid in this order: Avana's fee `A` (20% of the oracle gain, capped by the realized surplus), the keeper reward (the realized surplus up to the oracle gain, less `A`), then the borrower surplus (realized recovery above the oracle value of the seizure plus the debt's flash premium) to the borrower's wallet, falling back to the PayoutEscrow. Conservation is re-proved with the borrower term. Amends `Q-097`, `Q-190`. | The waterfall had no borrower surplus. Value recovered above what Aave priced the seizure at belongs to the borrower whose collateral produced it; the keeper's reward stays bounded by the bonus Aave paid. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-23 |
| `Q-338` | Pending-settlement destination | Each market records a pending-settlement debt reserve (at launch the Spoke's GHO reserve on Ethereum and its USDC reserve on Base). `settlePending` converts the released tokens to that reserve's asset and funds the Coverage Spoke of the market's (Hub, that asset). Amends `Q-211`, `Q-236`. | Pending settlement always funded the GHO Coverage Spoke, whatever the market's Hub and assets. Coverage is one Spoke per (Hub, debt asset), so the recovered value goes to the layer that covers that Hub's losses. | Resolves the pending-settlement destination finding |
| `Q-339` | Swap routes for every token class | Each listed pool token has a primary and an independent backup swap route into each approved debt asset of the Spoke. Stable tokens may use issuer facilities; Correlated and Volatile tokens use `DexPath` routes through deep pools that are not the market's pool. A token whose route to the debt asset is the identity (the token is the debt asset) needs no route. Supersedes `Q-062`; amends `Q-058`, `Q-187`, `Q-273`. | Routes existed only for stablecoins into GHO, so Correlated and Volatile markets could neither list nor liquidate at size. | `Q-062`. Resolves AMMN-FLOW-14 |
| `Q-340` | One-sided route stress | Listing and the route-depth monitor stress each token's routes at the full single-token value of the largest position the caps allow at each range edge, not at half. Amends `Q-084`. | A concentrated position at or beyond its range edge is 100% one token; two half legs understate constant-product slippage by about half (4.76% against 9.09% at depth 10V). | Resolves AMMN-RT-09 |
| `Q-341` | GSM routes | The GSM is never a primary route. A `GsmStatic` route may fill a backup slot of a GHO-debt market on Ethereum only, and `setRoute`/`restoreRoute` require the GSM to be unseized, unfrozen and with a non-zero exposure cap. The seized GSM addresses are removed from the spec. Amends `Q-058`, `Q-084`. | The GSM addresses previously named are seized with a cap of 0, and the live USDC GSM holds about $0.03 of USDC to buy. | Resolves AMMN-INT-02, AMMN-INT-03 |
| `Q-342` | Route token failure isolation | The primary and backup routes of one slot share no intermediate token as well as no contract. Routes approve with `forceApprove` and reset to 0. Tokens with an ERC-1820 `ERC777Token` implementer are not listed. A token issuer blocklisting LPVaultSwap is answered by an LPVaultSwap upgrade at the same address plus in-kind settlement for the markets holding that token. Amends `Q-187`, `Q-271`. | A pause of a shared intermediate (USDC) stopped every route that crossed it, including markets that hold no USDC. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-08 |
| `Q-343` | SettlementBudget per market | The SettlementBudget keeps one balance per asset and a rolling 24-hour window per market in that market's settlement asset, plus a per-call cap per (CDP Spoke, debt reserve) and a total window per asset. The sum of market window caps per asset is at most that asset's total window cap, checked when a cap is raised. Window totals are kept as running sums with expiring buckets. Amends `Q-117`, `Q-167`, `Q-236`, `Q-267`. **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed. | A shared window let one market's liquidations starve another's. Running sums replace 75 cold bucket reads per draw. | Resolves AMMN-RT-13, AMMN-GAS-10 |
| `Q-344` | Liquidation throughput | The Lock does not serialize liquidations. Throughput is bounded by block gas, the per-transaction gas cap, swap-route depth and flash liquidity. `listCollateralMarket` sizes each market's cap so that its liquidatable volume in a 20% drop clears within `liquidationPriceMaxAge` at the 95th-percentile keeper gas share, using `SPEC-LIQ-021`. | The Lock is transient and released at the end of each call, so parallel transactions never contend for it. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-07 |
| `Q-345` | Front-run tolerance | Keepers size `debtToCover` as a full close or at least `LENS_DUST_MARGIN_BPS` below the dust boundary, set `minPayout` at least 1% below the previewed payout, and use private order flow on Ethereum. LPVaultSwap measures `D` and `c` by delta, so a 1-wei repay changes the amounts, not the outcome. **Amended by `Q-404`:** `keeperOut` includes the returned funding, so the 1 % margin applies to the net payout: `minPayout ≤ X + ⌊0.99 × (keeperOut_preview − X)⌋`; through a flash liquidation helper the request's `minPayout` bounds the net payout after the loan and premium. | A 1-wei repay or a repay that leaves the debt just under Aave's dust threshold otherwise reverts a keeper's call every block. | Resolves AMMN-GAS-11 |
| `Q-348` | One LPVaultSwap per chain | One LPVaultSwap per chain serves every Hub, CDP Spoke and market of that chain. Its keeper list, flash source and fixed address are per chain; every cap, budget window, Coverage Spoke and route it uses is keyed by the market's Hub, Spoke, market or debt reserve. | A per-Hub LPVaultSwap gave no smaller blast radius and multiplied keeper sets and Lock members. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-03, AMMN-ARCH-04 |
| `Q-350` | No pool spot gate before a liquidation unwind | There is no pool spot gate: LPVaultSwap never compares the pool's spot price with the oracle reference before or during a liquidation, and a moved pool price never reverts a liquidation or switches its settlement mode. The seizure unwind asks the LPVault to remove the seized units with zero per-token minimums, checks after the unwind that the tokens received are worth at least the recovery floor at oracle prices, and protects each swap with its per-leg oracle `minOut` ([2.11](../02-contracts/11-lpvaultswap.md) `REQ-SWAP-025`). In-kind settlement stays a separate, governance- or trigger-enabled path driven by recovery shortfalls, not by spot price. A pool-depth breaker (`POOL_DEPTH`) trips when the pool's in-range liquidity over `twapWindow` falls below the market's listing depth minimum; it stops new activity and never liquidation. An earlier draft of this decision gated the unwind on a spot band with a `PoolSpotOff` revert; that gate is withdrawn. Amends `Q-085`. **Amended by `Q-401`:** in-kind settlement no longer exists. | Fair composition values a holding at the minimum of its oracle value over every spot price the pool can take, so removing the liquidity at any spot returns tokens worth at least the fair value at oracle prices. Moving the spot therefore cannot push the recovered mix below the floor that fair composition already priced in, and a spot gate would only give an attacker who moves the pool a way to block liquidation. Example: a USDC/USDT full-range position worth $10,000 at oracle prices returns about 5,000 of each token at the oracle ratio; after a swap pushes the pool to 2 USDT per USDC it returns about 3,536 USDC and 7,071 USDT, worth $10,607 at oracle prices, which is above $10,000. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-09 |
| `Q-358` | Liquidation flash leg and payout in the debt asset | LPVaultSwap flash-borrows the liquidation's debt asset through the chain's `IFlashSource`, implemented with the Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` (one asset, `interestRateModes = [0]`, Avana holding the `FLASH_BORROWER` role so the premium is 0; otherwise the live `FLASHLOAN_PREMIUM_TOTAL`). Before the loan the source checks the reserve's idle balance (`FlashLiquidityShort`) and the premium against `maxFlashFeeBps` (`FlashFeeTooHigh`); in the callback it checks the Pool as caller, itself as initiator, one asset, and LPVaultSwap's active settlement context. The flash source may never be the Hub of the CDP Spoke being liquidated. After the 5-argument `liquidationCall` with `receiveShares = false`, LPVaultSwap burns the seized vault tokens, the LPVault unwinds exactly the seized units into n tokens, the swap routes convert each token to the debt asset under per-leg oracle `minOut`, the loan plus premium is repaid, and the remainder `X = R − D − φD` (recovered `R`, repaid debt `D`, premium `φD`, all in debt-asset base units) is paid in the debt asset in this order: Avana's fee `A = min(⌊max(0, V − D − φD) × avanaFeeShareBps / 10⁴⌋, max(0, X))` (`V` the seized collateral's oracle value in the debt asset, `avanaFeeShareBps` = 2,000), then the keeper reward `K = min(X − A, max(0, V − D − φD) − A)`, then the borrower's surplus `B = X − A − K`, sent to the account's wallet or to the PayoutEscrow if the transfer fails. `A + K + B = X` exactly, with floor rounding on `A` so dust stays with the borrower. When flash liquidity is short or the source is paused, the caller funds `debtToCover` itself (caller-funded path) and is repaid from the same waterfall; the timelock can replace the flash-source implementation. Pending settlement sends recovered value to the Coverage Spoke of the market's (Hub, debt asset). Amends `Q-190`, `Q-256`. | Base has no GHO FlashMinter or GSM, and Aave V4 has no flash loans; the Aave V3 Pool holds deep debt-asset liquidity on both chains. Borrowing the debt asset itself removes the buy leg and its second slippage, and paying out in the repaid asset keeps the waterfall in one unit. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-02, AMMN-ARCH-04 (flow level), AMMN-FLOW-23 |
| `Q-389` | Failed payout transfers go to the PayoutEscrow | Every payout LPVaultSwap makes (Avana's fee to `feeRecipient`, the caller's payout and route residues, in-kind keeper shares and refunds, the borrower surplus and in-kind remainders, the `settlePending` residue) uses the gas-limited delivery of `REQ-SWAP-076`. A transfer that reverts, returns `false` or runs out of gas never reverts the liquidation or the pending settlement: the amount is deposited in the PayoutEscrow credited to that recipient, who claims it later to any address. Only a revert of the escrow deposit itself reverts the call. `deleverage` keeps its plain transfer to the borrower who called it. **Amended by `Q-401`:** in-kind shares and remainders no longer exist; the PayoutEscrow holds the failed payout transfers that remain (borrower surplus, Avana's fee, keeper transfers). | If an issuer blocklisted the treasury Safe, the unguarded fee transfer reverted every routed liquidation with a surplus and every `settlePending` residue in that asset chain-wide until a normal-delay `setFeeRecipient` executed. Escrowing any failed payout, as was already done for borrower payouts, removes every recipient as a way to stop liquidation. Rules: `REQ-SWAP-024`, `-026`, `-028`, `-030`, `-043`, `-064`, `-066`, `-076`, `-082`, `REQ-ESCROW-002`, `-005`, `-018`. Checked by FV-LIQ-05 (`05aa`, mutant `05am`). | Resolves new attack N2a |
| `Q-390` | Balance-delta flash path and transfer fees on a debt asset | Every amount LPVaultSwap receives is measured by balance delta (`Kr`, `Fr`, `D`, `c`, released tokens, route output, shortfall, budget draw). The flash source forwards its whole measured receipt, and LPVaultSwap repays it by topping its balance up to `owed = amount + premium` with at most `MAX_REPAY_TRANSFERS = 4` transfers measured by delta (`P`), reverting `FlashRepayShort` otherwise; four transfers always suffice for USDT's fee (`ρ ≤ 19` bps, `maxFee < 50` USDT). With a fee observed, `debtToCover = min(X, Kr + Fr)`, `Bd = 0`, and the fee cost `τ = (F − Fr) + (P − F − φ)` falls on the caller's payout, never on Avana's fee, the borrower surplus or the budget. A debt asset is enabled only with a zero transfer fee (`enableDebtReserve` reverts `TransferFeeActive`); a fee switched on later trips the issuer breaker (`SPEC-BRK-003`), which stops new borrows of that asset. **Amended for Aave V4's repayment check:** while that fee is on, Aave V4's Hub refuses every fee-reduced repayment of the asset (`InsufficientTransferred`), so liquidations, repays and `deleverage` of it revert until the fee returns to 0 and resume in the next block after; the balance-delta flash path and measured funding stay as defence in depth, and the fee-cost formula `τ` describes measured amounts, not a repayment Aave accepts (2.11 `REQ-SWAP-123`, TEST-048). | The flash source transferred a fixed `amounts[0]` it no longer held once USDT charged a fee, and required a balance LPVaultSwap's fixed repayment could not reach. Forwarding measured amounts and topping up by delta means no Avana contract transfers or books an amount it does not hold; a fee on the debt asset itself is then stopped by Aave's own accounting, which Avana does not route around. Rules: `REQ-FLASH-003`, `REQ-SWAP-011`, `-013`, `-108`, `-122`, `-123`, `-124`, `SPEC-LIQ-010`. Checked by MATH-ST-111, MATH-ST-112. | Resolves new attack N2b |
| `Q-379` | Liquidation and settlement interfaces in the debt asset | LPVaultSwap implements `IFlashReceiver.onFlashLoan(asset, amount, premium, context)`; the chain's `IFlashSource` (Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan`, its own Aave callback) lends the debt asset itself after checking liquidity, `maxFlashFeeBps` and that its lender is not the liquidated Spoke's Hub. LPVaultSwap calls Aave's standard 5-argument `liquidationCall(collateralReserveId, debtReserveId, user, debtToCover, receiveShares)` with `receiveShares = false`, unwinds n tokens, swaps each into the debt asset over swap routes keyed `(marketId, token, debtAsset, kind)`, and pays Avana's fee, the keeper reward and the borrower's surplus in the debt asset. GHO's flash minter, the GSM as a primary route, buy routes, sell-back routes and `IDebtBuyRoute` are removed; `ISettlementRoute.convert` becomes `swap` and routes declare `supportsTokenClass`. `settlePending(marketId, debtReserveId)` funds the Coverage Spoke of the reserve's (funding Hub, asset). `uniswapV3SwapCallback` stays on swap routes as plumbing for `DexPath` hops on Uniswap v3 and Slipstream pools; LPVaultSwap never swaps on a pool directly. The flash source is replaceable through the timelock (`setFlashSource`); with short flash liquidity the caller funds the debt. | Base has no GHO FlashMinter, GSM or deep GHO, and Aave V4 has no flash loans. Flash-borrowing the debt asset removes the buy and sell-back legs and their slippage, and paying out in the debt asset keeps GHO only where the debt is GHO. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-02, AMMN-INT-05, AMMN-INT-02, AMMN-INT-03, AMMN-ARCH-04, AMMN-FLOW-14, AMMN-RT-09, AMMN-FLOW-23 |
| `Q-380` | Per-market budget windows, per-chain minimum debt, 16-record limit | SettlementBudget windows are per (market, debt asset) inside a per-asset total, with the per-call caps of `Q-343`; draws name the market and the debt asset. `floorRoundingAllowance(asset)` replaces the GHO-wei allowance. The per-chain ParameterStore holds `minDebtUsd` (enforced by `preBorrowCheck`, `BelowMinDebt`), the sequencer feed and grace period, block time, liquidation gas and the per-feed `heartbeatMargin`. `minDebtUsd` follows `SPEC-CHAIN-001` (`Q-287`): $2,000 on Ethereum and $50 on Base at launch. Records per account per market are capped at `AmmConstants.MAX_RECORDS_PER_ACCOUNT_MARKET = 16` (`TooManyRecords`). **Superseded by `Q-381` on minimum debt:** the per-chain ParameterStore holds no `minDebtUsd`, and the 16-record cap rests on the per-transaction gas limit. **Amended by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget windows are removed; the minimum-debt and 16-record rules stay. | A shared window lets one market starve another; uncapped records make an account unliquidatable (about 31M gas for 125 records); small debt is unprofitable to liquidate on Ethereum; stablecoin feeds have 24-hour heartbeats, so freshness must be per feed. | Resolves AMMN-RT-13, AMMN-GAS-01, AMMN-GAS-02, AMMN-INT-01, AMMN-FLOW-03, AMMN-INT-06 |
| `Q-395` | No keeper minimum repayment | `keeperMinRepay` is removed. Avana's keepers liquidate whatever Aave allows per call; a recovery-floor failure is answered by the caller's shortfall top-up. Amends `Q-251`. | Aave's close factor and dust rule already set the size of each call. A keeper-side minimum was an off-chain rule with no on-chain effect that could leave a small account waiting. | — |
| `Q-397` | SettlementBudget removed | The SettlementBudget contract is removed with all of its parameters (`perCallCap`, `reservedFloor`, the market and asset windows, `PER_CALL_CAP_MAX_USD`, `BUCKET`) and every draw. Aave V4's dust rule can raise the repayment needed to finish a liquidation, so the caller (Avana's keeper) sizes `debtToCover` and its funding for that amount up front, accounting for the exact flash principal and premium. Any rounding gap at final settlement is paid by the caller through the existing shortfall top-up (`Q-251`). `floorRoundingAllowance` stays as the fixed allowance in the recovery floor, at most $1 per asset. Supersedes `Q-117`, `Q-127`, `Q-167`, `Q-267`, `Q-343`, `Q-387`; amends `Q-190`, `Q-198`, `Q-236`, `Q-278`, `Q-279`, `Q-361`, `Q-380`. | The budget paid rounding gaps of a few base units. It needed its own funding, windows, reserved floors, a breaker and a solvency term, and each was a liveness or starvation surface (AMMN-RT-13). The keeper already funds the liquidation and can size it exactly, so the caller pays the gap and no shared pool is needed. | AMMN-RT-13 |
| `Q-401` | In-kind settlement removed; escrow for failed payouts | Removed: automatic and governance in-kind settlement (`enableInKind`, `disableInKind`, `autoEnableInKind`, `setInKindAutoTrigger`), `reportRecoveryShortfall`, `probeLiquidation` where it served only in-kind, `inKindAutoDelay`, `inKindAutoTrigger`, `inKindExpiry` and the in-kind payout split. The caller shortfall top-up stays. The escrow contract stays, named `PayoutEscrow`, with one job: payout escrow for borrower surplus, Avana fee and keeper transfers that a token refuses. When no approved swap route can settle, a liquidation proceeds once a route is restored, a standby route is switched in, or a caller funds the shortfall. Supersedes `Q-133`, `Q-163`; amends `Q-084`, `Q-214`, `Q-251`, `Q-350`, `Q-389`. | In-kind settlement needed a trigger, an expiry, a split and a privileged enabling path, each its own surface, for the case where every approved route fails at once. The route-depth listing rule, the standby routes, the shortfall top-up and the Avana Umbrella already cover that case. | — |
| `Q-404` | Caller-funded liquidation and one entry point | Two decisions of 2026-11-10. (1) Funding: `LPVaultSwap.liquidate` is funded by its caller only. It pulls `X = debtToCover` from `msg.sender`, measures what arrived as `Kf` with `0 < Kf ≤ X`, and passes `Kf` to `liquidationCall`; it calls no flash source and measures no flash loan, so the flash principal, premium and transfer-fee terms leave every liquidation formula (`SPEC-LIQ-002`, `SPEC-LIQ-007`, `SPEC-LIQ-015`, `SPEC-LIQ-016`, `SPEC-SET-001`: `g = max(0, D + A + B − Kf − R)`, which never exceeds the floor gap). The caller's payout `keeperOut = Kf + R' − D − A − B` includes its unused funding and the recovered principal, is never negative, and is paid directly to `msg.sender` or the call reverts, never escrowed; Avana's fee, the borrower surplus, residues and dust legs keep the escrow fallback. A caller without the debt asset uses the flash liquidation helper: a public, immutable periphery contract outside the core that flash-borrows from the chain's Aave V3 Pool with `flashLoan` (no premium once it holds `FLASH_BORROWER`), binds its callback to the Pool, the initiator, the asset, the amount, the caller's premium bound and a single-use context, calls `liquidate`, repays from the payout with a bounded top-up, enforces a net `minPayout` and pays its caller after `flashLoan` returns; operators may deploy a variant bound to their signer and funding Safe (`REQ-HELPER-001` to `REQ-HELPER-014`). The core flash source serves `deleverage` only, which keeps its premium. (2) One path: `liquidatePermissionless` is removed; `liquidate(params)` is open to every address that is not frozen, with `PrimaryThenBackup` or `BackupOnly`, and every check applies to every caller (health factor, oracle poke and grace, Hub share check, recovery floor, fee, surplus, deadline, `minPayout`, the freeze on the caller). The keeper list becomes a registry of committed operators (Avana's keeper at launch, others added at the normal delay) that gates no call and feeds `keeperFloor` = 1, the borrowing halt at zero active committed operators and monitoring. An address freeze binds `msg.sender` only, so it cannot stop a caller that goes through the public helper. Supersedes the keeper/permissionless split of `Q-053`, `Q-082` and `Q-213`; amends `Q-219`, `Q-345`, `Q-400`. | A flash loan inside the core put a callback, its context binding, a repayment loop and premium and transfer-fee terms into the audited liquidation path, and made Avana's formulas carry costs the caller chose; with the caller funding, Aave pulls the debt out of the caller's own funds, so no gap can fall on Avana and the formulas lose three terms. The helper keeps flash funding available to everyone without a key or a balance to protect. A second, restricted entry point protected nothing once every check runs for every caller, and one path means one set of code, tests and monitoring; the committed-operator registry keeps the duty to liquidate unprofitable accounts visible and tied to the borrowing halt. | Folds in the flash source review and the external review of 2026-11-10 |

## Flash liquidity

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-334` | Flash source | LPVaultSwap funds liquidations and repays with collateral through one `IFlashSource` per chain. Its only implementation is `AaveV3FlashSource`, which calls the chain's Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` with one asset, mode 0, borrowing the debt asset itself. GHO's flash minter is not used on any chain. Supersedes `Q-008`, `Q-063`. **Amended by `Q-404`:** the flash source serves `deleverage` only; a liquidation is funded by its caller, and a caller without the debt asset borrows through the flash liquidation helper, which calls the same Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` directly and needs its own `FLASH_BORROWER` grant. | Aave V4 has no flash loan. The V3 Pool holds deep idle liquidity of the launch debt assets on Ethereum and Base, and `flashLoan` (not `flashLoanSimple`) honours the `FLASH_BORROWER` zero-premium role. | `Q-008`, `Q-063`. Resolves AMMN-FLOW-02, AMMN-INT-05, AMMN-ARCH-04 |
| `Q-335` | Flash checks | Before the loan LPVaultSwap requires `F ≤ maxFlashLoan(asset)` and `flashFee(asset, F) × 10⁴ ≤ maxFlashFeeBps × F`. In the callback the source requires `msg.sender == POOL`, `initiator == address(this)`, one asset and an active context; LPVaultSwap requires `msg.sender == flashSource`, an active context, matching asset and amount, and re-checks the premium charged. The flash source's lender must differ from the CDP Spoke's home Hub and from the debt reserve's funding Hub. Supersedes `Q-045`, `Q-135`. **Amended by `Q-404`:** these checks run in `deleverage`; the flash liquidation helper runs the same premium and liquidity checks for its own loan and binds its callback to the Pool, the initiator, the asset, the amount, the caller's premium bound and a single-use context. | A single external source fails under stress (pause, flash disabled, drain, fee raise); checking liquidity and fee before the loan turns each of these into a clean revert, after which the caller-funded path runs. Authenticating both hops of the callback stops a third party from driving the settlement core. Borrowing from the Hub being repaid would tie the funding to the reserve whose solvency the liquidation restores. | `Q-045`, `Q-135`. Resolves AMMN-ARCH-04 |
| `Q-386` | Flash source never lends a Hub's liquidity | The `IFlashSource` implementation reports, through `LENDER()`, the pool or Hub that holds the liquidity it lends: the Aave V3 Pool at launch, and for a flash Spoke on an Aave V4 Hub that Hub, not the Spoke. `setFlashSource` reverts `InvalidRoute` when `LENDER()` equals any Hub registered on the chain, and LPVaultSwap repeats the test at every flash loan, so a Hub registered after the source was set is caught at the call; `enableDebtReserve` applies the same test. Same rule as `Q-388`, which states it for LPVaultSwap and the flash source. | An address comparison with one Hub missed a source whose Spoke draws from that Hub, and it compared different identities (the liquidated Spoke's home Hub on one side, the reserve's funding Hub on the other). Comparing the reported lender with every registered Hub closes both gaps. | Amends `Q-336`, `Q-379`. Resolves N1 of the final re-check (AMMN-ARCH-04) |
| `Q-388` | Flash source must not draw from any recorded Hub | `IFlashSource.LENDER()` reports the pool or Hub whose liquidity the loan draws, never an intermediate contract (for a source that borrows through a V4 Spoke, the Hub behind that Spoke). LPVaultSwap reverts `FlashSourceIsHub()` on every flash-funded call if `MarketRegistry.isRecordedHub(flashSource.LENDER())`, and `setFlashSource` refuses such a source; `recordHub` refuses the current source's `LENDER()`. The source still refuses the home Hub passed as `spokeHub`. Same rule as `Q-386`, stated for LPVaultSwap and the flash source. | An address comparison against one Hub admitted a source whose address is a Spoke drawing from the liquidated market's Hub, and LPVaultSwap and the source checked two different Hub identities. Comparing the reported liquidity owner with the chain's whole Hub list, at the setter and per call, closes the path whichever is recorded first. Rules: `REQ-FLASH-001`, `-005`, `-006`, `REQ-SWAP-097`, `REQ-SWAP-120`. Checked by MATH-ST-110. | Resolves new attack N1 (and the N1 note under AMMN-ARCH-04) |
| `Q-336` | Replaceable source and caller-funded fallback | `LPVaultSwap.setFlashSource` (`APPLICATION_ADMIN`, normal delay, cancellable by every canceller) replaces the implementation. When flash liquidity is short or the source is unavailable, the caller funds the debt itself (`F = 0`). Approved keepers keep debt-asset inventory on each chain of at least the largest single `debtToCover` the caps allow. Amends `Q-118`, `Q-144`. **Amended by `Q-386`:** the source's reported lender must not be any Hub on the chain. **Amended by `Q-404`:** there is no flash-funded liquidation path in the core to fall back from; a liquidation caller funds the call itself or through a helper, and committed operators keep debt-asset inventory for the largest single liquidation. A short or replaced flash source affects `deleverage` only. | A replaceable adapter lets Avana move to a later Aave flash facility (for example a V4 flash Spoke) by governance without touching settlement code; the caller-funded path keeps liquidation running while a replacement waits its delay. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-04, AMMN-INT-05 |
| `Q-373` | Flash source interface | `flash/IFlashSource` lends LPVaultSwap the debt asset itself: `flashLoan(asset, amount, spokeHub, context)`, with `maxFlashLoan`, `flashPremium`, `LENDER`, `LP_VAULT_SWAP`, `PARAMETER_STORE`. Before the loan it checks the caller, `LENDER != spokeHub`, available liquidity and `premium × 1e4 ≤ maxFlashFeeBps × amount` (exact, no rounding); in the lender callback it checks the lender, the initiator, one asset and the active transient context. LPVaultSwap implements `flash/IFlashReceiver.onFlashLoan`. The launch implementation uses the Aave V3 Pool `flashLoan` (one asset, mode 0) on Ethereum and Base (`interfaces/external/aave/IAaveV3Pool`, `IAaveV3FlashLoanReceiver`), so the ACLManager `FLASH_BORROWER` role can waive the premium. The implementation is replaceable through the chain's timelock. The GHO flash-minter and GSM interfaces are removed. | Aave V4 has no flash loans; only `flashLoan`, not `flashLoanSimple`, honours `FLASH_BORROWER`. Borrowing the debt asset removes the GHO buy leg. Borrowing from the liquidated Spoke's own Hub would draw on the liquidity the liquidation restores. | Resolves AMMN-ARCH-04 (interface side) |

## Funding, fees and reserves

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-387` | SettlementBudget reserved floor per market | Each market has, in each asset, a reserve `reserved[m][T] ≤ reservedFloor[m][T] ≤ marketWindowCap[m][T]`, with `reservedSum[T]` kept as a running sum. A draw for market `m` is paid first from `m`'s reserve and then from the unreserved balance `balance − reservedSum`, and reverts `InsufficientBudget` if the unreserved part does not cover the rest. `refillReserve(m, T)` (anyone) moves unreserved balance into a reserve up to its floor; `withdraw` takes only unreserved balance; `tightenReservedFloor` (short delay) and `loosenReservedFloor` (normal delay, at most the market window) set the floor; a listing batch sets the floor equal to the market window. Guarantee (`INV-097`, `REQ-BUDGET-027`): per-market draws stay within the market window, total draws within the asset window and the balance, `reservedSum ≤ balance` at all times, and no draw lowers another market's reserve, so each market can always draw `min(reserve, window room, per-call cap)`. The budget pays only rounding gaps (solvency); it never reimburses keeper gas (`Q-381`). **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed. | The per-market windows partitioned the rate of draws but not the shared balance: draws within one market's window over several days lowered the balance for every market until an off-chain top-up, and other markets' draw-needing liquidations reverted. A per-market reserve closes this with two counters read and written per draw. Rules: `SPEC-SET-005`, `SPEC-SET-007`, `REQ-BUDGET-002`, `-003`, `-005`, `-008`, `-022`, `-027`, `-028`, `-029`, `-030`, `REQ-SWAP-029`, `REQ-SWAP-061`. Checked by MATH-ST-109, FV-LIQ-04 (`04o`, `04p`, mutant `04pm`). | Resolves AMMN-RT-13 and new attack N3 |
| `Q-006` | Borrowing liquidity | A capped GHO credit line from the GHO Reserve to the Avana Hub: `creditLineGho` 2,000,000 GHO initially, up to 10M, then 50M+ subject to performance criteria and Aave governance. The DAO treasury supplies it through the Lend Spoke (`Q-070`, `Q-168`). There is no Invest Spoke in v1; outside lender supply is a later phase. **Amended by `Q-233`:** GHO is the first credit line; other approved debt assets each have their own line and explicit funding path, either Lend Spoke supply into the Avana Hub or a capped draw from a source Hub. **Amended by `Q-282`:** a credit line is a draw cap per (Hub, asset, Spoke), and each debt reserve records its funding Hub. | A small initial line limits the GHO at risk while the design is proven; growth is tied to measured performance. | — |
| `Q-012` | CreditLineController | Superseded by `Q-070`. Was: whether a separate contract is needed for credit-line accounting; none exists (`Q-095`). | — | — |
| `Q-013` | First-loss funding and draw rules | Superseded by `Q-101` and `Q-117`. Was: the amount, asset and draw rules of the reserve used for liquidation shortfalls. | — | — |
| `Q-035` | Role of the First-Loss Reserve | The First-Loss Reserve is the Hub-level coverage for recorded GHO deficits from Avana Borrow Spokes (`Q-101`, `Q-142`). In-transaction settlement shortfalls are covered by the separate SettlementBudget (`Q-117`), never by the reserve. **Amended by `Q-236`:** First-Loss Reserve coverage is per debt asset on the Hub that funded it. **Amended by `Q-284`:** there is one Coverage Spoke per (Hub, debt asset) on each chain. | Separating bad-debt coverage from per-liquidation rounding means a run of liquidations cannot drain the capital meant for bad debt, and each pool is sized for its own job. | `Q-071` |
| `Q-070` | Funding and deficit Spokes | The Lend Spoke is the registered Spoke that adds the credit-line GHO to the Hub, with public lender deposits off at launch. A separate Coverage Spoke holds the First-Loss Reserve GHO and is the only Spoke that calls `eliminateDeficit`, so deficit coverage burns only reserve capital. There is no CreditLineController contract. **Amended by `Q-236`:** there is one Coverage Spoke per covered debt asset, each the only caller of `eliminateDeficit` for its asset. **Amended by `Q-282`, `Q-284`:** each chain has one supply-only Lend Spoke, and one Coverage Spoke per (Hub, debt asset). | Standard Spokes keep funding and coverage inside Aave's own accounting; limiting `eliminateDeficit` to the Coverage Spoke means deficits never consume the DAO's credit line. | `Q-012` |
| `Q-071` | First-Loss Reserve scope | Superseded by `Q-035` and `Q-117`. Was: the reserve covers recorded Hub deficits only, and an in-transaction shortfall reverts. | — | — |
| `Q-095` | Credit line ownership | The GHO Reserve (DAO) owns the Lend Spoke position and its interest. The Lend Spoke `addCap` equals the line; Borrow Spoke `drawCap`s sum to at most the line. Recall lowers the `drawCap`s to 0 and returns GHO as debt is repaid; only idle GHO can be withdrawn. **Amended by `Q-233`:** each debt asset's line is recalled through its own `drawCap`s. **Amended by `Q-282`, `Q-314`:** the sum of `drawCap`s is a configuration rule on caps, and each `drawCap` gates new draws only. | Interest goes to the provider of the capital; recall through draw caps stops new borrowing without forcing liquidations. | — |
| `Q-096` | GHO rate | A DAO-set flat base rate at or above the Core Hub GHO rate, plus Aave's per-account risk premium. **Amended by `Q-233`:** each debt asset has its own DAO-set rate; Avana takes no share of interest (`Q-231`). | A rate below the Core Hub would make riskier LP collateral the cheaper way to borrow GHO; the premium charges riskier collateral more. | — |
| `Q-097` | Avana liquidation fee | Avana's share is `avanaFeeShareBps` (20%) of realized surplus after flash repayment and costs, floored at 0; it never makes a liquidation revert. It is paid in the same call to `feeRecipient`, a dedicated Avana treasury Safe. LPVaultSwap measures amounts by delta within each call (`Q-124`) and keeps no accumulated balance. **Amended by `Q-256`:** the fee is computed from the oracle value of the seized units and capped at the realized surplus. **Amended by `Q-337`, `Q-358`:** the fee is paid in the repaid debt asset, first in the order fee, keeper reward, borrower surplus. | Avana earns only when a liquidation recovers more than the debt, and paying out in the same call leaves no balance in LPVaultSwap to take. | — |
| `Q-099` | Borrow or frontend fee | None in v1. **Amended by `Q-231`:** no contract carries a borrow or interest fee; the app charges a frontend fee outside the contracts. | Borrowing costs only the GHO rate, and the app and contracts carry no fee logic to audit. | — |
| `Q-101` | Avana GHO First-Loss Reserve | Avana Labs prefunds GHO before borrowing starts and tops it up to `firstLossReserveMinimum` (400,000 GHO on Ethereum, `coverageRatioBps` × the 2,000,000 GHO initial `drawCap`, `Q-353`); a committed fee share counts once received. Aave governance controls it and delegates coverage to a role that can spend only against a recorded GHO deficit of an allowlisted Avana Borrow Spoke on the same Hub, up to the funded balance, through `eliminateDeficit`, within `deficitCoverageWindow` (72 hours). Withdrawals need explicit DAO approval and wait `reserveWithdrawalDelay` (14 days); unused capital returns after the credit line closes and debt and deficits are resolved. The launch approval states who bears losses beyond the funded reserve. Form: `Q-142`. **Amended by `Q-236`:** the reserve is one Coverage Spoke per covered debt asset, each funded in its asset before that asset's `drawCap` rises above 0, with its own minimum. **Amended by `Q-284`, `Q-353`:** each (Hub, debt asset) has its own Coverage Spoke, funded to its minimum before any `drawCap` on it rises above 0 and at least `coverageRatioBps` of the summed caps. **Amended by `Q-391`:** `coverageRatioBps` and `firstLossReserveMinimum` are set in the funding ARFC with the Aave V4 risk service providers; 400,000 GHO is an illustrative figure only. | Avana's own capital in the first-loss position ties its incentives to the DAO's; DAO control of spending means Avana cannot withdraw the reserve when it is needed. | `Q-013` |
| `Q-102` | Low reserve | A First-Loss Reserve below its minimum raises a monitoring alert and a manual freeze decision by the Guardian or governance; there is no automatic borrow block. **Amended by `Q-227`:** the manual decision is an application freeze by the Emergency Guardian or Aave; the alert is per asset (`Q-236`). **Amended by `Q-241`:** a Coverage Spoke below its minimum automatically blocks borrowing of that asset through the coverage breaker; the alert and the application-freeze decision stay. | An automatic block would stop all borrowing on a single deficit; a human decision first checks the cause. | — |
| `Q-117` | Settlement shortfall budget | An execution shortfall draws from the SettlementBudget (`Q-167`), a capped, prefunded GHO budget held apart from the First-Loss Reserve and limited to rounding gaps (`Q-127`). If flash repayment still cannot be completed, the whole transaction reverts, including seizure, burns and any deficit report. **Amended by `Q-236`:** the SettlementBudget keeps its caps per debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-343`:** the budget holds one balance per debt asset, with a window per market and per-call caps per (CDP Spoke, debt reserve). **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed; the caller pays any rounding gap through the shortfall top-up. | Small rounding gaps would otherwise make dust liquidations revert; a separate, capped budget bounds what any one liquidation or day of liquidations can take. | `Q-013`, `Q-071` |
| `Q-142` | Coverage Spoke | The First-Loss Reserve is a supply-only Spoke holding prefunded GHO shares: Avana funds it, the Aave DAO controls it, zero draw capacity. Its GHO joins the liquidity of the Hub that carries Avana's GHO debt; the shares are earmarked for deficits from allowlisted Avana Borrow Spokes; any yield depends on the Hub's GHO configuration. It also receives the GHO recovered from pending settlements (`Q-211`). The coverage caller and its permissions are confirmed with Aave Labs in the credit-line review before launch. **Amended by `Q-236`:** one Coverage Spoke per covered debt asset. **Amended by `Q-284`, `Q-338`:** one Coverage Spoke per (Hub, debt asset); pending-settlement proceeds go to the Coverage Spoke of the market's pending-settlement reserve. **Amended by `Q-402`:** each Coverage Spoke is also called Avana Umbrella, Avana's protocol safety module. | A standard Spoke uses Aave's existing `eliminateDeficit` path, and zero draw capacity means the reserve cannot be borrowed against. | — |
| `Q-143` | Lend Spoke supply | The Lend Spoke's `addCap` equals the credit line, which fills it first; outside supply cannot enter before the Lend phase. Collateral-factor cuts go by AIP; Avana's immediate market freeze covers the time until the AIP executes. | With the cap filled by the DAO line, no outside lender is exposed before the Lend phase; the freeze stops new exposure while a cut passes. | — |
| `Q-167` | SettlementBudget | A small immutable SettlementBudget contract holding GHO, funded by Avana Labs (20,000 GHO at launch) and owned by the Avana timelock. Only LPVaultSwap can draw, within `settlementBudgetPerCallCap` (25 GHO) and `settlementBudgetPer24hCap` (500 GHO), below immutable maxima of 1,000 and 20,000 GHO; anyone can top it up; withdrawals are one AccessManager-scheduled call at `upgradeDelay` (14 days). **Amended by `Q-228`:** withdrawals are one timelock operation at the normal delay and the caps are set by timelock operations. **Amended by `Q-236`:** caps are kept per debt reserve. **Amended by `Q-267`:** corrected per-call bound, per-call caps of at least twice the rounding allowance, and per-market 24-hour sub-caps. **Amended by `Q-343`, `Q-380`:** the budget holds each debt asset, with windows per market and a per-asset total. **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed with all of its parameters. | Immutable caps bound what a compromised LPVaultSwap can drain to 25 GHO per call and 500 GHO per day. | — |
| `Q-168` | Lend Spoke funding | The DAO treasury supplies the credit-line GHO to the Lend Spoke directly. **Amended by `Q-233`:** other approved supply assets follow the same direct path. | No intermediary contract holds DAO GHO. | — |
| `Q-211` | Pending-settlement proceeds | When LPVaultSwap settles a pending-settlement balance created by reconcile-on-exit (`Q-201`), the unwound liquidity is converted to GHO and all of it goes to the First-Loss Reserve through the Coverage Spoke; the keeper receives nothing, and no Avana fee or SettlementBudget draw applies. **Amended by `Q-236`:** the proceeds fund the GHO Coverage Spoke. **Amended by `Q-224`:** the Adapter burns the settled vault tokens. **Amended by `Q-250`:** pending-settlement legs run with an oracle-derived minimum at a 30 bps allowance. **Amended by `Q-284`, `Q-338`:** the unwound liquidity is converted into the asset of the market's pending-settlement reserve and funds the Coverage Spoke of (Hub, that asset). | The reserve absorbs the losses such an incident causes, and paying no one removes any reason to trigger it. | — |
| `Q-231` | Fees | Avana charges no fee on borrow interest: the Adapter and proxies carry no interest-fee index, fee recipient or fee-first repay, and repay and repay-all read Aave's debt only. Avana's on-chain revenue is `avanaFeeShareBps` (20%) of realized liquidation surplus (`Q-097`, `Q-196`), changed only by an `APPLICATION_ADMIN` operation at the normal delay. Avana's other revenue is a fee the app charges outside the contracts; no contract computes, holds or enforces it. Amends `Q-099`. **Amended by `Q-256`.** | A fee on interest would make the debt a borrower sees differ from Aave's, add an accrual index to audit and model, and change repay-all sizing; the surplus share already ties Avana's income to liquidations that recover more than the debt. Keeping the app fee off-chain keeps it out of the audited code and out of every account's health. | — |
| `Q-236` | Per-asset deficit coverage and settlement budget | A deficit is covered in the asset it is recorded in, on the Hub that funded that debt reserve. Each covered (Hub, debt asset) has its own Coverage Spoke (First-Loss Reserve): a supply-only Spoke controlled by the Aave DAO, registered on that asset with zero draw capacity, funded by Avana Labs in that asset before the asset's `drawCap` rises above 0, with its own `firstLossReserveMinimum`, Borrow Spoke allowlist and `coverDeficit(borrowSpoke, maxAmount)`; a deficit in one asset is never covered with another asset's reserve. The SettlementBudget stays one immutable contract holding GHO, the settlement asset of every liquidation, with per-call and rolling 24-hour caps kept per (Borrow Spoke, debt reserve) under the existing immutable maxima and the sum of all draws in any rolling window at most `PER_24H_CAP_MAX`, drawn with `draw(spoke, debtReserveId, amount)`. Pending-settlement proceeds (`Q-211`) fund the GHO Coverage Spoke. Amends `Q-035`, `Q-070`, `Q-101`, `Q-102`, `Q-117`, `Q-127`, `Q-142`, `Q-167`, `Q-211`. **Amended by `Q-267`:** each market has a 24-hour sub-cap inside its (Borrow Spoke, debt reserve) cap and draws name the market. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-338`, `Q-343`:** liquidations settle in the repaid debt asset, so the SettlementBudget holds each debt asset with windows per market, and pending-settlement proceeds fund the Coverage Spoke of the market's pending-settlement reserve, not always GHO's. **Amended by `Q-391`, `Q-397`, `Q-402`:** no settlement budget exists; the first-loss amounts are set in the funding ARFC; each Coverage Spoke is also called Avana Umbrella. | A deficit is a shortfall of a specific asset owed to that asset's suppliers; covering it in the same asset on the same Hub burns exactly the lost shares and keeps one asset's losses from consuming the reserve set aside for another. Every liquidation repays a GHO flash loan, so the rounding gaps the SettlementBudget fills are always in GHO; one GHO budget with caps per debt reserve bounds each asset's draws without holding idle balances of every asset. | — |
| `Q-249` | Cap sizing as DAO guidance | Caps, reserve minimums, the keeper reimbursement budget and the SettlementBudget caps stay Aave DAO and service-provider values (`Q-197`); Avana enforces none of them on-chain. The cap-sizing rule is stated as guidance with two requirements that both bind: the incident term (the loss factor over the 48-hour window, which already counts bonus paid from the reserve because seized collateral is divided by the bonus) and a tail term for one pool token going to zero: for every non-GHO pool token t and every debt asset a, `min((tokenUsdCap[t] + Σ_{m∋t} topUpSliceUsd[m]) × CF_t, Σ drawCap_a) ≤ firstLossReserveMinimum[a]`, with `CF_t` the largest collateral factor of a market holding t, because the debt those positions can carry before liquidation is at most their booked value times `CF_t` (health factor 1); dividing by `minOpeningHealthFactor` would hold only at the moment of borrowing, before interest accrues. At the launch `CF_t = 0.312`, the 400,000 GHO Ethereum launch reserve covers a token cap of $1,282,051 less the token's slices. The Lens reports the cap-based tail coverage per debt asset (`getTailCoverage(asset)`: funded reserve, largest tail exposure, ratio in bps) and monitoring reports the live ratio from current debt. The reimbursement budget is sized assuming approved keepers receive only loss-making liquidations, and each reserve's 24-hour budget cap from the expected dust-liquidation count of the listing stress case. Amends `Q-185`, `Q-186`, `Q-192`, `Q-197`. **Amended by `Q-391`:** the 400,000 GHO reserve in the tail-term example is illustrative; the real minimum is set in the funding ARFC. **Amended by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget and its caps no longer exist. | The incident term alone allowed any debt cap for stable pools, because their worst historic two-day drops are far below the drop at which it binds; the real tail is one token failing completely, which takes every position in every market holding it close to zero within hours. Tying each token's exposure to the reserve covers that tail. The DAO and its service providers own these values because they depend on market data they maintain; Avana's part is a formula that binds and a live ratio showing how far the configuration is from it. | — |
| `Q-267` | SettlementBudget bounds and market sub-caps | Per call the SettlementBudget pays at most one vault-token unit plus 2 wei plus `U + floorRoundingAllowanceGho` plus `(2 + ⌈drawnIndex / RAY⌉)` debt-asset base units valued at `Pdebt × (1 + buySlippageAllowanceBps)`. Every non-zero `perCallCap` is at least `2 × floorRoundingAllowanceGho`, and the ParameterStore's relation between the allowance and the caps covers only reserves whose caps are not both zero. The flash fee a draw may cover is `φD = flashFee(GHO, min(F, Gcov))`. Each market has a rolling 24-hour sub-cap inside its (Borrow Spoke, debt reserve) cap, `max(100 GHO, ⌊per24hCap / activeMarketCount⌋)`, and LPVaultSwap draws with `draw(spoke, debtReserveId, marketId, amount)`. Amends `Q-167`, `Q-236`. **Amended by `Q-343`, `Q-380`:** draws name the market and the debt asset, and the flash fee a draw may cover is the `IFlashSource` premium on the debt asset. **Superseded by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget is removed; `floorRoundingAllowance` stays, at most $1 per asset. | Aave pulls up to two debt units more than the debt it priced the seizure from, so an honest dust draw could exceed a per-call cap equal to the allowance; the corrected bound and the 2× floor keep dust liquidations settling. Covering the fee on the smaller of the flash amount and the covered cost stops a draw from paying the caller a fee that was never charged. A per-market sub-cap stops a dust wave in one market from spending the whole day's budget and leaving another market's draw-needing liquidations to become deficits. | — |
| `Q-282` | Lend Spoke, credit lines and funding Hubs | Each chain has one supply-only Lend Spoke. Its reserves point to (Hub, asset) pairs and can list any asset. A credit line is a draw cap per (Hub, asset, Spoke): the Lend Spoke's `addCap` on (Hub, asset) equals the line, and the CDP Spokes' `drawCap`s on that (Hub, asset) sum to at most it. GHO is the first credit line and the first borrowable asset. Each debt reserve records its funding Hub and asset in the MarketRegistry (`DebtReserveDef.fundingHub`, `hubAssetId`); a debt reserve's `drawCap` is at most the flash liquidity of its asset in the chain's `IFlashSource` and the depth of its swap routes, measured at listing. Amends `Q-006`, `Q-033`, `Q-070`, `Q-095`, `Q-233`. | Recording the funding Hub on each debt reserve tells settlement, coverage and pause checks which Hub's suppliers carry the loss. Bounding a cap by flash liquidity keeps every borrowed amount liquidatable in one transaction. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-05 (funding Hub), AMMN-ARCH-04 (cap side) |
| `Q-283` | Cross-Hub credit lines | A debt reserve whose funding Hub differs from its CDP Spoke's home Hub is a cross-Hub credit line. It is fully specified and off at launch (`crossHubLinesEnabled = false` per chain; `enableDebtReserve` reverts `CrossHubLinesDisabled`). When enabled: the funding Hub owns the cap; the MarketRegistry keeps one register per (funding Hub, asset) that lists every drawing Spoke on every Hub of the chain and sums their `drawCap`s; `enableDebtReserve` and every cap change require `Σ drawCap ≤ creditLine(fundingHub, asset)` and `Σ foreign drawCap ≤ crossHubCap(fundingHub, asset)`; lowering a line or cross-Hub cap reverts until the caps under it are lowered; `crossHubCap(fundingHub, asset)` is at most the funded balance of the funding Hub's Coverage Spoke for that asset; a borrow reads the pause state of its home Hub and of the funding Hub, so a funding-Hub pause stops the line. | Without a register per funding Hub, each home Hub checks only its own Spokes, so caps on one funding Hub can sum above its line, and a funding Hub that withdraws its line leaves foreign draws open. Capping foreign draws by the funding Hub's coverage keeps a foreign Spoke's losses within what that Hub's first-loss capital can absorb. Off at launch because simulation T3 showed no benefit for the launch markets. | Resolves AMMN-SM-01, AMMN-RT-11, AMMN-FLOW-05, AMMN-FLOW-06, AMMN-SM-06 |
| `Q-284` | Coverage per (Hub, debt asset) | There is one Coverage Spoke per (Hub, debt asset) on each chain. It covers recorded deficits in its asset from every CDP Spoke that draws that asset from its Hub, whatever that Spoke's home Hub, and only those. Pending settlement sends recovered value, in the asset of the market's pending-settlement reserve, to the Coverage Spoke of that reserve's (funding Hub, asset), not always to the GHO Coverage Spoke. Each Coverage Spoke is funded to its minimum before any `drawCap` on its (Hub, asset) is above 0. Amends `Q-101`, `Q-211`, `Q-236`. **Amended by `Q-402`:** each Coverage Spoke is also called Avana Umbrella. | A deficit lands on the Hub that funded the debt, so the coverage that pays it must belong to the same Hub and asset. Routing pending proceeds by the reserve's funding Hub puts recovered value into the reserve that absorbs that incident's losses. | Resolves AMMN-RT-11 (coverage), AMMN-FLOW-05 (coverage) |
| `Q-314` | `drawCap` is a gate on new draws | No draw may complete that leaves `drawn + premium + deficit` above the Spoke's `drawCap`. A cap cut or accrued interest can leave a Spoke above its cap, and then only repayment, liquidation and coverage lower it. The cap-sum rule of `Q-312` is a configuration invariant on caps, not on drawn amounts. Amends `Q-095`. | Aave checks the cap only at a draw, so the state form of the rule is broken by normal events. | Resolves AMMN-SM-02 |
| `Q-353` | Lenders' loss rule for uncovered deficits | Every credit line is bounded by first-loss coverage at the time the cap is set: for each (funding Hub, asset), the sum of `drawCap`s of every drawing Spoke (on every Hub) is at most the committed balance of that (Hub, asset)'s Coverage Spoke divided by `coverageRatioBps` (launch 2,000 bps: coverage at least 20% of the summed caps), checked by the MarketRegistry's credit-line registry at `enableDebtReserve` and at every `drawCap` raise. An uncovered deficit (Aave `deficitRay` left after the Coverage Spoke is exhausted) is a loss of that Hub asset's suppliers pro rata. Avana cannot change Aave's share accounting, so the moment the uncovered amount is non-zero the Guardian runs playbook P-18: it sets `drawCap = 0` for every Spoke on that (Hub, asset), raises an `UncoveredDeficit(hub, asset, amount)` alarm and asks the Aave DAO to realize the deficit against supplier shares, so lenders who withdraw after the report and before realization leave at a price at most the deficit-adjusted price published by the Lens (`uncoveredDeficit(hub, asset)` and exposure per drawing Spoke). Until realization the Lend Spoke stays open; the Lens figure lets every lender see the loss. **Amended by `Q-391`:** the rule stays; `coverageRatioBps` is set in the funding ARFC, with 20 % as an illustrative guide. | First-come losses arise only when coverage is exhausted. Sizing coverage to the caps keeps that case rare, and publishing the uncovered figure removes the information advantage of early withdrawers; realization itself is an Aave governance action. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-15 |
| `Q-360` | Draw caps as a gate, and cross-Hub caps owned by the funding Hub | `INV-057` is a gate: no borrow completes that leaves a CDP Spoke's `drawn + premium + deficit` on its funding Hub's asset above its `drawCap` (`Q-314`). The cap-sum rule (Spoke `drawCap`s ≤ the credit line) is a configuration invariant on caps. A cross-Hub line draws within `crossHubCap[F][a]`, owned and set only by the funding Hub `F`; one registry per chain sums the `drawCap`s of every foreign Spoke drawing `(F, a)`, and `Σ drawCap ≤ crossHubCap[F][a] ≤ firstLossCoverage[F][a]`. A pause of `F` stops every new draw of its liquidity. Every `crossHubCap` is 0 at launch (`INV-094`). Example: coverage $1.2M, cross-Hub cap $1.0M, one foreign Spoke at $600k; a second at $500k reverts. | Aave V4 checks `drawCap` only at a draw, so the state form fails after ordinary interest (QNT-TOPO-04). A cap checked by the borrowing Hub's registry lets a funding Hub's suppliers carry risk their governance never approved (QNT-TOPO-05, QNT-TOPO-X1). | Resolves AMMN-SM-01, AMMN-SM-02, AMMN-SM-06, AMMN-RT-11, AMMN-FLOW-05, AMMN-FLOW-06 |
| `Q-391` | First-loss reserve size is set in the funding ARFC | The coverage rule stays: for each (funding Hub, asset), `fundedBalance × 10⁴ ≥ coverageRatioBps × Σ drawCap` of its Avana Umbrella (`DEF-019`), checked by `enableDebtReserve` and every recorded `drawCap` raise. `coverageRatioBps` and `firstLossReserveMinimum` are set in the funding ARFC with the Aave service providers. 20 % is an illustrative guide only; no parameter row or ARFC text fixes a 100,000 or 400,000 GHO amount, and worked examples that use such amounts are labelled illustrative. Amends `Q-101`, `Q-196`, `Q-236`, `Q-249`, `Q-353`. | The rule is what protects lenders, and it holds at every level. The level depends on the credit-line size the Aave service providers accept, so it is set where the line is funded. | — |
| `Q-392` | Base GHO credit line set in the funding ARFC | Each debt reserve's `drawCap` stays at most that chain's flash liquidity of its asset and its route depth. The Base GHO credit line is set in the funding ARFC within that bound; no figure is fixed in the specification. | Flash liquidity on Base changes over time; the bound is the rule that keeps every borrowed unit liquidatable, and the funding ARFC applies it to the liquidity measured when the line is set. | — |
| `Q-402` | Avana Umbrella | Each Coverage Spoke is also called Avana Umbrella, Avana's protocol safety module, named after Aave's Umbrella because that design inspired it. Each page writes "Avana Umbrella (Coverage Spoke)" on first mention. Amends `Q-142`, `Q-236`, `Q-284`. | The name tells Aave governance what the module does by reference to a design it already knows. | — |

## Governance, roles and emergency

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-007` | Governance structure | The Aave DAO approves deployment and initial parameters and owns the Hub and Spokes. Every upgrade and parameter change to Avana's contracts passes through Avana's AccessManager delays, which Avana Labs cannot bypass, and the Aave DAO and Protocol Guardian can cancel queued changes (`Q-136`). The Aave Protocol Guardian is the Emergency Guardian (`Q-050`). LPVaultSwap has the same governance as other Avana contracts (`Q-090`). Avana Labs prepares market listings and parameter changes; listings wait `upgradeDelay` (`Q-114`). **Amended by `Q-228`:** Avana's upgrades and parameter changes pass through the Avana timelock (normal delay 5 days, short delay 12 hours, 14-day grace); the 3-of-5 Emergency Guardian and Aave pause, freeze and cancel. | The Aave DAO lends the GHO, so it holds final authority and a cancel on every change; Avana runs day-to-day operation within delays the DAO can watch. | — |
| `Q-047` | Governance model | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: the Avana multisig as ADMIN of an OpenZeppelin AccessManager, acting through delays, with admin actions at `upgradeDelay`. | — | — |
| `Q-048` | Change classes and delays | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: Tighten (immediate), Loosen (`loosenDelay`, 3 days), Structural and Admin (`upgradeDelay`, 14 days) change classes in the AccessManager, with a 7-day execution window. | — | — |
| `Q-050` | Emergency Guardian | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: the Aave Protocol Guardian as Emergency Guardian with pauses, freezes, halting restrictions and cancels. | — | — |
| `Q-052` | Market freeze | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: a per-market freeze made of an Avana RiskModule flag or a Guardian `MARKET_FREEZE` plus an Aave reserve freeze. | — | — |
| `Q-083` | Temporary restrictions and recovery | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: Guardian halting restrictions on one incident clock (`incidentDuration`) with an independent Recovery multisig that could lift them. | — | — |
| `Q-089` | Cancel implementation | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: routing the AccessManager's own admin functions through a role guarded by the DAO cancel role. | — | — |
| `Q-090` | LPVaultSwap ownership | LPVaultSwap has the same governance as other Avana contracts: upgradeable by the Avana multisig behind `upgradeDelay`, cancellable by the Aave DAO or Guardian. The keeper list lives on it (`Q-155`). **Amended by `Q-226`:** LPVaultSwap is a UUPS proxy upgraded by `APPLICATION_ADMIN` through the Avana timelock at the normal delay (`Q-228`). | One governance model for all Avana contracts leaves no contract with a separate, less watched owner. | — |
| `Q-091` | New price sources | New price sources and changes to price computation wait `upgradeDelay` (14 days); failover to the pre-approved alternate route is automatic. **Amended by `Q-228`:** new price sources wait the normal timelock delay of 5 days. **Amended by `Q-248`:** a guardian can switch a token to its pre-approved standby route at once; new sources still wait the normal delay. | A new price source can change every account's health at once, so it gets the same review time as code. | — |
| `Q-114` | Governance operations | Avana multisig 4-of-7 with mixed signers (Avana Labs plus external), no signer overlap with the Aave Guardian or the Recovery multisig (3-of-5). Immediate tighten-only functions: the Avana multisig and the risk reviewer. New market listings wait `upgradeDelay`, which provides the two-week public review; activation follows once the on-chain match check against the executed Aave payload passes. Wind-down: a pre-agreed DAO handover of the admin role and an offboarding plan. **Amended by `Q-228`:** the 4-of-7 multisig is the timelock's proposer and a canceller with no instant powers; the Recovery multisig no longer exists and the 3-of-5 Emergency Guardian pauses, freezes and cancels; listings wait the normal delay of 5 days. **Amended by `Q-285`:** each chain has its own timelock, governance Safe and Emergency Guardian. | Separate signer sets mean no single group can both impose and lift restrictions or override a cancel. | — |
| `Q-118` | Emergency matrix | Repay, fee collection and debt-free withdrawal are always available unless Aave itself blocks them. Beyond those: Live, everything. Alternate route serving, everything; liquidation while every input is within `liquidationPriceMaxAge`. Drifting, top-ups and liquidation (Aave decides HF < 1 at served prices); no new-account pledge, borrow or withdraw-with-debt. USD-band breach, pledges, top-ups, and liquidation while both routes are fresh and agree; no borrow or withdraw-with-debt. Feed disagreement, top-ups only; liquidation paused per `Q-162` and `Q-208`. Cached and Expired, nothing; liquidation paused for every market of the account. Recovery grace, per state, with liquidation only below `graceHealthFactor`. Market freeze or offboarding, withdraw-with-debt and liquidation. Aave configuration drift, top-ups of indebted accounts, withdraw-with-debt and liquidation. Hub shares ≠ assets, top-ups of indebted accounts; liquidation in that market stops with an alarm. Aave GHO-reserve freeze, pledge, top-up, withdraw-with-debt and liquidation. Aave vault-token reserve pause, nothing in that market except zero-credit release; other markets' liquidation continues. Aave GHO-reserve pause, top-ups and debt-free withdrawal; repay and liquidation are blocked by Aave. Hub halt, `Q-140`. Avana function pause, only that function stops (top-ups of indebted accounts bypass a pledge pause; a liquidation pause also blocks borrow and withdraw-with-debt). Credit line exhausted, everything except borrow. FlashMinter unavailable, everything; liquidation with keeper GHO only, never funded by the SettlementBudget. Primary route unavailable, liquidation on the backup. Both routes unavailable, liquidation in-kind only while enabled, with keeper GHO; withdraw-with-debt blocked while both route records are disabled and in-kind is not enabled. Pool broken, `Q-171`. SettlementBudget empty or capped, liquidations that need a draw revert. First-Loss Reserve below minimum, everything, plus alert and a manual freeze decision (`Q-102`). Combined states: the most restrictive applies, over every enabled market for borrow and withdraw-with-debt, per account for price staleness in liquidation, and by the seized market's own state otherwise. **Amended by `Q-227`:** the rows for Avana function pauses, market freeze, offboarding, pool broken, keeper suspension and liquidation pause are replaced by the None, Frozen and Paused rows and the `Offboarding` market status; the GHO-reserve rows apply to every debt reserve (`Q-233`). **Amended by `Q-241`, `Q-242`, `Q-247`, `Q-252`:** the matrix gains breaker, address-freeze and pause-reason rows, rescue top-ups pass Frozen, `Paused(General)`, breakers and the freeze, and liquidation staleness is checked only for the seized and repaid reserves. **Amended by `Q-336`, `Q-357`, `Q-350`:** the FlashMinter row becomes "flash source short or unavailable: everything; liquidation with the caller's own debt asset"; the sequencer Down and Grace rows of `Q-357` join the matrix, and a moved pool price adds no row because it never stops a liquidation (`Q-350`). | One table of what each state allows lets every contract, test and runbook agree; keeping repay and exits available in every Avana state means no emergency action can trap a borrower. | `Q-140`, `Q-171` (rows) |
| `Q-136` | Role-change cancellation | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: role-change and AccessManager admin operations cancellable only by the Aave DAO, everything else by the Guardian. | — | — |
| `Q-137` | Guardian incidents | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: a new-incident cooldown needing Recovery consent and incident extensions by Recovery. | — | — |
| `Q-138` | Restriction types | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: halting restrictions that expired with the incident and new-risk restrictions lifted through the Restriction-lift delay. | — | — |
| `Q-139` | Liquidation liveness bounds | Immediate tightening stays, but on-chain bounds ensure every allowed value still lets liquidations run: `slippageAllowanceBps` ≥ the per-market `slippageAllowanceMinBps`, `graceHealthFactor` ≥ 0.90, `graceDuration` ≤ 2 hours, `twapToleranceTicks` ≥ 10; keeper removal, suspension or quarantine cannot take active keepers below `keeperFloor` (3: the primaries plus the standby). **Amended by `Q-227`:** the bounds now constrain timelock setters; keeper suspension and quarantine no longer exist, so only removal is held to `keeperFloor`. **Amended by `Q-317`:** the TWAP bound is `consistencyToleranceBps` of at least `consistencyToleranceMinBps` (10). **Amended by `Q-400`:** `keeperFloor` is 1 (Avana's own keeper). | A tightening key must not be able to stop liquidations by setting a parameter to an extreme value. | — |
| `Q-140` | Hub halt | When the Hub halts the Borrow Spoke, only fee collection and the release of zero-credit records continue; everything else touching the Spoke waits for the DAO. A halt of a single Hub asset blocks only operations that use that asset. Deficit coverage can continue. **Amended by `Q-290`, `Q-351`:** a Hub halt stops add, remove, draw, restore and share transfers while deficit reporting, elimination, premium refresh and fee shares continue, and containment uses a reserve freeze and `drawCap = 0` before any halt. | Aave's halt stops the Spoke's accounting; the matrix states only what Aave still allows. | — |
| `Q-145` | Idle-account refresh | Only the DAO's position-updater role refreshes accounts, through payloads listing every proxy from the indexer; stale premiums between payloads are accepted. | A third-party refresh would let an outsider change other accounts' premiums; the cost of stale premiums is small and bounded by payload frequency. | — |
| `Q-155` | Keeper list | Superseded by `Q-228`. Was: `addKeeper` at `loosenDelay` (3 days) and immediate `removeKeeper` down to `keeperFloor`. | — | — |
| `Q-156` | Quarantine | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: quarantine of a keeper, route or tightening account requested by Recovery and co-signed by the Guardian. | — | — |
| `Q-164` | Pledge pause and rescue | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: top-ups of indebted accounts bypassing a pledge pause, and Guardian-set new-risk restrictions lifted through the Restriction-lift delay. | — | — |
| `Q-171` | Pool broken | Superseded by `Q-227`. Was: a Guardian pool-broken restriction blocking pledge, top-up, borrow and withdraw-with-debt for one market. | — | — |
| `Q-227` | Application pause state | Avana has one pause state, held by the MarketRegistry and read by the AaveAdapter (borrower actions) and the Lens: `None`, `Frozen` or `Paused`. `freeze()` moves None → Frozen (`FREEZE_GUARDIAN` or `APPLICATION_ADMIN`); `pause()` moves None or Frozen → Paused (`PAUSE_GUARDIAN` or `APPLICATION_ADMIN`); `unpause()` moves Paused → None and `unfreeze()` moves Frozen → None, each only by `PAUSE_GUARDIAN` or `APPLICATION_ADMIN`; the freeze guardian cannot unfreeze, and unfreezing while Paused reverts. None allows everything. Frozen blocks new pledges by accounts with no debt and borrowing; it allows top-ups of indebted accounts, repay, fee collection, debt-free withdrawal, withdraw-with-debt (with every check of `Q-229`) and liquidation. Paused blocks new pledges, top-ups, borrowing and withdraw-with-debt; it allows repay, fee collection, debt-free withdrawal, zero-credit release, both liquidation entry points and pending settlement. Neither Frozen nor Paused ever stops liquidation: LPVaultSwap does not read the pause state, and liquidation is gated only by the LPOracle price states, the other liquidation checks and Aave-side reserve pauses. Repay, fee collection, debt-free withdrawal, zero-credit release, liquidation and pending settlement never read the pause state. Per-function and per-market Avana restrictions no longer exist: pledge, borrow, withdraw-with-debt and liquidation pauses, the Avana market freeze, pool broken, keeper and route suspension, tolerance overrides, quarantine, the incident clock, the new-incident cooldown and the Recovery multisig. Per-market containment uses the Aave reserve freeze or pause, a separate source the RiskModule and LPVaultSwap still read; market offboarding is the MarketRegistry market status `Offboarding`, set at the short delay, which blocks new pledges and borrowing against that market. A pause has no on-chain expiry and needs none, because it never stops liquidation; caps stay sized by `Q-192` for the 48-hour incident window, which covers the liquidation stops that remain (Aave-side reserve pauses, which the listing AIP asks Aave to lift within 48 hours, and LPOracle price-state pauses). Supersedes `Q-052`, `Q-083`, `Q-137`, `Q-138`, `Q-156`, `Q-164`, `Q-171`; amends `Q-051`, `Q-054`, `Q-102`, `Q-118`, `Q-139`, `Q-192`, `Q-202`, `Q-206`, `Q-209`, `Q-213`. **Amended by `Q-241`, `Q-242`, `Q-252`:** automatic circuit breakers and the address freeze also stop new activity only; `pause` carries a reason, and rescue top-ups pass Frozen, `Paused(General)`, breakers and the freeze, while `Paused(MintPathSuspect)` refuses every mint. **Amended by `Q-285`, `Q-308`:** the pause state and its reason are kept per Hub, with `pauseAll` and `freezeAll` for every Hub of a chain. | Two levels cover the two kinds of incident: Frozen stops new exposure from new accounts and new debt while everything that reduces risk keeps working, and Paused also stops every path that mints vault tokens or adds debt-backed risk (top-ups and withdraw-with-debt) when the Adapter's minting or check code itself is suspect. Liquidation never stops for an Avana pause because stopping it lets unhealthy accounts lose more value with nobody able to close them, so a pause would turn a code incident into bad debt; a fault in LPVaultSwap is bounded instead by Aave's liquidation sizing (it can seize only accounts Aave deems liquidatable), Collateralization's share-drop and supply-drop checks, the SettlementBudget caps and the timelock, through which a fix or a cancellation lands. Keeping repay and debt-free withdrawal outside both states is what lets borrowers leave before an upgrade lands (`Q-228`). Only the pause guardian or the admin can unfreeze, so a key holding only the freeze role, used for fast containment, cannot undo a freeze. One switch replaces a restriction module whose kinds, scopes and expiries each needed their own rules and checks. | `Q-052`, `Q-083`, `Q-137`, `Q-138`, `Q-156`, `Q-164`, `Q-171` |
| `Q-228` | Timelock governance | Every privileged change to Avana's contracts executes through one Avana timelock, a TimelockController extension that is not upgradeable and holds its own `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` with no other admin and no bypass. On every Avana contract with roles the timelock holds `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` and `APPLICATION_ADMIN`; `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` only grants and revokes roles, and upgrades, fee changes and every parameter setter require `APPLICATION_ADMIN`. Delays: the normal delay is 5 days and can never exceed 5 days; the short delay is 12 hours and applies to an operation only when every call in it is on the short-delay list (tightening setters within liveness bounds, `disableRoute`, `restoreRoute` of a previously approved route, `removeKeeper` down to `keeperFloor`, in-kind enablement, market offboarding, `activateMarket` and `cancelRegistration`); a ready operation expires 14 days after it becomes executable and can then only be cleaned up. Execution is public: anyone can execute a ready operation. The 4-of-7 governance multisig is the only proposer and a canceller. The 3-of-5 Emergency Guardian holds `PAUSE_GUARDIAN` and `FREEZE_GUARDIAN` and cancels; it has no upgrade or parameter power. Aave holds `PAUSE_GUARDIAN` and `FREEZE_GUARDIAN` through the Aave Protocol Guardian, which also cancels, and the Aave governance executor cancels. An operation that only grants or revokes roles can be cancelled only by the Aave governance executor. Pause, freeze and cancel are immediate; no parameter changes without a delay. Old classes map as follows: Tighten → short delay; Loosen, Structural, Admin and the Avana half of a listing → normal delay; Restriction lift → `unfreeze` or `unpause` by the pause guardian (immediate) or a normal-delay operation; Emergency → immediate pause, freeze and cancel; Immutable and Aave governance unchanged. Keepers are added at the normal delay and removed at the short delay; in-kind enablement lasts `inKindExpiry` (24 hours) from execution. The signer sets of the governance multisig, the Emergency Guardian and the Aave Protocol Guardian do not overlap. Supersedes `Q-047`, `Q-048`, `Q-050`, `Q-089`, `Q-136`, `Q-151`, `Q-155`, `Q-163`; amends `Q-007`, `Q-090`, `Q-091`, `Q-104`, `Q-114`, `Q-133`, `Q-167`, `Q-196`, `Q-209`, `Q-210`, `Q-219`. **Amended by `Q-242`, `Q-245`, `Q-248`, `Q-259`, `Q-262`, `Q-263`:** address unfreeze and liquidation-restoring operations are cancellable only by the Aave governance executor; the executor is also a proposer; admin roles can be granted only to the timelock; the normal delay is at least 4 days; the short-delay list is an immutable allowlist that includes the new-activity stops. **Amended by `Q-285`:** each chain has its own AvanaTimelock with the same delays and roles. | A self-administered timelock means nobody, including the multisig that proposes, can shorten a delay or grant a role without waiting the same delay in public. Five days is enough for borrowers to exit before an upgrade lands because repay and debt-free withdrawal are never blocked by a pause or a gate (`Q-227`): leaving takes one repay and one withdrawal, and the same five days give the Aave DAO and the Emergency Guardian time to cancel. The 12-hour delay applies only to changes that reduce risk or restore reviewed configuration, so it cannot loosen anything.
