The grace window stops an approved operation being executed long after conditions changed, and public execution stops a proposer holding an approved change back to time it. Keeping role changes cancellable only by the Aave governance executor stops a guardian blocking its own removal or a replacement's appointment. | `Q-047`, `Q-048`, `Q-050`, `Q-089`, `Q-136`, `Q-151`, `Q-155`, `Q-163` |
| `Q-241` | Circuit breakers | Avana has automatic circuit breakers that stop new activity only. The RiskModule evaluates them in `checkPledge`, `preBorrowCheck` and `preWithdrawCheck`; repay, debt-free withdrawal and partial removal, fee collection, zero-credit release, pending settlement, liquidation, rescue top-ups and repay with collateral never read them, and LPVaultSwap never reads them. A market breaker on market m blocks new-account pledges, non-rescue top-ups and re-credits into m, and borrowing and withdraw-with-debt by every account holding m. A debt breaker on (Borrow Spoke, debt reserve r) blocks borrowing r and withdraw-with-debt by every account with debt in r. Condition breakers are evaluated from live on-chain state at each check and clear with the condition: oracle failure (the price-state and debt-price gates: Cached, Expired, Drifting, stale or disagreeing debt price); price drop (the market's served price at least `breakerPriceDropBps`, 300, below the LPOracle's hourly checkpoint taken within the last two hours); pool share (Avana's supply above `breakerPoolShareBps`, 2,000, of the pool's time-weighted in-range liquidity, counted only while the LP's uncapped USD level is at or below peg, `q0 · q1 ≤ 10^16`, because a premium cannot inflate collateral valued at the $1 ceiling and liquidation never trades through the market's own pool); issuer action (a pool token or debt asset reporting `paused()` or a non-zero USDT fee rate through the probe recorded at listing, a reverting probe counting as tripped); reserve coverage (the debt asset's Coverage Spoke below `firstLossReserveMinimum`); deficit (the asset's Hub deficit on that Spoke above `breakerDeficitMax`, 1,000 GHO at launch); budget (SettlementBudget balance below the reserve's `per24hCap`). Latched breakers are stored in the MarketRegistry and stay tripped for `breakerCooldown` (6 hours) after their last trigger: seizure surge (more than `breakerSeizureBps`, 2,000, of the market's credited units seized within one hour) and route failures (at least `breakerRouteFailureCount`, 3, primary sell-route or buy-route failures within one hour). Only LPVaultSwap writes latched counters, from completed liquidations and keeper shortfall reports (`Q-251`), through a call with a fixed gas limit whose failure never reverts the liquidation. The Emergency Guardian and the Aave Protocol Guardian trip a market or debt breaker manually at once (`tripMarketBreaker`, `tripDebtBreaker`, `APPLICATION_FREEZE_GUARDIAN`); `APPLICATION_PAUSE_GUARDIAN` resets a latched or manual breaker at once and `APPLICATION_ADMIN` through the timelock; latched breakers also reset when their cooldown ends. Keeper outages are found by monitoring (`Q-244`) and answered with a manual trip; on-chain, having no unfrozen approved keeper stays a liquidation halt for new borrowing. Thresholds live in the ParameterStore: more sensitive at the short delay, less sensitive at the normal delay. The Lens reports every breaker. Amends `Q-102`, `Q-118`, `Q-227`, `Q-229`. **Amended by `Q-309`, `Q-350`, `Q-317`:** the `CONFIG_DRIFT` breaker covers the standard-Spoke settings, a `POOL_DEPTH` breaker joins the condition breakers, route-failure counting covers swap routes only, and `breakerDeficitMax` is per (Hub, asset). **Amended by `Q-399`:** the breakers follow the final v1 table: price drop, seizure surge, route failures, coverage and budget are alerts or removed, the pool-share threshold is `poolShareMaxBps` (10 %; off by default with an optional per-pool cap since `Q-403`), the deficit freeze is at 10,000 GHO and needs an authorized reopening, and temporary gates reopen after 30 minutes of healthy observations. | Fast failures (a feed outage, a depeg, a drained pool, an issuer pause, an exploit that seizes many positions at once) reach new borrowing faster than a person can react; a breaker stops new exposure at once while every action that reduces risk keeps working, so a breaker can never turn an incident into bad debt. Triggers use values one actor cannot move in one transaction (Chainlink prices, time-weighted pool liquidity, real deficits, real seizures), and no trigger counts withdrawals, so a borrower's own exit never trips one; forcing a trip costs real money or needs a real failure, and its worst effect is six hours without new borrowing in one market or one debt asset. A reverted transaction leaves no trace on-chain, so failures are counted only from calls that complete, and counting can never make a liquidation fail. | — |
| `Q-242` | Address freeze | The MarketRegistry keeps a set of frozen addresses. `freezeAddress(account)` is immediate for `APPLICATION_FREEZE_GUARDIAN` (the Emergency Guardian and the Aave Protocol Guardian), at most 20 freezes per caller in any rolling 24 hours, and is on the short-delay list for `APPLICATION_ADMIN`. `unfreezeAddress(account)` is an `APPLICATION_ADMIN` operation at the normal delay that only `DAO_CANCELLER_ROLE` can cancel. As the calling wallet, a frozen address cannot open an account, make a non-rescue top-up or re-credit, borrow, withdraw or remove liquidity with debt, repay with collateral or have a proxy created; it cannot be a borrow receiver; it cannot call `liquidate`, `liquidatePermissionless` or `reportRecoveryShortfall`, and cannot be added as a keeper. The AaveAdapter, LPVaultSwap and the ProxyManager check it. A frozen address keeps repay as payer or borrower, debt-free withdrawal and partial removal, `repayAllAndWithdraw`, fee collection, zero-credit release, rescue top-ups, PayoutEscrow claims and `settlePending`; its positions are liquidated as any account's are, and contract-to-contract calls are never checked. `freezeAddress` reverts for the AvanaTimelock, the Aave governance executor, every Avana contract and vault token, every registered proxy, the Hub, every approved Spoke and Coverage Spoke, the GHO FlashMinter and every route, and for an approved keeper when the unfrozen approved keepers would fall below `keeperFloor`; keeper floors and the zero-keeper halt count unfrozen keepers only. The vault token is unchanged: immutable, with no blocklist. Amends `Q-227`, `Q-228`. **Amended by `Q-285`, `Q-356`, `Q-334`:** the freeze set is per chain; a frozen wallet may repay with collateral when the call only reduces debt; and the protected set names the flash source instead of the GHO FlashMinter. | During an exploit the attacker's contract keeps opening positions while the incident is handled; freezing that address stops new exposure from it in minutes without pausing every user. Exits and liquidation stay outside the freeze so that a guardian, even a compromised one, can neither trap a borrower's collateral nor shield an account from liquidation, and rescue top-ups stay open so a freeze cannot force a borrower into liquidation. The protected set keeps a freeze from stopping Avana itself, and counting only unfrozen keepers keeps the keeper path running. Unfreezing waits the normal delay and only the Aave DAO can cancel it, so the guardian that froze an address cannot keep it frozen by cancelling every unfreeze. A freeze limits an address, not a person: a frozen actor can exit debt-free and start again from another wallet, so the freeze is an incident tool. | — |
| `Q-243` | Emergency playbooks | 3.6 Emergency Operations holds a step-by-step playbook for each emergency type: oracle failure, staleness, disagreement or manipulation; market crash or stablecoin depeg; pool or route liquidity drain; exploit in progress; malicious or mistaken governance operation; compromised signer or key; keeper outage or misbehaving keeper; bad debt; Hub liquidity crunch or withdrawal cascade; token issuer action (pause, blocklist, fee switch); Aave-side incident; frontend or infrastructure compromise. Each playbook states its detection (the alert of `Q-244`), the breaker that trips by itself (`Q-241`), who acts immediately and with which call, the ordered steps, what stays open (repay, debt-free withdrawal, fee collection, pending settlement and liquidation, always) and the recovery: the pause guardian lifts Paused or Frozen only after the playbook's recovery checks pass, and every loosening (address unfreeze, switch back to a primary route, new price source or route, parameter loosening, upgrade) goes through the timelock. Every incident starts with the same triage (confirmation on-chain within 15 minutes, classification, paging both Safes, an incident log with every transaction hash) and ends with a status note within 1 hour and a published post-mortem within 7 days that adds the tests and alerts the cause showed were missing. Each playbook is rehearsed on testnet before mainnet and on a fork every quarter. **Amended by `Q-351`, `Q-353`, `Q-355`:** playbooks P-16 (Aave-side halt), P-18 (uncovered deficit) and P-19 (ledger or LPVault bug) join the set. | In an incident the order of actions matters as much as speed: stop new exposure first, keep exits and liquidation open, and change configuration only through reviewed timelock operations. Writing the steps down beforehand, with the actor and the call for each, removes decisions that would otherwise be made under pressure and keeps every response within the powers each role actually holds. | — |
| `Q-245` | Multisig and key security | Every signer key of the governance Safe (4-of-7), the Emergency Guardian (3-of-5) and the Avana treasury Safe is on a dedicated hardware wallet with on-device typed-data display; no hot, browser-extension or shared keys. Keeper keys are in an HSM or KMS per operator. At most 3 of the governance Safe's 7 signers belong to Avana Labs, and its signers span at least 3 independent organisations and 3 jurisdictions; the Emergency Guardian's 5 signers are 5 people across at least 3 time zones; no person sits on two Safes. For every transaction each signer decodes the calldata from the raw transaction, recomputes the Safe transaction hash against the device, runs a fork simulation of the operation and its result and checks `requiredDelay` against the intended class; two signers check every target against the deployment manifest. Every signer of both Safes sends a signed liveness message each month, with an alert when fewer than threshold + 1 answer within 72 hours; both Safes run pause and cancel drills on a fork every quarter. A departing or suspected signer is replaced within 72 hours, and every key is rotated at least every 24 months. A Safe guard limits the governance Safe to calls to the AvanaTimelock and to itself, and a delay module makes owner and threshold changes wait 48 hours. The Aave governance executor also holds `PROPOSER_ROLE`, and the live-configuration check requires at least two proposers. Amends `Q-228`. | The timelock protects borrowers only while the keys that propose, cancel and pause are independent and reachable. Hardware keys, independent signers and checked signing stop one compromised device or organisation from passing a harmful operation, liveness checks find a lost quorum before an incident needs it, and the owner-change delay stops four compromised keys from replacing the other three at once. With the governance Safe as the only proposer, losing four keys or renouncing the role by mistake would have stopped every repair for good; the DAO as a second proposer restores that path through Aave governance, which every canceller still watches. | — |
| `Q-259` | Admin roles only for the timelock | Every upgradeable Avana contract reverts `AdminRoleOnlyTimelock(role, account)` on a grant of `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` or `APPLICATION_ADMIN` to any account other than the AvanaTimelock, and the AvanaTimelock reverts the same error for a grant of its `DEFAULT_ADMIN_ROLE` to anyone but itself. Moving to a new timelock is an upgrade of each contract to an implementation that names it, at the normal delay and cancellable by every canceller. Amends `Q-228`. | A role grant is a role-change operation that only the Aave DAO can cancel; if it could hand admin power to an address, that address could then upgrade the Adapter at once with no delay and no canceller, which defeats the timelock. Fixing admin power to the timelock in code means every upgrade and setter always waits its delay in public. | — |
| `Q-262` | Timelock delay bounds and the short-delay allowlist | The AvanaTimelock has `MIN_NORMAL_DELAY` of 4 days: `setNormalDelay` accepts only values in `[MIN_NORMAL_DELAY, MAX_DELAY]`. The short-delay selectors form an immutable allowlist `SHORT_ELIGIBLE` fixed in the constructor; `setShortDelaySelector(target, selector, true)` reverts `InvalidShortDelaySelector` unless the pair is in it, and disabling an entry is always allowed. `SHORT_ELIGIBLE` holds every tighten setter, `disableRoute`, `disableBuyRoute`, `restoreRoute`, `restoreBuyRoute`, `removeKeeper`, `enableInKind`, `disableInKind`, `setMarketOffboarding`, `activateMarket`, `cancelRegistration`, and the new-activity stops `freeze`, `pause`, `freezeAddress`, `tripMarketBreaker`, `tripDebtBreaker` and `useStandbyRoute`. Amends `Q-210`, `Q-228`. **Amended by `Q-312`:** buy routes no longer exist, so `disableBuyRoute` and `restoreBuyRoute` have no target. **Amended by `Q-397`, `Q-398`, `Q-399`, `Q-401`:** `enableInKind`, `disableInKind`, `disableBuyRoute`, `restoreBuyRoute`, the SettlementBudget tighten selector and the removed breaker-threshold tighteners are not in `SHORT_ELIGIBLE`; it holds the 31 pairs of [2.15](../02-contracts/15-governance-and-timelock.md) `REQ-ACCESS-155`, including `tightenHardHealthFloor` and `tightenGateRecoverySeconds`. | The exit guarantee rests on the normal delay exceeding a borrower's time to leave plus the cancellers' reaction; a delay that one operation could cut to 12 hours, or a short list that could grow to include upgrades, would void it from then on. A fixed allowlist and a floor keep both properties for the life of the timelock. Putting the stops on the short list gives the governance Safe a 12-hour way to halt new activity when both guardians are unavailable, and each of them only blocks new activity. | — |
| `Q-263` | Cancelling operations that restore liquidation | An operation whose every call restores liquidation or an address (`restoreRoute`, `restoreBuyRoute`, `useStandbyRoute`, `enableInKind`, `setInKindAutoTrigger` enabling a trigger, `addKeeper`, `unfreezeAddress`) can be cancelled only by `DAO_CANCELLER_ROLE`, as role changes are; an operation that mixes such calls with any other call keeps the ordinary cancel rule. Amends `Q-228`. **Amended by `Q-312`:** buy routes no longer exist, so `restoreBuyRoute` has no target. **Amended by `Q-401`:** `enableInKind` and `setInKindAutoTrigger` no longer exist, so the DAO-only cancel set is `restoreRoute`, `useStandbyRoute`, `unfreezeAddress` and `addKeeper` ([2.15](../02-contracts/15-governance-and-timelock.md) `REQ-ACCESS-156`). | Any single canceller could cancel every repair while liquidation was halted, so a compromised guardian could keep a market unliquidatable for as long as its role lasted. Repairs that only restore reviewed configuration carry little risk of their own, so the Aave DAO alone may stop them; requiring every call of the operation to qualify stops a proposer from hiding another call behind one. | — |
| `Q-315` | Wind-down ends | `stepWindDown` applies pre-approved liquidation-threshold steps after the notice period. `closeMarket` runs once the market has no debt. Debt-free positions stay withdrawable forever. A ledger or LPVault bug is handled by pausing, letting users exit and relisting on a new CDP Spoke. Adopts `Q-355` on 2.7 and 2.8. | Without a terminal step, one dormant borrower keeps a market's feed, routes and keepers alive forever. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-22 |
| `Q-355` | Wind-down ends; ledger-bug exit | Delisting is `Offboarding` → `Closed`. After the notice period (`offboardNoticeSeconds`, at least the normal delay), the market's liquidation threshold ramps down linearly over `windDownRampSeconds` (launch 30 days) to `windDownFinalLtBps` (launch 5,000 bps), applied by `MarketRegistry.stepWindDown(m)` through Aave dynamic configuration updates that the Aave payload pre-approves. When the ramp ends, any account of the market with debt is liquidatable at the Spoke's bonus once its health factor is below 1 at the final threshold, and keepers close it through the normal liquidation flow. When the market's total debt across all its accounts is 0, anyone calls `MarketRegistry.closeMarket(m)`: the market enters `Closed`, its LPOracle, swap routes, keeper duty and settlement-budget share are released, and every remaining debt-free position is withdrawable by its depositor forever, because a debt-free withdrawal reads no price. A bug in an immutable LPVault or in Collateralization is handled by playbook P-19: pause new activity on the chain, let users repay and withdraw, deploy a fixed contract, and relist the markets on a new CDP Spoke (market identity includes the Spoke, `Q-299`, so relisting the same pool and range is allowed). | Without a terminal step one dormant borrower keeps a market's feed, routes and keepers alive forever. The ramp makes every remaining borrower act or be liquidated, and debt-free positions need no oracle to exit. | Resolves AMMN-FLOW-22 |
| `Q-399` | Circuit breakers, final v1 table | Oracle or stablecoin band breach (the existing oracle and peg checks, with the issuer probe): block new borrowing and collateral withdrawals that leave debt. Price drop of 3 % in 2 hours: alert only. Avana's combined share of a pool above 10 % across all borrowers and Spokes on the chain (`poolShareMaxBps`, which absorbs `breakerPoolShareBps`): block new borrowing and pledges that increase pool exposure. Collateral seized above 20 % of a market in 1 hour: alert only. Swap-route failures, 3 in 1 hour: alert; a guardian or the timelock freezes new borrowing only when the approved routes are confirmed unable to settle valid liquidations at the required size, with no on-chain failure counter. Outstanding deficit above `breakerDeficitMax` (10,000 GHO for the affected CDP Spoke; a per-reserve materiality set at listing for other assets): new borrowing on that debt reserve freezes automatically, and every new deficit raises an alert. Reopening: temporary price or depth gates (`ORACLE`, `POOL_SHARE`, `POOL_DEPTH`) clear after `gateRecoverySeconds` of healthy observations, 30 minutes as a provisional launch setting; a deficit or security freeze (`DEFICIT`, `ISSUER`, `CONFIG_DRIFT`, `MANUAL`) needs an authorized reopening by a guardian or the timelock (2.15). The permissionless `pokeBreakers(marketId)` records breaches and latches freezes, and a live condition blocks without it. Removed as on-chain trips: price drop, seizure surge, route failures, coverage and budget, with `breakerCooldown`, `breakerSeizureBps`, `breakerRouteFailureCount`, `breakerPriceDropBps`, `BREAKER_WINDOW` and `BREAKER_RECORD_GAS`; their thresholds stay as monitoring alert parameters. Repayment, debt-free exits, rescue top-ups and eligible liquidations stay available through every breaker; liquidations still need a valid price, Aave eligibility and executable settlement, and an Aave reserve pause can independently block repay or liquidation. Amends `Q-084`, `Q-196`, `Q-241`, `Q-367`. **Amended by `Q-403`:** Avana's combined share of a pool above 25 % is an alert only; the concentration gate blocks only above a per-pool cap that the pool's listing sets, and the chain default `poolShareMaxBps` is 10,000 bps (off). | Counters of seizures and route failures were written from liquidation outcomes: a reverted call leaves no count, and an attacker can feed or starve a counter. A price-drop trip stops borrowing in ordinary volatility. The gates kept read live state that is costly to fake, and the freezes that need a person to reopen are those where a loss or an external security event has already happened. | — |
| `Q-400` | Keeper floor of one | `keeperFloor` = 1 (Avana's own keeper) per chain and `MAX_KEEPERS` = 32. `removeKeeper` below the floor reverts; freezing a keeper is never blocked. New borrowing halts on every Hub of the chain only when 0 active keepers remain. More keepers can be added later at the normal delay. Amends `Q-139`, `Q-196`, `Q-289`. **Amended by `Q-404`:** the keepers counted are the registered committed operators; the registry gates no call, and liquidation stays open to every address while borrowing halts at zero. | Avana's own keeper is the committed liquidator, and the permissionless fallback stands beside it. A floor of 3 made borrowing depend on outside parties' availability without adding a liquidation guarantee, and a compromised keeper must always be freezable. | — |

## Parameters

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-014` | Delays, signers and thresholds | Superseded by `Q-196`. Was: timelock delays, grace window, signers and thresholds to be agreed with Aave service providers; delays are in `Q-048`, signers in `Q-114`. | — | — |
| `Q-015` | Oracle and settlement values | Superseded by `Q-196` and `Q-197`. Was: TWAP, heartbeat, cache, bonus, fee, target HF, cap and risk-factor values to be set by the risk reviewer. | — | — |
| `Q-072` | Economic parameters | `minOpeningHealthFactor` 1.10. No minimum debt (`Q-169`). Pool risk factors are set by the risk service providers within the formula and bounds of `Q-020` and `Q-186`. Worked examples use Aave V4's real bonus curve and dust rule (`Q-179`). **Amended by `Q-287`:** a minimum debt per chain applies: $2,000 on Ethereum and $50 on Base at launch. **Superseded by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt. **Amended by `Q-394`, `Q-398`:** `minOpeningHealthFactor` no longer exists, and every economic value is a proposed default whose final value the listing or funding ARFC sets. | The spec fixes formulas and bounds so that each value set later can be checked against them. | — |
| `Q-186` | Initial pool risk factor | 0.40 is the ceiling at launch; the risk service providers set each pool's factor. With a 78% underlying collateral factor: LP CF 31.2%, LTV 28.4% at HF 1.10. Debt cap per market = the lowest of the GHO allocation, what LPVaultSwap can unwind and settle under stress, and what the funded First-Loss Reserve can cover. For a token shock, losses are summed across every exposed pool and Borrow Spoke on the same Hub (each account counted once) and tested against the remaining reserve; the outage scenario is rerun at full utilization, including recovery shortfalls from reverted settlements, before caps are approved. **Amended by `Q-249`:** sizing adds the single-token tail term against each asset's reserve. | LP positions carry pool and unwind risk on top of their tokens' risk; a low ceiling keeps the launch margin wide, and sizing caps against the reserve keeps the worst modelled loss covered. | — |
| `Q-192` | Pause-loss cap sizing | Caps are sized with the proved per-account pause-loss bound debt × max(0, (1 + g) − h0(1 − dLP)/(CF × LB)), using each pool's worst historic LP drop over the incident window plus margin, and h0 = `pauseLossStartHealthFactor` = 0.95 for keeper lag; caps ≤ reserve / loss factor. **Amended by `Q-227`:** the incident window stays 48 hours; Avana's pause state never stops liquidation, so the window covers the liquidation stops that remain: Aave-side reserve pauses (`Q-202`) and LPOracle price-state pauses. **Amended by `Q-234`:** sizing includes a debt-asset price rise. **Amended by `Q-249`:** the incident term is joined by the single-token tail term, and both are guidance for the Aave DAO and its service providers. | Liquidations can be paused for up to an incident; caps sized to the worst loss during that pause keep the resulting bad debt within the First-Loss Reserve. | — |
| `Q-196` | Parameter values | Launch values (full table in 1.5): `PROXY_HF_FLOOR` 1.05; `borrowPriceMaxAge` 1 hour; `liquidationPriceMaxAge` and `feedMaxAge` = feed heartbeat + 10 minutes; TWAP 30 minutes and 50 ticks; USD band 0.90–1.02; feed-agreement tolerance = primary + alternate deviation thresholds + 50 bps; grace 30 minutes below HF 0.95; slippage allowance 100 bps; Avana fee 20% of realized surplus to a dedicated Avana treasury Safe; SettlementBudget 20,000 GHO funded, 25 GHO per call, 500 GHO per rolling 24 hours; in-kind lifetime 24 hours; minimum liquidation bonus just below HF 1 at least 2%; pool-share cap 10% (amended by `Q-403`: 10,000 bps, off by default, with an optional per-pool cap); top-up slice 10% of each market cap; stress floor $1.00 per whole vault token; First-Loss Reserve minimum 400,000 GHO on Ethereum (`coverageRatioBps` 2,000 × the 2,000,000 GHO initial `drawCap`, `Q-353`) and 24,000 GHO on Base; Guardian incident 48 hours, new-incident cooldown 7 days; Avana multisig 4-of-7, Recovery multisig 3-of-5; `pokeMinGas` 150,000. **Amended by `Q-228`:** the incident and Recovery values are replaced by the timelock values (normal delay 5 days, short delay 12 hours, grace 14 days), the 4-of-7 governance multisig and the 3-of-5 Emergency Guardian; the cap-sizing incident window stays 48 hours (`Q-192`). **Amended by `Q-274`:** `borrowPriceMaxAge` is `min(heartbeat + 600 s, 4,200 s)` per feed. **Amended by `Q-317`, `Q-322`, `Q-287`:** feed ages are each feed's heartbeat plus `heartbeatMargin` (1,800 s); the TWAP tolerance is `consistencyToleranceBps`; the SettlementBudget is held per debt asset with windows per market (`Q-343`); and the minimum debt is $2,000 on Ethereum and $50 on Base. **Amended by `Q-391`, `Q-394`, `Q-398`, `Q-399`, `Q-400`:** the health floors are removed, the First-Loss Reserve minimum and coverage ratio are set in the funding ARFC, breaker thresholds follow the final v1 table, `keeperFloor` is 1, and every economic value is a proposed default whose final value the listing or funding ARFC sets. | Fixed launch values let caps, liveness bounds and the verification models be checked against concrete numbers; every value sits inside the on-chain bounds in 1.5. | `Q-014`, `Q-015` |
| `Q-197` | Values set with Aave service providers | Per-market, per-token and per-depositor USD caps, each pool's risk factor (within the 0.40 ceiling) and the keeper reimbursement budget are set by the Aave V4 risk service providers: caps and risk factors in each market's listing payload, the reimbursement budget in the keeper agreement. **Amended by `Q-230`:** each market's `minPledgeUsd` is set in its listing operation with the caps. **Amended by `Q-249`:** the sizing rule is published as guidance with a tail term, and the Lens and monitoring report the coverage ratio. | These values depend on market data the risk service providers already maintain for Aave, and the DAO relies on their review. | `Q-015` |
| `Q-210` | Remaining parameter values | `incidentDuration` bounds 1 hour – 7 days; `newIncidentCooldown` at most 30 days; liveness bounds: grace HF ≥ 0.90, grace duration ≤ 2 hours, TWAP tolerance ≥ 10 ticks; `deficitCoverageWindow` 72 hours. Technical constants: `POKE_MIN_GAS_FLOOR` / `POKE_MIN_GAS_CEILING` 100,000 / 500,000; `MAX_ROUTE_ANSWER` $10,000; `LISTING_SHOCK_FACTOR` 100; `MAX_FULL_RANGE_EPSILON` 10⁻¹²; SettlementBudget cap maxima 1,000 / 20,000 GHO; budget bucket 1 hour; `LENS_MAX_PAGE` 200; `floorRoundingAllowanceGho` 1 GHO. **Amended by `Q-228`:** the incident and cooldown bounds no longer exist; the timelock bounds are normal delay at most 5 days, short delay 12 hours, grace 14 days. **Amended by `Q-261`, `Q-262`:** liveness bounds gain the per-reserve buy-slippage minimum; the normal delay is bounded below by 4 days. **Amended by `Q-317`, `Q-325`, `Q-343`:** the TWAP bound is `consistencyToleranceBps` ≥ 10, `MAX_FULL_RANGE_EPSILON` is removed, the rounding allowance is `floorRoundingAllowance(asset)` per debt asset, and SettlementBudget caps are per asset and market. **Amended by `Q-394`:** economic values are proposed defaults whose final value the listing or funding ARFC sets. | Fixed bounds stop any setter, including an immediate tighten, from moving a value outside the range the verification models cover. | — |
| `Q-246` | Risk-parameter change process | Every Avana-side risk parameter changes through one process: (1) the risk service providers publish the change with the cap-sizing calculation of `Q-249`; (2) the governance Safe builds the operation and every signer follows `Q-245`; (3) it is scheduled with `delay ≥ requiredDelay`, the short delay only when every call is a tightening on the short-delay list, so one loosening call makes the whole batch wait the normal delay; (4) monitoring alerts on `CallScheduled` and any canceller may cancel during the delay; (5) anyone executes it from ready time until 14 days later, and each setter re-checks its bounds at execution; (6) the live-configuration check and the Lens cap headroom confirm the result. The ParameterStore bounds each loosening: `minOpeningHealthFactor` down by at most 0.03, any USD cap up by at most 50 %, `slippageAllowanceBps` and `buySlippageAllowanceBps` up by at most 25 bps, and at most one loosening of each parameter per `normalDelay`; tightening is bounded only by the liveness bounds. `usdBandLow`, `usdBandHigh`, `graceDuration` and `graceHealthFactor` change only at the normal delay. Aave-side values (collateral factor, bonus curve, target health factor, Hub and Spoke caps, credit lines, reserve minimums) change through Aave governance; for a collateral-factor cut Avana first tightens `poolRiskFactor` at the short delay so the drift check (`Q-270`) stops new borrowing while Aave's factor is above the new expected one, then the Aave payload lowers it; for a raise the Avana change at the normal delay executes first. Amends `Q-110`, `Q-196`, `Q-228`. **Amended by `Q-317`:** `buySlippageAllowanceBps` no longer exists. **Amended by `Q-398`:** `LOOSEN_HEALTH_FACTOR_STEP` applies to the optional `hardHealthFloor`. | Each change can look safe on its own while risk accumulates through many small loosenings of caps and tolerances. Bounding each loosening and spacing them one normal delay apart keeps every step reviewable and gives borrowers and cancellers the full window for each, while tightening stays fast for incidents. Ordering the two sides of a collateral-factor change keeps Avana from opening loans on a factor larger than its caps and margins assume. | — |
| `Q-277` | Minimum pledge value stays $10 | `minPledgeUsd` stays $10 per market and there is still no minimum debt. The cost of liquidating small accounts is handled by the keeper minimum-size rule (`Q-251`), Aave's dust rule, a reimbursement budget sized for loss-making liquidations (`Q-249`) and the per-market budget sub-cap (`Q-267`). Reaffirms `Q-169`, `Q-215`, `Q-230`. **Superseded by `Q-287`:** `minPledgeUsd` stays $10 per market, and a minimum debt per chain derived from liquidation gas now applies to borrows. **Superseded by `Q-381`:** there is no minimum debt; `minPledgeUsd` stays $10. | A higher floor would shut out small LPs to defend against an attack whose cost is bounded: each dust account costs its opener a pledge and gas, its liquidation gas is reimbursed within a sized budget, and an unliquidated dust deficit is covered by the reserve. With liquidation gated per seized reserve (`Q-247`), a dust position no longer shields a large one. | — |
| `Q-317` | ParameterStore keyed by chain, Hub, market, token, feed and debt asset | Each chain runs one ParameterStore behind its own timelock. Values are keyed by the narrowest scope they govern. Chain: `CHAIN_BLOCK_TIME` (immutable, 12 s Ethereum, 2 s Base), `sequencer` (feed and `gracePeriod`; Base feed `0xBCF85224fc0756B9Fa45aA7892530B47e10b6433`, grace 3,600 s; Ethereum none), `maxFlashFeeBps` (launch 10 on both chains: the Aave V3 Pool premium is 5 bps today, which leaves room for one premium change, and 0 applies once Avana's flash source holds `FLASH_BORROWER`), `minDebtUsd` (Ethereum $2,000, Base $50, `Q-287`). Hub: `tokenUsdCap(hub, token)`, `depositorUsdCap(hub)`, `breakerDeficitMax(hub, asset)`. Market: consistency window and tolerance (`consistencyToleranceBps`, replacing the tick tolerance), `twapMinObservations = ⌈twapWindow / CHAIN_BLOCK_TIME⌉ + 1`, slippage, `poolShareMaxBps`, `minPledgeUsd`, caps, and swap-route depth thresholds in 8-decimal USD sized for a one-sided unwind. Feed: the feed's own published `heartbeat` plus `heartbeatMargin`, `borrowPriceMaxAge ≤ heartbeat + heartbeatMargin` (the 3,600 s / 4,200 s cap is removed). Token: `tokenCollateralFactorBps` (explicit value for tokens without a Core Hub collateral factor), `secondSource`. Debt asset: `floorRoundingAllowance(asset)`, `debtStressRiseBps`. The buy-leg parameters (`buySlippageAllowanceBps`, its floor, buy-route depth) are retired; liquidation liveness becomes the single inequality `minLB × (10⁴ − slippageAllowanceMinBps) ≥ 10⁴ × (10⁴ + maxFlashFeeBps)` per (market, debt reserve). No Hub immutable. Supersedes `Q-261`; amends `Q-139`, `Q-159`, `Q-188`, `Q-196`, `Q-210`. **Amended by `Q-381`:** the chain holds no `minDebtUsd`. **Amended by `Q-383`:** `poolShareMaxBps` is stored per pool. **Amended by `Q-385`:** each token may also record a standby second source. | Caps held per Spoke multiply with every Spoke; Hub keying keeps one counter per Hub. Feed heartbeats differ by feed (stablecoin feeds 82,800–86,400 s), so a global age cap stops borrowing most of the day. A TWAP buffer sized for 12 s blocks is cheap to exhaust on 2 s blocks. Flashing the debt asset itself removes the GHO buy leg, so its parameters have nothing to bound. A minimum debt per chain keeps every account worth liquidating at that chain's gas cost. | `Q-261`. Resolves AMMN-INT-01, AMMN-RT-08, AMMN-RT-05, AMMN-GAS-02, AMMN-FLOW-21, AMMN-RT-09, AMMN-FLOW-03 |
| `Q-394` | Proposed defaults and final values | Every economic or risk value in 1.5 shows its number marked "proposed default; final value set in the listing ARFC" (or the funding ARFC for credit lines, first-loss coverage and the deficit coverage window). Engineering constants (gas limits, buffer sizes, `MAX_*` and other implementation bounds) stay fixed. These proposed defaults were confirmed on 2026-11-10: `poolShareMaxBps` 10 % (lower per pool at listing; amended by `Q-403` to 10,000 bps, off by default, with an optional per-pool cap), the rescue top-up slice 10 % of the market cap, the stablecoin band $0.90–$1.02, `consistencyToleranceBps` 0.5 %, `twapWindow` 30 minutes, `feedAgreementMarginBps` 0.5 %, `heartbeatMargin` 30 minutes, `cachedWindow` 1 hour, `disagreementPauseMax` 1 hour, `graceDuration` 30 minutes, `sequencer.gracePeriod` 1 hour, `maxQuoteChangeBps` 1 %, `slippageAllowanceBps` 1 %, `maxShortfallBps` 10 %, wind-down notice 5 days, ramp 30 days and final threshold 50 %, and `deficitCoverageWindow` 72 hours. Amends `Q-072`, `Q-196`, `Q-210`. | Aave governance approves each listing and each credit line with its own service providers. Marking a value as Avana's proposal keeps the record exact about who sets it. | — |
| `Q-403` | Pool-share gate off by default | The chain-wide default `poolShareMaxBps` is 10,000 bps: no limit on Avana's share of a pool. A risk provider may set a cap on a specific pool at listing or later through `tightenPoolShareMaxBpsOf(marketId, v)` (short delay) and raise it through `loosenPoolShareMaxBpsOf(marketId, v)` (normal delay, at most the chain default); where a cap is set, the pledge test (`SPEC-CAP-005`) and the concentration gate (`SPEC-BRK-002`) apply to that pool unchanged. Avana's combined share of each pool above 25 % raises a monitoring alert. The contracts and the per-pool setters are unchanged. Amends `Q-196`, `Q-383`, `Q-394`, `Q-399`. | Removing liquidity has no price impact. Liquidation proceeds can be sold through any approved swap route: other pools, DEXs and aggregators. Liquidation safety therefore comes from the route-depth checks and the draw caps, not from Avana's share of the pool. Concentration risk is covered by token caps, market caps and listing review. A default cap would limit growth, and no test showed it to be needed. | — |

## Off-chain components

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-039` | Convex and Ponder responsibilities | Superseded by `Q-073`. Was: Convex for app data, transaction preparation, preferences and users; Ponder for chain indexing. | — | — |
| `Q-040` | Where calldata is built | Superseded by `Q-073`. Was: calldata built client-side through `packages/protocol`. | — | — |
| `Q-073` | App data path | Convex: app data, preferences, users, intent and progress tracking, daily history snapshots. Ponder (Postgres): live chain-event indexing. The browser builds calldata through `packages/protocol` from a bundled, verified deployment manifest; Convex never supplies a transaction target or calldata (`Q-149`). | If the backend could hand the browser calldata, a compromised backend could redirect signatures; building transactions client-side from a verified manifest takes it out of the signing path. | `Q-039`, `Q-040` |
| `Q-076` | Events | Full event coverage, one atomic call per borrower or keeper action, each contract emitting its own events. Inventory: `ProxyDeployed` (ProxyManager); `UserProxyCreated`, `CollateralAdded`, `Borrowed` (naming the debt reserve and the receiver), `Repaid`, `CollateralWithdrawn` and `SeizedUnitsBurned` (AaveAdapter); `ProxyAdded` and the mint and burn `Transfer` (vault token); `CollateralPledged`, `CollateralReleased`, `RecordDebited`, `SeizureSettled`, `SeizureReconciled`, `PendingSettled` and `CounterSaturated` (Collateralization); `NftReceived`, `NftReturned`, `LiquidityRemoved` and `FeesCollected` (LPVault); Aave `Supply`, `SetUsingAsCollateral`, `Borrow`, `Repay`, `Withdraw` and the Spoke liquidation event; `LiquidationSettled` (debt reserve, debt repaid, debt bought and sold back, GHO spent, units seized, GHO recovered, recovery floor, budget draw, Avana fee, caller payout), `InKindSettled`, `DebtBought`, `DebtSoldBack`, `RouteFailed` and `PendingSettlementForwarded` (LPVaultSwap); `BudgetDrawn` (SettlementBudget); `PauseStateChanged` (MarketRegistry); and configuration, role, timelock and emergency events. Keys: `chainId + Spoke + proxy` for accounts, `chainId + Collateralization + recordId` for collateral records, `chainId + Spoke + debtReserveId` for debt reserves, `txHash + logIndex` for logs. **Amended by `Q-223`:** the Adapter emits the borrower-action events. **Amended by `Q-241`, `Q-242`, `Q-248`, `Q-254`, `Q-255`:** breaker, address-freeze, standby-switch, partial-removal, re-credit and repay-with-collateral events join the inventory. **Amended by `Q-292`, `Q-333`:** collateral records are keyed by (`integrationId`, `positionKey`), and `DebtBought` and `DebtSoldBack` are removed because liquidation settles in the repaid debt asset. | The indexer and monitoring must reconstruct every balance and restriction from events alone; stable keys let them join Avana and Aave events without guessing. | — |
| `Q-149` | Intent storage | Convex stores only intent ID and status; the browser rebuilds calldata from the borrower's inputs and the bundled manifest, and the wallet shows receiver and amount before signing. | Storing calldata in the backend would let a backend compromise alter a transaction between preparation and signing. | — |
| `Q-219` | Keeper operations | The keeper service has an authenticated kill switch that stops new submissions at once. Before each liquidation call it reads the EIP-1967 implementation slot of every upgradeable target the call reaches (LPVaultSwap, LPOracle, AvanaConfig) and the runtime code hash of every immutable target (LPVault, SettlementBudget, GuardianRestrictions, PayoutEscrow, routes, Lock) and compares them with the verified deployment manifest; on any mismatch it stops submitting, alerts and stays in observe-only mode until a verified manifest lists the new code. It reserves its GHO for transactions still pending, so concurrent liquidations never spend the same balance. A transaction not mined within a fixed number of blocks is re-sent at the same nonce with a higher fee; once its `deadline` has passed, the nonce is freed with a zero-value self-transfer. **Amended by `Q-226`:** the keeper also reads the implementation slots of the Adapter, ProxyManager, MarketRegistry, ParameterStore and RiskModule, the ProxyManager's beacon implementation and the code hash of Collateralization; AvanaConfig and GuardianRestrictions are gone; the normal delay is 5 days (`Q-228`). **Amended by `Q-248`:** the code check also covers every DebtOracle implementation. **Amended by `Q-306`, `Q-333`, `Q-336`:** there is no ProxyManager beacon to read; the code check also covers the flash source; and the keeper reserves its debt-asset inventory per chain. **Amended by `Q-404`:** a liquidation reaches no flash source, so the code check for liquidation drops the flash source and covers instead the runtime code hash of the flash liquidation helper the keeper calls (public or its own); the flash source stays in the check for the keeper's `deleverage` monitoring only. | A keeper that keeps submitting after an unexpected upgrade, spends the same GHO twice or leaves a stale transaction pending can fail liquidations or execute them late. A proxy's own bytecode never changes on upgrade, so only the implementation slot reveals one; the 14-day `upgradeDelay` leaves time to publish a verified manifest, so an announced upgrade does not interrupt liquidation. | — |
| `Q-244` | Monitoring and alerting | The monitoring service raises two alert levels (warn, page) per market and debt asset, each naming its responder and playbook: price state (Cached on entry; Expired page), disagreement start (page 30 minutes before `disagreementPauseMax` ends), every feed leg's age (50 % and 90 % of `feedMaxAge`), debt feed age (above `borrowPriceMaxAge`; 90 % of `liquidationPriceMaxAge`), USD band distance (0.95; 0.90), collateral value per market (−10 %; −25 % in one hour), live pool share (25 %, alert only, `Q-403`), any breaker trip, accounts below health factor 1 not liquidated (5; 25 blocks), liquidation reverts by reason in simulation or mempool (`RecoveryBelowFloor`, `NoRouteAvailable`, `NoBuyRouteAvailable`: 1; 3 per market per hour), route depth at stress size from a daily fork simulation against `routeDepthThresholdGho` (120 %; 100 %), GSM headroom per token (2×; 1× the market's stress size), Coverage Spoke balance (150 %; 100 % of `firstLossReserveMinimum`) and the coverage ratio of `Q-249`, Hub deficit (any; uncovered after 24 hours), SettlementBudget (`remainingToday` below 100 GHO or balance below 1,000 GHO; balance below 500 GHO), keeper reimbursement budget (50 %; 25 %), `drawn / drawCap` (80 %; 90 %), Aave configuration against the recorded values (any change), backing-check simulation failures and `CounterSaturated` (page), every `CallScheduled` (page for role changes, upgrades, `setShortDelaySelector` and `setNormalDelay`), Safe owner or threshold changes, pause-state changes, address freezes and standby switches. Borrowers receive health alerts and the state of their markets through the app. **Amended by `Q-333`, `Q-341`, `Q-357`:** route depth is checked against the one-sided `routeDepthThreshold` in the debt asset, buy-route alerts are removed, GSM headroom applies only to GSM backup routes, and the sequencer feed and flash-source liquidity are monitored per chain. **Amended by `Q-397`:** the SettlementBudget alerts no longer apply, because the contract no longer exists. | Breakers stop new activity by themselves, but only people can switch feeds, cover deficits, fund reserves, cancel operations or ask Aave to act, and each needs an alert early enough to act within the window caps are sized for. Two levels separate drift that needs attention from failures that need action now, and naming the responder and the playbook removes the time spent deciding who acts. | — |

## Verification and repository

| ID | Decision | Resolution | Reasoning | Folds in |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `Q-027` | Package manager | npm for the TypeScript workspace, with `package-lock.json`. | Matches the existing sandbox, so one lockfile format is used throughout. | — |
| `Q-038` | Repository | The repository is `avana-monorepo`, holding contracts, app, packages and spec. | One repository keeps the spec, the deployment manifest and the code that implements them in step. | — |
| `Q-041` | Formal verification location | Certora specifications live in `contracts/certora/`. | Formal properties sit next to the contracts they check and run in the same build. | — |
| `Q-042` | Aave V4 licence | Licensing of Aave V4 code is handled outside engineering and does not affect the build. | The spec covers technical design only. | — |
| `Q-043` | Deployment and operations practice | One shared fork environment for contracts and app; bytecode and live-configuration verification before a deployment enters `deployments/verified/`; the keeper runs observe-only (no signer) before it submits transactions. CI fork tests of governance payloads and per-market folders are not part of the launch setup. | Verifying bytecode and live configuration catches a deployment that differs from the reviewed code; observe-only mode tests keeper logic against real state before it can act. | — |
| `Q-044` | Aave V4 test deployments | Superseded by `Q-074`. Was: Avana deploys its own Aave V4 for testnet and sandbox testing. | — | — |
| `Q-046` | Marketing claims | The spec never repeats marketing claims such as "feature-complete". | A spec states testable behaviour. | — |
| `Q-074` | Test environment | Avana deploys the pinned Aave V4 release on Sepolia and local Anvil; mainnet forks are used for Uniswap v3, Chainlink, GHO FlashMinter and GSM behaviour. One shared environment for contracts and app. **Amended by `Q-366`:** forks of each launch chain test each AMM integration on its own chain, the Chainlink and sequencer feeds and the Aave V3 Pool flash loan; the GHO FlashMinter and the GSM are not dependencies. | Deploying the pinned release means tests run against exactly the code that will be listed; external protocols are tested on forks because their real state cannot be reproduced. | `Q-044` |
| `Q-115` | Audit scope | The audit covers new code written from this spec. Schedules, bounties and other process items are outside the spec. | Keeps the spec to technical requirements. | — |
| `Q-119` | Engineering defaults | The engineering defaults adopted during design review are written directly into the contract, flow, security and verification pages as numbered rules. Where a rule conflicts with a decision in 4.4, the decision wins. | Each default is stated once, in the page that implements it, with its own ID for traceability. | — |
| `Q-152` | Verification stack | Spec stage: Quint state machines (Quint simulator, with Apalache bounded model checking on the same models), Z3 and SymPy for math, Hypothesis state-machine tests and Python economic simulations, and adversarial review by AI and humans; Alloy is added if relationship bugs appear. Implementation stage: Foundry, Solidity SMTChecker, Halmos, Certora CVL and audits. All tools are pinned in the repository under `spec/05-verification/` and `contracts/`. Every critical requirement has a verification method recorded in `spec/05-verification/traceability.md`; every failed check becomes a finding. | Different tools find different classes of error (state-machine, arithmetic, economic, implementation); checking at spec stage finds design errors before code exists. | — |
| `Q-173` | Requirement traceability | Every English rule, formula, invariant, flow step and decision has a stable ID. Checks link to it with `<CHECK-ID> verifies: <ID>` (Z3 `MATH-*`, SymPy `SYM-*`, Quint `QNT-*`, Hypothesis `HYP-*`, simulation `SIM-*`, Foundry `TEST-*`, formal `PROP-*`) and Solidity with `/// @custom:spec <ID>`. `spec/05-verification/trace.py` regenerates the matrix and fails on duplicate IDs, dangling links and requirement text that changed since its checks were last accepted (hash lock in `traceability.lock.json`); `--strict` also fails on coverage gaps. It runs in `run_all.sh`. | When a rule changes, every check that relies on it is flagged until re-run, so no proof silently refers to an old rule. | — |
| `Q-204` | Repository contents | The design-review record and working reference notes are kept outside the repository; spec pages state each decision's reasoning in place. Spec scaffolding (templates and the to-do list) is removed. Every document in the repository describes how Avana works, with math, examples and reasoning, and contains no template text, to-do items or commentary on how the documents were produced. | Readers and auditors see only the design as it stands, with no stale material to reconcile. | — |
| `Q-205` | Decision record | 4.4 is Design Decisions: one entry per decision with its final resolution and reasoning; superseded decisions resolve to the decision that replaced them; Q-IDs stay stable for traceability. | A single final record is easier to audit than a log of changes, and stable IDs keep every reference from the spec and verification models valid. | — |
| `Q-207` | Reference material | Working reference material, including its diagrams, is not part of the repository, because the design changed after it was made; copies are kept internally. No document links to it. | Outdated diagrams next to the spec would contradict it. | — |
| `Q-278` | System invariants and sequence testing | The global invariants include system-level economic invariants, asserted after every step of the stateful suites: solvency per debt asset (collateral realizable for that debt, pending value, Coverage Spoke balance and SettlementBudget at least the debt plus the Hub deficit), conservative valuation at most realizable value at the listing stress size, no zero-risk profit loop for any actor across any set of wallets, the liquidation deficit bound `deficit_after ≤ deficit_before + seizedValue × (1 − 1/LB)`, no cross-market contagion, GHO conservation across whole sequences, and the invariants of every feature added by `Q-241` to `Q-277`. Verification adds a multi-actor sequence suite (borrowers, outside LPs, approved keepers, permissionless liquidators, a flash-loan attacker, a pool manipulator, a lender, guardians and the timelock), solvency fuzzing per debt asset across arbitrary sequences, conservative LP-value fuzzing, withdrawal and liquidation cascades, oracle fuzzing including zero, extreme answers, sudden moves and a Chainlink phase change, token edge cases (fee-on-transfer, the USDT fee switch, USDC pause and blocklist, reentrancy through `collect` and `decreaseLiquidity`), end-to-end LP journeys on a fork and a `forge script` simulation of the full deployment and first listing through the Safes and the timelock. **Amended by `Q-359`:** conservation is asserted per debt asset, and the invariants cover every chain, Hub, CDP Spoke, market and AMM integration. **Amended by `Q-397`:** the solvency invariant has no SettlementBudget term. | Single-call checks cannot see failures that need sequences: a shield needs a pledge, a later oracle failure and a liquidation attempt; reflexive depth loss needs several liquidations in a row; reserve erosion needs many small deficits; a deployment error needs the real deployment order. Stating the economic invariants and testing them across arbitrary sequences of every actor shows that the design stays solvent, not only that each call is correct. | — |
| `Q-359` | Global invariants for the AMM-neutral, multi-Hub, multi-chain design | 4.1 states every invariant for each chain, Hub, CDP Spoke, market and AMM integration. A market is one (chain, AMM protocol, pool, exact position shape); a record carries `integrationId` and `positionKey`, and amounts are n-token arrays. A pledge requires the exact market shape (`INV-005`(b): for concentrated liquidity `tickLower` and `tickUpper` equal the market's). Each wallet has one logic-free immutable PositionProxy per (wallet, CDP Spoke, market), created by CREATE2 with salt `keccak256(abi.encode(wallet, spoke, marketId))`, driven only by the chain's one AaveAdapter, with no delegation or upgrade (`INV-016`, `INV-063`, `INV-064`, `INV-065`, `INV-052`). Fair-composition pricing with the range clamp, "floor then −1 raw unit", signed tick alignment and fee exclusion is the conservative-valuation invariant (`INV-078`), with the readiness guards for Uniswap v2, Curve, Balancer and Uniswap v4. Settlement accounting is in the debt asset repaid (`INV-035`, `INV-070`, `INV-079`), and pending settlement funds the Coverage Spoke of the market's (Hub, debt asset). Rewritten: `INV-002`, `003`, `005`, `006`, `007`, `012`, `013`, `016`, `017`, `018`, `020`, `022`, `033`, `035`, `041`, `044`, `052`, `058`, `063`, `064`, `065`, `070`, `073`, `078`, `079`. Amends `Q-278`. | A unit of liquidity is worth a different amount in every range, so crediting a position at another range's per-unit value misprices it (a $10,000 position priced at $9.79M). One account per market keeps each health factor isolated and leaves no proxy code that an exit depends on. Proxies with no logic remove the version-support problem. Payout in the debt asset lets Base settle with no GHO depth. | Resolves AMMN-RT-01, AMMN-RT-02, AMMN-RT-04, AMMN-RT-10, AMMN-RT-12, AMMN-RT-15, AMMN-RT-16, AMMN-FLOW-04, AMMN-FLOW-08, AMMN-FLOW-11, AMMN-FLOW-18, AMMN-FLOW-23, AMMN-FLOW-24, AMMN-SM-03, AMMN-ARCH-06, AMMN-ARCH-08, AMMN-ARCH-09 |
| `Q-361` | Isolation covers pool-level failures; token prices are shared; token exposure is capped per funding Hub | `INV-075` promises no contagion from failures confined to one market's pool, oracle state, breaker, route or budget window, and one debt reserve's feed or route. A token's price move reaches every market and debt that holds that token, by design. Token, depositor and pool-share counters are keyed by Hub in the one ledger and summed over every Spoke that draws from that Hub, including foreign Spokes on a cross-Hub line (`INV-092`). **Amended by `Q-383`:** the pool-share counter is per pool, summed over every market of the pool on the chain, not per Hub. **Amended by `Q-397`:** there is no settlement budget window. | The topology model showed per-Spoke isolation cannot isolate a shared token's price (QNT-TOPO-10 counterexample by design), while pool-local shocks stay isolated (QNT-TOPO-10P). Counting exposure per funding Hub keeps the cap a Hub's suppliers approved binding (QNT-TOPO-X2). | Resolves AMMN-SM-05, AMMN-RT-05, AMMN-FLOW-16 |
| `Q-362` | Structural invariants of the AMM-neutral core | New invariants: `INV-091` (the Lock has exactly two members, AaveAdapter and LPVaultSwap, and every value-moving LPVault and Collateralization entry point is reachable only through them, so new Spokes and markets need no Lock redeploy); `INV-092` (caps keyed by Hub); `INV-093` (standard-Spoke settings: `receiveSharesEnabled = false`, liquidation fee 0 or paid to Avana's fee receiver, no reinvestment controller, vault-token reserve not borrowable; a drift trips the breaker, which stops new activity and never liquidation); `INV-095` (`IFlashSource`: liquidity and premium checks before the loan, caller, initiator and context checks in the callback, never the Hub that funds the liquidated Spoke's debt reserve, replaced only through the timelock, caller-funded path when short); `INV-096` (at most 16 records per account per market; a minimum debt per chain derived from liquidation gas, $2,000 on Ethereum and $50 on Base at launch, `Q-287`); `INV-097` (SettlementBudget window per market); `INV-098` (L2 sequencer gate with a grace period; exits and liquidation never gated). Rewritten to match: `INV-013`, `INV-029`, `INV-037`, `INV-040`, `INV-041`, `INV-054`. **Amended by `Q-381`:** `INV-096`(b) sets no minimum debt; it states that one liquidation of the largest account fits the per-transaction gas limit. **Amended by `Q-382`:** `INV-093` states the drift exposure window and the residual risk of seizures outside LPVaultSwap. **Amended by `Q-386`:** `INV-095` compares the flash source's reported lender with every Hub on the chain. | An immutable Lock list cannot name contracts created later, so membership is fixed at the two contracts that exist from day one. On a standard Spoke, liquidation exclusivity depends on Aave-side settings plus the vault token's transfer rule. Without a record bound, Aave's dust rule can make an account unliquidatable in one transaction.
